- *Report by relation:* a chart's `groupBy` may be a MANY_TO_ONE field name (no `bucket`): `putCharts` reuses `rankView`, so the bars are named from the target's list (`optionsPath`/`optionValue`/`optionLabel`, through the shared `useOptionNames` hook in `widgets.tsx`) and a click drills into the list on the relation's `<fk>Id` filter.
- *Side detail:* an `entity-list` page's `detail` is `drawer`, `side` or `record` (absent: the record page when there is one, else the drawer). `side` renders the selected row's `<Entity>Detail` (+ Edit/Delete/close) in a pane beside the rows, the open row in the route as `#/<page>/<id>` (`selectedId`/`onSelect` route props, like master-detail); only an entity with such a page gets the pane and its props (`SIDE_DETAIL_KEY` → `listSideDetail`, in both frontend sets). `side` needs a single-key entity; `record` needs a record page. The list screen passes `onOpenRecord` on `opensRecord`, not on the raw `hasRecordPage`.
- *Parent card:* a master-detail page's `showParent` renders the selected parent's `<Parent>Detail` above its child rows (fetched by id when it is not on the loaded list page) and, for a writable parent, an Edit drawer (`showParentEdit`) that saves with PUT and reloads the list. Absent keeps the screen byte for byte; new pages in the editor start with it on.
- *Parent New:* a writable master-detail parent's list has a **New** link (`parentCreatable`). It opens the parent's form/wizard page when it has one (`parentCreatesInPage`/`parentCreatePageId`, from `PageLinks.createPageOf`); otherwise it opens a form drawer that POSTs and selects the new parent (`parentCreatesInDrawer`; this drawer is shared with the card's Edit, and `creating` exists only when both are on, because the generated lint rejects an unread state). Without it, a parent shown only on a master-detail page could never be created, and neither could its children through their required relation. Orders' customers, Tickets' teams and Blog's authors all had this gap. The imports come from Java-built lists (`parentIconImports`, `parentFeatureImports`). A read-only parent keeps its old bytes. Pinned by `fullstackEndpoint_masterDetailShowsTheParent`. All three variants are built by the Orders and Tickets layout smoke tests and by `fullstackFrontendFormPagesInstallAndBuild`.
- *Navigation:* a request's `nav` (`{style: sidebar|topbar, collapsibleGroups}`, needs `pages`) drives the tailwind `App.tsx`: `navTopbar` lays the nav sections out in a row under the brand (no off-canvas menu, no `Menu` icon import) and `navCollapsibleGroups` folds each labelled sidebar section in a `<details>`; absent, the shell is byte for byte what it was, and the Menora shell ignores it. (Round 10, phase 8 added the header's tools to `nav` — `userMenu`, `notifications`, `commandPalette` — which both shells take.) An example may carry it as `settings.nav` (validated by `FullstackExampleAdminController`).
- *Editor:* the UI mirrors every rule above in `pageLayout.ts` (`validatePages(pages, entities, { ldapAuth })`, whose `warnings` are advice that never blocks Generate: a hidden page nothing embeds, a chart whose entity has no list page in the nav to drill into, recent rows without a record page, roles on a page out of the nav, the silent 5-tab/4-tile caps, widgets a period picker cannot limit — each with a jump-to and a Fix), keeps settings across a widget kind/entity switch (`retargetWidget`/`retargetReport`/`retargetWizardSteps`, naming what was dropped with an undo) and across a page **type** switch (`retargetPage`), and maps a Generate **or Explore** 400 naming a page onto that page (`pageOfServerError`). Every field, relation and enum value shows its label with the raw name after it only when they differ (`fieldOptions.ts`); the page id sits behind a "Route #/… · edit id" disclosure; a row can be made the start page; the Tabs gallery card opens a picker of the pages to embed (hiding them by default) and the Master–detail card is disabled without a relation pair; a "Navigation" strip renames and reorders nav groups (`renameGroupInPages`/`moveNavGroup`, a page dragged into a section joins it — `adoptedGroup`) and, for tailwind sets, sets the shell's `nav`; the preview reorders and resizes dashboard widgets in place; the Guide's `fs-pages` topic opens from the section's help button. The Pages section sits below Entities (both in the section nav). "Add widget" opens a gallery (entity + kind, each kind disabled with the validator's reason — `widgetKindDisabledReason`, also on the card's kind switcher; `newWidget` pre-fills a progress target / note text). A list page's "Split into tabs" (`splitListByField`, `splitDisabledReason`) makes one hidden preset list per enum/boolean value under a new tabs page as one undo step. A hand-edited page id is kept on the page as `idLocked` (editor-only — `requestPages` strips it from every request and from the admin example's stored JSON), so it survives a reload or a load. The command palette offers Add page / Start from my entities / Use classic layout / Open the layout preview (`addRequest`/`previewRequest` props).

**Generated-app additions (2026-09 round 3).**
- *List state in the route:* a list page in the nav keeps its whole state in the hash — filters by their own names, the rest `_`-prefixed (`_q`, `_sort` = `field`/`-field`/blank for none, `_page` from 1, `_size`, `_view`) — via `route.ts` `listStateOf`/`listQueryOf` (gated `hasListStatePages`). The screen (`listKeepsState`, route props `query`/`onQueryChange={setQuery}`; a side pane's `onSelect` carries `route.query`) remounts the list only for a route it did not write itself (`written` ref vs `mount.key`), and a preset value is left out of the query (a cleared one kept blank). Only an entity with such a page (`__listStateEntities` → `listUrlState`) gets `listState`/`onListStateChange`; its page resets to the first page in render (`resetKey`), so the page it opened on stands, and reports only what differs from its opening values. Hidden (tab-only) lists keep local state. `ListState` lives next to `SortSpec` in both `Table.tsx`.
- *Empty lists:* `Table`/`CardGrid` (both sets) take `filtered` (search or a filter on: "No records match") and `emptyAction` (writable entity: "New X" → `openNew`/`onCreate`); Menora draws it outlined (the New pill is the view's one yellow). This applies to the classic shell too.
- *Detail formatting:* `EntityDetail` words booleans (`t('trueLabel')`) and formats dates/date-times for `LOCALE`; `detailUsesT`/`detailUsesLocale` gate the imports.
- *Relation pickers:* a relation whose target's label field is searchable (`labelSearchable` → `targetSearchable`) is picked with the shared `RelationPicker` (combobox over `?q=`, 20 at a time, the picked row's name read by `GET /{id}`) in the form and the filter bar (`searchable: true` on the descriptor); others keep the `useOptions` `<select>` (`formUsesOptions`/`formUsesPicker`). `useOptionNames` reads a group past the first 1,000 rows by id.
- *Dashboard refresh:* a dashboard with a data widget has a Refresh button (`hasRefresh`); `refreshSeconds` (30/60/300/900, dashboard only, needs a data widget) adds a timer that ticks while the tab is visible (`hasAutoRefresh`). `stats.ts` `RefreshTick` (context) is read by `useStats`, `useNumber` and `RecentList`, which keep what they show during a reload (they clear only for a new query).
- *Wizard:* a new record's answers are kept in `sessionStorage` (`wizard-draft:<page>`) until saved; finished steps are clickable; "Start over" (`t('startOver')`) forgets the draft.
- *Record pages:* the first two enum fields show as chips by the heading (`statusFields`); Previous/Next walk the rows of the list the record was opened from (`record-siblings:<entity>` in `sessionStorage`, written by the entity's page when `entityHasRecordPage` and it has `onOpenRecord`), so record screens always take `onNavigate`.
- *Charts:* `bar`/`donut` group by a MANY_TO_ONE too (`rankBy(…, "group by")`, named through `useOptionNames`); a trend fills its empty day/month/year buckets for count/sum (`stats.ts` `fillBuckets`, capped at 400 points) and the line has a min/mid/max scale; a report chart's `table` (Boolean, default: first chart only) says whether its totals table is drawn (`chartHasTable`).
- *Related lists:* a record page's `childTabs` entry and a master-detail page take `columns`/`sort` like a list page's (validated against the child; the child entity joins `__listPresentationEntities`), passed as literal props to the embedded page.
- *Form sections:* `EntityDefinitionDto.formSections` (`[{title, fields}]`, ≤ 6, fields and MANY_TO_ONE names once each, never a generated key; `FullstackRequestValidator.parseFormSections`) groups the form (`<fieldset>` + `<legend>`, hidden in a wizard step that shows none of its fields — `groupShowExpr`), the details (`EntityDetail`, a heading row per section) and, when a wizard names no steps, its steps (one per section, then the rest). The view-model is `formGroups` (`groupFields`/`groupRelations`; without sections one untitled group — the old bytes); `buildEntityContext` hands the groups the record-linked relation copies too.
- *Editor:* the page gallery asks which entity a new page is about when several fit (`newPageChoices`); four whole-layout templates build from the model (`layoutTemplates.ts`: Work queue, Reporting, Parent–child browser, Admin console — each warning-free on every seeded example) and a page can be saved as a template (per browser); a page search (`/`, palette "Find page…"); enum values rename in place and presets follow (`renameEnumValueInPages`), relation renames follow every reference, and a rename's layout follow-up is one undo step with it; a deleted entity's page or widget offers "Switch to X" when exactly one entity is unmentioned; removals of widgets, charts, tabs, tiles and steps are labelled undo entries with a notice; the preview steps through a wizard to its review, switches a record's tabs, and jumps from a list's headings/sort/side pane to their controls.

Pinned by `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests` (`…DonutStackedAndTextWidgets`, `…recordTabsAndTilesLinkThroughTheNamedRelation`, `…restrictsPagesToRolesWithLdapAuth`, `…shellShowsNotFoundAndRecordBreadcrumbs`, `…rendersAListWidget`, `…rendersALinksWidget`, `…presetsDateAndNumberRanges`, `…reportGroupsByARelation`, `…listOpensRowsInASidePane`, `…masterDetailShowsTheParent`, `…rendersATopbarNav`, `…listPagesKeepTheirStateInTheRoute`, `…dashboardsRefreshTheirWidgets`, `…wizardKeepsADraftAndRecordPagesWalkTheirList`, `…chartsGroupByRelationsFillTheirGapsAndChooseTheirTables`, `…formSectionsGroupTheFormTheDetailsAndTheWizard`, `…relatedListsOpenWithTheirOwnColumnsAndSort`) and `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` (`…listsOfferTheFirstRecordAndTheDetailFormatsValues`, `…picksASearchableLinkByTyping`). The Blog and Inventory examples ship layouts that use them; Course enrolments stays layout-free to show the classic shell. The Orders example carries a list widget and a parent card, Tickets a launcher and a side-pane list, Inventory a per-warehouse report chart and a period preset, so the smoke builds cover them; the Orders smoke build also runs with `nav: {style: topbar, collapsibleGroups: true}`. Round 3: Tickets' overview refreshes every 5 minutes, Inventory has a donut by warehouse and a totals table on its third report chart, and Orders has form sections, a master-detail child list and a record tab with their own columns/sort.

**Generated-app fixes (2026-09 round 4).**
- *Board and calendar load what they show:* `wholeViews` (kanban, or a `calendarRanged` calendar) makes `EntityPage` fetch page 0 with `WHOLE_VIEW_LIMIT` (500) rows in those views instead of the table's page. The calendar reports its month through `CalendarView.onRangeChange` and the page adds the date field's own `<f>From`/`<f>To` (whole days for a date-time, `calendarIsDateTime`) — so `calendarRanged` needs the calendar field to be a filter field — and says "first n of m" when the month holds more. The board takes each lane's real total from `/stats?groupBy=<lane>` (`useResource.countBy`, search + filters only); a lane holding more than it shows offers "+n more · show in the list", which filters the table to that lane (`kanbanShowsLane`: the table is emitted and the lane field is a filter). Both keep their rows on screen during a reload (skeleton only on the first load).
- *Lists:* the delete confirm promises Undo only with soft delete (`cannotUndo` otherwise); deleting the last row of the last page steps back a page; a new search or filter clears the bulk selection (a page change keeps it).
- *Dashboard refresh reaches list widgets:* `RefreshTick` now lives in `useResource.ts` (re-exported by `stats.ts`), and `useResource` reloads on it.
- *Failed fetch by id:* the side pane and the master-detail parent card say "Couldn't load" with a retry instead of loading forever.
- *Charts* key their rows by index (two groups may share a label); `PeriodSelect` follows the route on Back/Forward.

Pinned by `fullstackEndpoint_kanbanCalendarFiltersExportAndBulkDelete` and the matching `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests` cases; built for real by every `fullstackFrontend*` smoke test.

**Page types (round 5): calendar, board, content, import, search.** Their type-only settings ride in one nullable `PageDefinition.Spec` (`CalendarSpec`/`BoardSpec`/`ContentSpec`/`SearchSpec`, read by `calendar()`/`board()`/`content()`/`search()`; the wire fields are flat on `PageDefinitionDto`, and `rejectForeignProps` rejects them on other types). All five can be tab targets and default to their own nav icons (`Calendar`, `Columns3`, `FileText`, `Upload`, `Search` — the last three new in `NAV_ICONS`).
- *Calendar* (`entity`, `dateField` — default the first filterable non-key date, must be filterable — optional `endField`, `modes` ⊆ month/week/agenda/timeline, the first opening, default `[month]`; timeline needs `endField`; `presetFilter` may not name either date). `ScreenCalendar` fetches only the period on screen (`schedule.ts` `windowOf`) through the list's `<d>From`/`<d>To` — with an end date the overlap `<d>To=end&<end>From=start` — up to 500 rows, drawn by the shared `ScheduleView.tsx` (gated `hasCalendarPages`; helpers in `schedule.ts` because a component file may export only components). The day and mode ride in the route (`_date`, `_mode`); an event opens the record page, else the quick-look drawer; a day creates in the wizard, else in the form drawer with the date filled (`calendarCreatesInWizard`/`…InDrawer`).
- *Board* (`entity`, `laneField` — a filterable enum/boolean, default the first — `lanes`, 1–4 `cardFields` (fields or MANY_TO_ONE names, the first the heading; default `defaultCardFields`), `wipLimits` lane → 1..999, `laneSize` 10/20/50, `presetFilter` (not on the lane field) and `sort`). Each lane is its own list call (`?<lane>=V&page=n`), so it shows its real total and loads more on its own; the shared `BoardView.tsx` (gated `hasBoardPages`) drags cards or moves them by a "Move to" menu (touch, keyboard); a move is an optimistic PUT undone on failure, and a lane at its limit refuses the card (`laneFull`). Card cells are TS built in Java (`putBoard` → `cardFields[].renderTs`: enum labels, booleans, relation labels); `boardUsesT` gates the `t` import.
- *Content* (`title` required, `body` ≤ 8000 chars in the Markdown subset `ContentMarkdown` reads: headings, paragraphs, bullet/numbered lists, `>` callouts, `---`, `**bold**`, `*em*`, `` `code` ``, `[text](#/page-id | page:id | https://… | mailto:…)`). Rendered **at generation time** into JSX (`PageContext.contentJsx`): every text run is a `tsString`, so nothing can become markup and no parser ships. A page link must name a page that is not a record page and not hidden (a wizard or a search page excepted); anything else but http(s)/mailto is a 400.
- *Import* (`entity`, writable, one per entity; switches on its `csvImport` — see the opts table). `ScreenImport` wraps `CsvImport` and goes to the entity's home page when done.
- *Search* (one per layout; `entities` default every entity with a searchable text field, ≤ 8; `perEntity` 3–10, default 5; `shellSearch` puts a search box in both shells' headers — `hasShellSearch`/`searchPageId`, the Menora one the `SearchField` port — and a hidden search page must have it; a hidden search page is still a route, and links may open it). `ScreenSearch` asks every entity's list endpoint `?q=` at once (a request counter drops stale answers), groups the matches, opens a match on its record page else the drawer, and "See all" opens the entity's list page with `_q`. The words ride in the route (`q`).

The Project planning example (`projects`) uses all five; `FullstackNewPageTypesTests` pins them (and their 400s), `ContentMarkdownTest` the parser, and `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*PlanningLayout*` / `…ClassicCsvImport…` build them.

**Generated-app fixes (round 5).** Escape in a `RelationPicker` closes only the picker (the drawer/dialog handlers skip a `defaultPrevented` event); the calendar reads a date's first ten characters instead of `new Date("yyyy-mm-dd")` (UTC — the day before, west of UTC); CSV export runs through `useDownload` (busy label, a second click ignored, a toast on failure); the API client words its errors (`failureMessage`: the server's detail for 400/404/409, plain sentences for 401/403/5xx, `errorOffline` for a network failure) and keeps the parsed body on `ApiError.body`; New on a list filtered to one value starts with it (`newFromFilters` → `fromFilters`); board and calendar say when a filter emptied them; the list's load error has a Retry; a new page opens scrolled to its top.

**Round 6 (2026-10).**
- *Lists (every generated list, both sets — shared list improvements, so the classic shell too):* a table row opens on click (not on a button, link, field or text selection) and on Enter, the arrows move between rows (`Table.tsx` `opensRow`/`onRowKey`; the Menora table port gains `onRowOpen` with `.is-openable` rows); `/` focuses the list's search and `n` starts a new record (`useListShortcuts`: the first `[data-list-table]` on screen, no field focused, no dialog open; New is the table's `emptyAction`; the Menora `SearchField` port gains `inputRef`). A **Columns** menu (`ColumnMenu.tsx`, `columnMenu` prop; the first column always shows; keys = `sortKey` else label) hides columns; rows per page, sort, view and hidden columns are kept per browser and entity (`listPrefs.ts`, `localStorage` `list-settings:<entity>`, only what differs from the page's defaults; read back sanitized — a sort the API no longer allows is dropped) and open the list unless the route says otherwise (route > saved > page props); **Reset view** forgets them. The non-route list branch now names its `openingSize`/`openingSort`/`openingView` like the route branch.
- *Unsaved edits:* a `FormDrawer` (both sets) with `dirty` edits holds the page (`leaveGuard.ts` `holdLeave`/`mayLeave`, always shipped): the layout router's `navigate` asks first, Back/Forward away is undone when the user stays (`route.ts` pushes the old hash back), and closing the tab gets the browser's prompt (`beforeunload`). `ConfirmDialog` (both sets) `preventDefault`s its Escape so the drawer under it stays open, and takes `busyLabel`.
- *Dashboards:* `filters` (dashboard pages only, ≤ 4 enum/boolean/MANY_TO_ONE names, matched ignoring case; `PageDefinition.DashboardSpec`) draw one `FilterBar` over the grid (`DASH_FILTERS`, described by the first widget entity that has the field); every data/list widget whose entity filters by one takes it — `dashParams([...keys])` joined into its `paramsExpr` (`DashboardContext.dashKeys`/`dashFilterField`; not a key its own preset fixes) — and a list widget opens with `dashValues(...)` and is keyed on them so a change remounts it. Filters and the period share the route query (`query`/`onQueryChange`, `setPeriod` merges), so a period change keeps the filters; a dashboard without filters keeps its old `period`/`onPeriodChange` props byte for byte. A widget's `drillTo` (a visible entity-list or report page of its entity, resolved after the page loop like links) replaces both its click target (`putDrill`) and its View all/tile target, with the widget's params on. Validator: `FullstackPageValidator.dashboardFilters` (unknown, duplicate, or shaped differently on two entities — "one control cannot set both") and `drillTo` (missing, wrong type, hidden, other entity); text/links widgets take no `drillTo`. The Sales reporting example filters by region and drills its region chart into the report page.
- *Model in the zip:* the request's `editorModel` (the editor's snapshot, never read by validation or generation) is written verbatim to `<artifact>/menora-model.json` when it is an object with an `entities` array within the team-model size (`FullstackModelController.MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES`); otherwise the file holds the request itself (`{format: "menora-fullstack-request/1", request}` — `body.dependencies()`, not the resolved list). The root README says how to reopen it; the editor's Import JSON reads both shapes (`snapshot.ts` `snapshotFromRequest`).
- *Wizard detect endpoints:* `/starter-wizard.detect-paths` and `.detect-services` answer 400 `{error, messages}` for a spec/WSDL that does not parse (and a WSDL with no `<service>`) instead of `200 []`, so the drawers show their parse-error panel.

Pinned by `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` (`…optimisticLockingVersionsWritableEntities`, `…rejectsAVersionFieldUnderOptimisticLocking`, `…inlineEditMakesScalarCellsEditable`, `…listsOpenRowsRememberTheirSettingsAndGuardUnsavedEdits`, `…shipsTheModelItWasGeneratedFrom`), `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests` (`…dashboardFiltersReachEveryMatchingWidget`, `…rejectsInvalidDashboardFiltersAndDrillTo`) and `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests` (`soapDetectServicesEndpoint_rejectsMalformedWsdl`, `openApiDetectPathsEndpoint`).

**Round 7 (2026-10): presets, chart kinds, pivot and activity widgets, hidden list actions.**
- *Enum and relation filters take several values (every generated backend):* the generated `Controller` binds an enum or MANY_TO_ONE filter as `List<X>` (list, `/stats` and CSV alike — Spring reads both `status=OPEN,PAID` and repeated params) and `Service.specOf` matches it with `in(...)`; booleans, ranges and dates are unchanged. The backend render path cannot see `pages`, so this is not gated — with one value it behaves as before. On the frontend `FilterValues` stays `Record<string, string>` (a multi-value is the comma-joined string, so route state, presets, `dashParams` and drill-down are untouched); the enum control of both `FilterBar.tsx` is a checkbox popover (`EnumMultiSelect`), and `fromFilters` (`oneValue`) prefills a new record only from a filter naming one value.
- *Presets:* a `presetFilter` enum value may be a comma list (`"open, PAID"` → `OPEN,PAID`, in the enum's order, duplicates dropped; an empty part or unknown constant is a 400; a boolean takes one value). A MANY_TO_ONE may be preset by its field name or its `<field>Id` filter (kept as the latter) to one or more ids, checked against the target's key type (`FullstackPageValidator.relationIds`: whole numbers, UUIDs, else `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]+` — `presetQueryExpr` splices values unencoded). `presetFilter` now takes `entitiesByLower`, and that map is a `LinkedHashMap` (request order).
- *Report chart kinds:* `ChartDto`/`PageDefinition.Chart` take `kind` (`ChartKind`: `bar`/`donut`/`stacked`/`table` for an enum/boolean/relation grouping, `line`/`area`/`table` over a date — a mismatch is a 400 naming the chart) and `series` (stacked only: an enum/boolean other than `groupBy`, default the next; a relation `groupBy` cannot stack). The default kind for the grouping is canonicalized to null, so naming it keeps the bytes. `putCharts` adds `chartHasShape`/`chartShape`, `series` in `rollupQuery` and its labels ref; `ScreenReport` passes `shape`/`seriesLabels` only when set; `ReportChart` draws a `DonutChart`, the new shared `StackedBars` (pulled out of `StackedCard`, which now wraps it), `LineChart area` or only the totals table (`table` chart: `table` is forced on, `table: false` is a 400).
- *Pivot widget* (`kind: "pivot"`, default span 4): rows = `groupBy`, columns = `series`, each an enum, boolean or MANY_TO_ONE (`rankBy`; default columns = the next one, `pivotColumns`; the same twice is a 400), any `agg`. It reads `/stats?groupBy=&series=` (the backend `groupPath` already whitelists relations) with the widget's preset, period and dashboard filters. `PivotCard` (`widgets.tsx`) names relation axes through `useOptionNames`, shows row/column/grand totals for count and sum only, and a cell drills into the list on both keys when the list filters by both (`seriesDrillKey`).
- *Activity widget* (`kind: "activity"`, no `entity`): `entities` (`WidgetDto`/`Widget.entities`, ≤ 6, default every entity the `audit` opt applies to — `auditApplies` with the request's scaffold opts; a named entity without audit, a duplicate or no audited entity at all is a 400), `limit` 5–20 (default 10), `title`, `span`; nothing else. `ActivityFeed` fetches each list with `?size=limit&sort=updatedAt,desc`, merges by `Date.parse(updatedAt)`, says created (`createdAt === updatedAt`) or updated with `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat(LOCALE)`, opens a row's record page when there is one and reloads on `RefreshTick`; i18n keys `recentActivity`/`activityCreated`/`activityUpdated`/`noActivity`. The widget-kind lists in `EntityScaffoldContext` (`uses<Kind>`, the `widgetIs*` false-lists) carry `Pivot`/`Activity`. A `recent` widget may now sort by `createdAt`/`updatedAt` when audit applies.
- *Hidden list actions:* an `entity-list` page's `hideActions` (`new`/`edit`/`delete`/`export`/`import`/`bulk`, canonicalized to that order into `PageDefinition.ListSpec`, `hiddenActions()`; unknown or repeated is a 400, and so is any on a read-only entity without an export — it would have nothing to hide) leave those buttons out of that page's list. Only an entity with such a page (`__listActionEntities` → `listActionsConfigurable`, which also needs a writable entity or an export) gets the `hideActions` prop, `ListAction` type and `can(...)` helper in both `EntityPage` sets: `emptyAction` (and with it the `n` shortcut) is undefined without `new`, `editRow`/`deleteRow` replace `openEdit`/`setPendingDelete` on cards, table, kanban (whose `onMove` needs `edit` too), side pane and quick look, inline cells need `edit`, `selectable` needs `bulk`. Presentation only — roles and the backend gate access.
- *Examples:* Blog's home has an activity feed; Orders' products list hides Delete; Sales reporting draws its region chart as a donut and the north trend as an area; Project planning has a "Work per person" pivot (assignee × status) and an "Open work" report (`status: TODO,DOING,REVIEW`, a stacked priority chart and an area by due date). `ui/src/components/fullstack/__fixtures__/fullstack-examples.json` is the API-shaped copy (`id`, no `sortOrder`) — edit it alongside, never copy the catalog file over it.

Pinned by `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests` (`…multiValueAndRelationPresets`, `…reportChartKinds`, `…rendersPivotAndActivityWidgets`, `…listPageHidesActions`) and the updated filter pins in `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests`.

**Round 8 (2026-10): KPI trend, form pages, entity workflows ("human tasks") and the inbox.** Everything here is gated, so a request that uses none of it keeps its bytes.
- *KPI trend:* a `kpi` widget's `trend` (`day`/`month`/`year`, `WidgetDto.trend`; kept on `Widget.bucket`/`groupBy`, which a kpi otherwise leaves unused) draws a sparkline under the number (`widgets.tsx` `KpiSparkline`, its own `useStats` call, `fillBuckets`, not clickable — the tile is a button). The date it plots over is the period picker's widget date; without a picker, a named non-key `dateField` (allowed then only for this), else the first non-key date (`FullstackPageValidator.trendOn`; none → 400). `trend` on another kind is a 400. View-model `hasTrend`/`trendOn`; the screen passes `trendOn`/`trendBucket`.
- *Form page* (`type: "form"`, `entity`): the entity's whole form (its `formSections` as fieldsets) on a page of its own — `#/<page>` creates, `#/<page>/<id>` edits (single key only, `formEditable`). It takes over New (list, empty state, `n`), a calendar day's create and the record page's Edit; a list row's Edit stays the quick drawer. A writable entity has at most one, and **a wizard or a form, not both** (`checkOneCreatePage`). Save validates first (`validate<X>`), maps a 400's field errors onto the fields, turns a stale 409 into the `StaleNotice` (`formLocking`), and lands on the record page, else the entity's home, else starts over. While dirty it holds the page (`holdLeave`), **released before navigating after a save**. A hidden form page stays a route (like a hidden wizard). `ScreenForm.tsx.mustache` (sortOrder 240, `pageIsForm`; Menora borrows it). Default icon `FilePlus` (new in `NAV_ICONS`). The wizard-only names became create-page names (`PageLinks.createPageOf` = wizard ∪ form; `hasCreatePage`/`createPageId`/`recordEditsInPage`/`calendarCreatesInPage`), with no byte change.
- *Entity workflow* (`EntityDefinitionDto.workflow`: `{statusField, initial?, assigneeField?, dueField?, transitions: [{name, label?, from[], to, roles?, requires?}]}`, parsed by `FullstackRequestValidator.parseWorkflow`, messages `Entity 'X' workflow: …`). It applies to a writable, table-backed, single-key entity. `statusField` is a non-key ENUM. `initial` defaults to the field's `defaultValue`, else its first value. There are 1–20 transitions, each `name` a unique `^[a-z][A-Za-z0-9]{0,39}$`. `roles` ⊆ ADMIN/USER and need `ldap-auth`/`ldap-auth-rest` among the resolved deps (checked in `FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts`). `requires` names fields or relations once each, never the key or the status. `assigneeField` is a non-key, non-unique STRING; `dueField` is a non-key date. A wizard step may not ask for the status. **The status moves only by transitions:** its field view is `isReadOnly`/`isWorkflowStatus` (the `FieldDefinition` is not mutated, so demo data still cycles the states). It is left out of the form, PUT, bulk and inline edit, and `create` sets the initial state.
  - Backend (gated `hasWorkflows` / per entity `hasWorkflow`, both backend sets via `sourceSet`): `Service.transition(id, name, values, user)` (a `switch` over the transitions: `requireState` 409, `requireRole` 403 / 401 without a user, `requires` 400 with each missing field under `errors`), `claim`/`release` with an assignee, and `Filters.inbox`/`me` → `inboxOf` (`mine`/`unassigned`/`open`; `OPEN_STATES` = the states with an outgoing transition). The controller adds `POST /{id}/transitions/{name}`, `/{id}/claim` and `/{id}/release`, plus an `inbox` param and the `userinfo` header on list, `/stats` and CSV. `web/WorkflowException` (status + `errors`), answered by `ApiExceptionHandler`. `web/CurrentUser` (`idOf(header)`; roles from `PermissionService` groups under `hasLdapAuth`, else none). `web/CurrentUserController` (`GET /api/me` → `{id, roles}`, works without ldap). `DemoDataLoader` assigns every third row to `dev-user`, so "My tasks" has rows in dev. `putWorkflows` runs on **both** render paths.
  - Frontend: per entity `model/workflow.ts` (`TRANSITIONS`, `OPEN`, `<X>Workflow.available`/`between`/`run`/`claim`/`release`) and `ui/<X>Transitions.tsx` (the moves the caller's roles allow, Claim/Release, and a drawer showing only a move's `requires` fields through `<XForm only>`). It also adds `src/shared/api/me.ts` (`useMe`, one cached `/api/me`) and the i18n keys gated `hasWorkflows`. The api client sends the dev `userinfo` when `sendsDevUser` (ldap ∨ workflows). The moves appear in the quick look, the side pane and the record page header. A kanban or board lane drag runs **the one transition between the two states** (`between`: none, not allowed or several → a refusal message; `kanbanIsWorkflow`/`boardIsWorkflow`). The server is authoritative.
  - Found on the way: under optimistic locking, saving a row that links to a versioned entity answered 409, because the id stub carried no version (a Round-6 bug). `withReferences` now resolves such relations with `entityManager.getReference` (`targetLocked`/`hasLockedRelations`).
- *Inbox page* (`type: "inbox"`, `entity` whose workflow has an `assigneeField`; `queues` ⊆ `mine`/`unassigned`/`open`, 1–3 distinct, default `[mine, unassigned]`; `cardFields`, `presetFilter`, `sort` — default due ascending, else key descending; `InboxSpec`, `PageDefinitionDto.queues`). `ScreenInbox.tsx.mustache` (sortOrder 241, `pageIsInbox`; Menora borrows it) shows one tab per queue with its count (`?inbox=<q>&size=1`) and a table of the card fields, status chip, assignee and due date (late ones in red). Each row has `<XTransitions compact>`. A row opens the record page, else a `DetailDrawer` with the moves. `_queue`/`_page` ride in the route, and stale answers are dropped. Default icon `Inbox`; i18n keys gated `hasInboxPages`.
- *Example:* **Approvals** (`approvals`) — `ExpenseClaim` (five-step workflow: submit, approve [ADMIN, asks for `approvedAmount`], send back [ADMIN, asks for `rejectionReason`], reopen, mark paid [ADMIN]; PAID is final) charged to a `CostCenter`. Its pages: a dashboard with two trend KPIs, an activity feed and a launcher, a `my-tasks` inbox over all three queues, a `new-claim` form page, a claims list (table + kanban), and a record page for each entity. Settings `audit`/`optimisticLocking`/`csvExport`/`seedData`. Sales reporting's "Total revenue" KPI has `trend: month`; Inventory has a hidden `new-item` form page. Every example now opens warning-free in the editor (`examples.test.ts`): Tickets lists "All tickets" in the nav (still the queue's third tab) and has a `ticket` record page, Inventory has a "Movement log" list and links to its catalog instead of two dateless count tiles, and Sales reporting has a `sale` record page. Tests that pin the tabs-only Tickets shape (widgets opening the tabs page, rows opening the quick look) build it with `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests.tabsOnlyTickets`.

Pinned by `FullstackPagesIntegrationTests` (`…kpiDrawsATrendSparkline`, `…kpiTrendPlotsOverThePeriodDate`, `…rejectsInvalidKpiTrend`), `FullstackNewPageTypesTests` (`formPage_*`, `rejectsInvalidFormPages`), `FullstackRequestValidatorTest` (`workflow_*`) and `FullstackWorkflowTests` (transitions and the locked status, roles, `/api/me`, inbox queues, demo data, entities without a workflow byte-identical, `approvalsExample_…`). They are built for real by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests` (`…FormPages…`, `…Workflow…`, `…Approvals…` on both sets — the Menora one in Hebrew/RTL — and `…ClassicWorkflow…` without a layout).

**Generated backends, built for real.** `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests` (opt-in, `-Dsmoke.be=true`, needs `mvn` on the PATH) runs `mvn package` on a generated backend, which compiles it and runs its own tests (the `tests` opt's controller slices and the context-load test with demo data). The other generation tests only assert generated Java as text, so a template that emits code that does not compile passes them. It builds Approvals and the Orders entities with every backend opt. The requests name `web, data-jpa, h2, validation` and drop the workflow's roles, because the ldap-auth artifact lives in the company repository.

**Generated aggregation endpoint (`GET /api/x/stats`).** Every entity with something to group,
bucket or reduce gets one: `?groupBy=` (an enum/boolean column, a MANY_TO_ONE relation — grouped by the id it points at,
`root.get(rel).get(pk)`, so no join — or a date column with
`&bucket=day|month|year`), `&agg=count|sum|avg|min|max` over `&field=`, `&top=1..100` (the largest
groups only, biggest first, via `setMaxResults`; a date or missing `groupBy` is a 400), plus the entity's own `q`
and filter params — so a chart always agrees with the list beneath it. It answers
`{"buckets":[{"key","value"}],"total"}` (`total` skips empty groups — an avg over no rows is `null`, never the NPE it used to be — and is `null` when every group is empty); `key` is the enum constant, `true`/`false`, a date bucket
(`2026-09`) or `""` when nothing is grouped. `Service.stats` reuses the list's `Specification`
through the extracted `specOf(...)`, and both `groupBy` and `field` are resolved through generated
whitelist `switch`es — anything else throws `IllegalArgumentException`, which the generated
`ApiExceptionHandler` already answers as 400. Date parts go through Hibernate's
`HibernateCriteriaBuilder.year/month/day`, which lower to `extract(<unit> from x)` and are rendered
per dialect; the portable-looking `cb.function("year", …)` emits a raw SQL `year()` that only H2 and
MySQL register, so **do not "simplify" it back**. There is no `week` bucket, because `extract(week
from …)` is the ISO week on PostgreSQL/Oracle but a locale-dependent one on H2. The gate
(`statsApplicable`, with the three whitelists) is derived in `EntityViewModel.of`
**from the entity model alone** — the backend render context never sees `pages`
(`putPageContext` is frontend-only), and both render paths have to agree on whether the endpoint
exists. The frontend's breakdown charts, trend charts and aggregate tiles all read it; a plain
record count still comes from the list's page metadata (`?size=1`), which is already exact. Pinned
by `fullstackEndpoint_generatesTheStatsRollupForChartableEntities`.

**Round 9 (2026-11): relations, field types, the update patch, and the safety net under them.** Phases 2, 3, 5 and 6 left every existing golden manifest unchanged; the two deliberate changes to generated bytes are named where they happen.

- *Golden output (`FullstackGoldenOutputTests`, runs by default).* Every bundled example × frontend set × locale × classic/layout (plus one Boot 3.5 case) is generated and compared with `src/test/resources/golden/fullstack/<case>.sha` — one sha256 per file. A mismatch names the files and writes the whole case to `target/golden-actual/<case>/` to diff. Re-pin deliberately with `-Dgolden.update=true` and review the manifest diff: it is the list of files a change reaches. The model file's `templatesHash` is masked before hashing (otherwise any template edit would touch all cases). `FullstackExampleRequests` holds the request bodies the other suites share (`fieldTypesBody`, `manyToManyBody`, `oneToManyBody`).
- *Template sync (`db/EntityTemplateSetSync`).* The fullstack template sets live in the database, and used to be seeded once — an upgraded database kept its old templates for ever. Each row now carries `seed_hash` (`V26`): on start a row whose content still hashes to it (nobody edited it), or that has none (first start after the upgrade — **overwritten**), follows the shipped file; a row an admin edited is kept and logged (`seed_hash = "custom"`, preserved by the admin PUT); missing rows are added. `initializr.fullstack.template-sync=off` disables it, `POST /admin/entity-template-sets/reset` re-seeds on demand. The bundled examples follow the same rule (`DataSeeder.syncFullstackExamples`). The dependency **catalog** (file contributions, build customizations) was still seeded once then: an upgraded database kept those rows until edited in admin (since *Catalog sync*, at the end of this file, they follow the release too). Flyway runs `out-of-order` and ignores missing/future migrations, because branches number migrations independently (`V24`/`V25` belong to the unmerged Design Studio branch; Round 9 is `V26`, `V27`). Pinned by `EntityTemplateSetSyncTest`.
- *One line ending.* `GeneratedProjectFiles.normalizeLineEndings` rewrites every generated text file to LF (batch files excepted) at the end of `buildArtifacts`, so a project is the same bytes whatever machine generated it.
- *Names the generated code already uses (`fullstack/ReservedNames`, `fullstack/reserved-type-names.txt`).* An entity called `Page`, `Email`, `Service`, `Version`… clashed with a type the templates import and produced a project that does not compile; about 140 such names are now a 400 (a label can still say it). So is an entity named like a class another generates (`OrderDto` beside `Order`), and a field or relation spelled like a member the generator adds (`customerId` beside relation `customer`, `createdAt` under audit, a collection or its `Count`) — `checkGeneratedMemberClashes`. `ReservedNamesGuardTest` derives the list from the templates (`-Dreserved.print=true` prints it): a template that starts using a new type fails it until the name is added. Not covered: sample classes of optional dependencies.
- *One schema source.* `fullstack/ScaffoldOption` is the single list of `opts.scaffold` values (key, context flag, which render path reads it, per-entity or not); both render paths call `ScaffoldOption.putFlags`, and an unknown project-level key is a 400 (it used to be ignored). `fullstack/FullstackSchema` → `GET /metadata/fullstack/schema`: field types and aliases, relation types, page/widget enums and limits, nav icons, roles, locales, reserved names, scaffold options. `FullstackSchemaTest` pins it to `src/test/resources/golden/fullstack-schema.json`; the UI checks in a copy (`npm run sync:schema`) and derives its constants from it. Identifiers are ASCII (`[A-Za-z_$][A-Za-z0-9_$]*`) on both sides. **Adding an opt** is now: a `ScaffoldOption` constant, the templates, re-pin the schema, `npm run sync:schema`, and a label in the UI's `scaffoldOptions.ts` (its parity test names what is missing).
- *Located errors.* `WizardArgumentException` carries a `path` (`entities[1].fields[3].type`, `pages[2].widgets[0]`): throw sites mark the property with `.on("type")` and every loop adds its segment with `.at("fields[" + i + "]")`. The 400 body is `{error, detail, path?}` (and `side` for an update). Pinned by `FullstackErrorPathTests`.
- *Spring Boot 3.5.* A `3.5.16` version row beside `3.2.1` (still the default for generated projects; the application itself moved to 3.5.16 later — see *Tool upgrade* at the end of this file). Build customizations take a `compatibilityRange` (`V27`, admin form included): the spring-cloud BOM is 2023.0.3 / 2024.0.3 / 2025.0.3 by Boot range and springdoc 2.8.x from Boot 3.4. Chosen from Maven Central — check them against the company mirror. `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests` builds on both lines.
- *Field types.* `FieldType` gains `LOCAL_TIME`, `INSTANT`, `MONEY` (BigDecimal, scale 2, with `FieldDefinition.currency`, default `ILS`), `MARKDOWN` (a TEXT column, rendered as React nodes by the shared `Markdown.tsx` — no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no new dependency), `FILE` and `IMAGE`. What a type gets in the form, a bulk edit and a CSV import is decided by exhaustive switches in `EntityScaffoldContext` (`inputType`, `bulkCoerceJava`, `importKind`, `cellTs`, `detailTs`), so a new constant fails compilation instead of emitting broken code. `FieldType.ALIASES` are the spellings the wire and the editor's quick-add both take.
  - *Attachments.* A `FILE`/`IMAGE` field holds the id (UUID) of a row in `app_attachment`. Gated on `hasAttachments`, both backend sets get `attachment/` — `Attachment`, `AttachmentRepository`, the `AttachmentStorage` interface with `DbAttachmentStorage` (a plain `@Component`: a project swaps in object storage by declaring its own implementation `@Primary`), `AttachmentController` (`POST /api/attachments`, `GET /api/attachments/{id}`) and `AttachmentConfig` (`app.attachments.max-size`, 10MB; 413 from `ApiExceptionHandler`). Bytes are `@JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.LONG32VARBINARY)`, never loaded for a list (the file name is an `@Formula`). A download is `nosniff`; only png/jpeg/gif/webp verified by their magic bytes are served inline, everything else as an attachment — so an uploaded SVG or HTML cannot run. An attachment is deleted with its row or when replaced. **Limits:** verified on H2 only (check the blob mapping on PostgreSQL/Oracle/MSSQL/DB2); abandoned uploads are not collected; anyone who can call the API and knows an id can read the file; no thumbnails. (Storage choices, the access check and the clean-up came later — see *Attachments (2026-11)* at the end of this file.) Attachments are not searchable, sortable, filterable, bulk-editable or importable. Frontend: `api.upload`, `AttachmentField.tsx`, `format.ts`.
- *Relations.* `RelationDefinition` gains `joinTable`, `inverseJoinColumn`, `mappedBy`. `EntityDefinition.relations` stays **the foreign keys** — what every existing reader means — with the other kinds kept apart: `collections` (MANY_TO_MANY) and `children` (ONE_TO_MANY).
  - *`MANY_TO_MANY`* — the owning side of a link through a join table (`<entity>_<field>`, columns `<entity>_id`/`<target>_id` unless named). Both ends single-key, never an entity to itself, never `required`. Entity: `@ManyToMany @JoinTable @BatchSize(100) Set<T>`. The DTO carries `<field>Ids` and `<field>Labels`; the service initializes the sets inside the transaction (the DTO is built after it), resolves ids with one query (an unknown id is a 400) and reads a missing list as "leave the links alone". Filter `?<field>Id=` is an `exists` subquery, so a row with two matching links stays one row. Frontend: `MultiRelationPicker.tsx`, labels in list and details. Unidirectional; not in bulk edit, inline edit, CSV import or page-layout grouping; demo data creates no links; a wizard page cannot set one.
  - *`ONE_TO_MANY`* — the read side of a MANY_TO_ONE the target declares (`Order.lines` over `OrderLine.order`). `mappedBy` names that link, or is inferred when there is only one (`resolveChildLists`; none or several is a 400 that says which). It is the collection `inverseCollections` derives, under the model's name and whether or not the option is on — `@OneToMany(mappedBy)` plus the counting `@Formula`, `<name>Count` on the DTO — and stands in for the derived one, so a link never gets two. In the details it is a read-only table of the first ten child rows (`RelatedRows.tsx`). The templates read `childLists`/`childTables` (built in `buildEntityContext`, where the option is known); `inverseRelations` and `optScaffoldInverse` remain in the context for templates edited in admin.
  - *Cut then:* editable child rows in the parent's form (composition) were planned as the round's last item and left out — a plain one-to-many is read-only, its rows edited on their own page. Round 10, phase 6 added it as `composition: true`.
- *Updating a project (`POST /starter-fullstack.patch`, `fullstack/patch/`).* Body `{base, target, baseTemplatesHash?}` — two ordinary fullstack requests. Both are generated (the per-request contexts cleared between them) and the difference comes back as `<artifact>-update.zip`: `update.patch` (git format; every `index` line carries full blob ids, which is what lets `git apply --3way` merge with a team's own edits), `files/` (each added and changed file whole), `DELETED.txt`, `UPDATE.md` (how to apply, what to do on a conflict), and `X-Update-Added/Changed/Deleted` headers. `menora-model.json` is replaced whole, never patched. `LineDiff` is an in-house Myers diff (the build is offline); `GitPatchWriter` writes the patch; `ProjectUpdateBuilder` the zip. **What it is not:** both sides are generated with today's templates, so the patch is the model change only — it does not bring template upgrades, and where templates moved on since the project was generated a hunk may need the three-way merge. To say which is the case, every generated `menora-model.json` now ends with `generator.templatesHash` (`templatesHash`: the two template sets' files; also the `X-Templates-Hash` header of the zip response). Generated projects also gain a root `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`). Those two and a README paragraph are Round 9's deliberate changes to existing output. Pinned by `LineDiffTest`, `GitPatchWriterTest` and `FullstackPatchTests` — the last runs the whole journey with real git (generate, commit, edit by hand, change the model, `apply --3way`) when git is on the PATH.
- *Examples:* Blog has tags (`Post.tags`, MANY_TO_MANY) and a page for them; Orders declares `Order.lines` (ONE_TO_MANY); Approvals' amounts are MONEY and a claim takes a `receipt` (FILE).
- *Found on the way:* a decimal or date field with a `defaultValue` plus the `seedData` opt generated a `DemoDataLoader` that did not compile (unqualified types) — fixed; the generated controller test named JUnit's `Test`, clashing with an entity called `Test` — now fully qualified; the frontend Dockerfiles copy `package-lock.json` when there is one.

**Round 10 (2026-11), phase 1: the fix sweep.** Defects in generated code, found by reading the generators and — for the ones no test could see — by driving a generated backend. Each has a pin; the deliberate changes to generated bytes are named.

- *Display text is escaped where it is spliced.* A field or entity label, a relation label, the dashboard heading and blurb are whatever the user typed (Hebrew abbreviations carry `'` and `"`), and were spliced raw into TS literals, JSX attributes and JSX children — a project that did not compile. `TemplateText.putText(view, key, text)` puts the text with its escaped forms: `<key>Ts` (single-quoted TS literal), `<key>Attr` (double-quoted JSX attribute: `&quot;`, never a backslash), `<key>Jsx` (JSX children), `<key>Comment` (block comment). **A frontend template never splices `{{label}}`, `{{entityLabel}}`, `{{entityLabelPlural}}`, `{{dashboardTitle}}` or `{{dashboardOverview}}` raw** — `TemplateSpliceGuardTest` fails on one. Text without `' " \ { } < >` keeps its bytes.
- *Relations take a `label`* (`RelationDefinitionDto.label`, ≤ `limits.maxRelationLabel` = 80; any kind). `TemplateText.labelOf` falls back to the PascalCase field name, so a request without one keeps its bytes. It names the column heading, form label, filter, import column, card field and chart axis; a ONE_TO_MANY's names its count column (`collectionLabel*`) and its table of child rows.
- *Attachments behind a hand-written datasource config.* Every database but the default H2 gets a `<Db>Config` class that names the packages it scans; the attachment entity and repository are outside them, so the app did not start (`No qualifying bean of type AttachmentRepository`). `FeatureContext.supportPackages` lists the packages the scaffold adds beside `<domain>.entity` / `.repository`; `DynamicProjectGenerationConfiguration` hands them to the five JPA `*-config-primary` templates as `dsExtraPackages`. **A new generated package that holds a JPA entity must be added to `supportPackages`** (the saved views' `views` package was, in phase 9). These templates are catalog rows: an upgraded database keeps the old ones until re-seeded or edited in admin.
- *Per-entity audit.* `JpaAuditingConfig` follows `hasAudit` (`putAudit`: any writable entity that resolves the option on), not the project option — an entity that opted in alone got timestamps nothing filled.
- *Soft delete with optimistic locking.* Hibernate binds id **and** version to a versioned entity's delete, so `@SQLDelete` ends `AND version = ?` under `lockingApplicable` (with one placeholder every DELETE answered 409). `Repository.restore` moves the version on and, with audit, sets `updated_at`.
- *Bulk calls keep what a single save keeps.* `Service.updateMany` / `deleteAll` answer 404 naming the ids that name no row, before anything changes (`requireAll`); a required field cannot be cleared (400); with the validation starter the value passes the DTO's constraints (`validator.validateProperty` on the first changed row). The Validator is injected on `usesValidator` (= the CSV import ∨ `bulkValidates` ∨ `transitionValidates`).
- *Workflow.* The **assignee** is locked like the status (`isWorkflowAssignee`; `isWorkflowLocked` = either, which the form reads): out of the form, PUT, bulk and inline edit and the import, null on create. `POST /api/x/{id}/assign {assignee}` hands an unfinished row to another user, or — blank — back to the unassigned queue; it needs the ADMIN role when the project has an ldap-auth dependency (`assignNeedsAdmin`) and is any signed-in user's without one (no roles exist). The service takes `CurrentUser` on `serviceUsesCurrentUser`. The frontend offers **Reassign** beside the moves, outside a table row. A transition's values pass the DTO's validation (`requireValid` → the 400 with `errors`). New 400s: a required `assigneeField`, a wizard step or a transition that asks for the assignee. The `secured` hints cover transition, claim, release and assign. **A workflow status is never asked of a client:** its view has `isRequired = false` (no `@NotNull` on the DTO, no client-side check) while the column keeps NOT NULL through `isNotNullColumn` — a required status used to make every POST without one a 400, hidden by the form pre-filling the default.
- *CSV.* `csv()` leads a text cell that starts with `= + - @` with an apostrophe (a spreadsheet would run it as a formula; numbers and dates are not text and are left); the generated `csv.ts` drops that apostrophe on import, so an exported file still round-trips. `importAll` resolves each row's many-to-many ids (`resolveLinks`), an unknown id being that row's `ImportError`.
- *One display function.* `FieldViewModel.displayTs(type, v, enumType, currency, Display.CELL|DETAIL|CARD)` is behind `cellTs`, `detailTs` and the board/inbox cards (an exhaustive switch over `FieldType`): an enum by its label, a boolean in the app's words, a date and a date-time through `format.ts` `formatDate`/`formatDateTime`, money in its currency, an instant in the reader's zone, a file by its name. `format.ts` ships with the first date field; `formatImports` (list page and details) and `cardFormatImports` (board, inbox) name the helpers a file calls. A KPI, progress or record-header tile over a MONEY field passes `currency` to `formatStat` (`putAggCurrency`); charts, pivots and report totals still print plain numbers. This is the sweep's widest change to generated bytes (all golden manifests).
- *DDL import types.* `SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter.mapType`: `TIME` → LOCAL_TIME, a zoned timestamp → INSTANT, `UUID` → UUID; a key column keeps a keyable type.
- *Small.* `FieldType.sortable()` — a list sorts by neither long text nor a file: out of the controller's `SORTABLE`, no `sortKey` on the column, and a page `sort` or a recent widget's `sortBy` naming one is a 400. The classic `DashboardPage` and its barrel are generated only without a layout (`classicShell`). The kanban's "no value" key is written as the escape `\u0000` (the template held a raw NUL byte, which made git and the line-ending pass treat the file as binary). Lombok entities keep a lazy `@ManyToOne` out of equals/hashCode/toString.

**Driving a generated backend (`GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.writeLiveTest`).** The generated tests mock the service, so none of the SQL or the checks an option adds were ever executed by a build. `liveApiOfABackendWithEveryOptBehaves` (Orders, every option) and `liveApiOfAWorkflowBackendBehaves` (Approvals) add a `LiveApiTests` class to the generated project — a `@SpringBootTest` with MockMvc over the real context, H2 and the demo rows — before running `mvn package`. Its methods are text blocks in the smoke test (`LIVE_ORDERS_TESTS`, `LIVE_APPROVALS_TESTS`). This is what found the soft-delete 409 and the required-status 400; add a method there for anything a text assertion cannot prove. `attachmentsBehindAHandWrittenDatasourceConfig…` builds with H2's own `h2-primary` role, the same config class the other databases get.

**Round 10, phase 2: the plain generators get a safety net and templates.** No generated byte changed in this phase.

- *Golden manifests for the plain generators (`WizardGoldenOutputTests`, runs by default).* 19 cases (21 since phase 3, 23 since phase 4) under `src/test/resources/golden/wizard/`: `/starter.zip` (default, the common dependencies, Java 17 with a secondary datasource), the SQL wizard in each `apiMode` over `wizard-fixtures/library-postgresql.sql` plus a T-SQL script, the OpenAPI wizard (controllers, with validation, client, both) over `petstore.yaml`, the SOAP wizard (endpoints, client, both) over `countries.wsdl`, all three wizards in one request, and `/starter-multimodule.zip`. It also pins that `/starter-wizard.zip` without a wizard payload is `/starter.zip` byte for byte. The fixtures are written to reach every shape a generator renders differently — **extend the fixture when a wizard learns a new shape**. `GoldenManifests` is the hashing/diff/dump code both golden suites share (`-Dgolden.update=true` re-pins; a failing case is dumped under `target/golden-actual/wizard/<case>/`).
- *One request builder (`config/ProjectRequestFactory`).* `/starter.zip` is bound by the framework, which fills what a caller left out from the metadata. The wizard, fullstack and multi-module endpoints each built the framework's `WebProjectRequest` by hand with their own hard-coded defaults (`"3.2.1"`, `"21"`), so changing the default version in the Versions table moved `/starter.zip` alone. All three now call `requestFactory.create(new Base(…), dependencies)`: every default from the metadata, an unknown Boot or Java version a `WizardArgumentException` (400 naming the parameter) on all of them, `configurationFileFormat` always set (see the gotcha in the root `CLAUDE.md`). **A new endpoint that builds a request by hand goes through it.**
- *Multi-module.* The zip and the preview assemble one tree through `MultiModuleController.assemble` (they were two copies). No known module among those named is a 400 `{error, detail}` on both (the zip answered a bare 400, the preview a 200 holding a parent POM with no modules); the preview with no module named stays an empty 200 (the editor asks while the user is still ticking).
- *Honest metadata.* `application.yml` no longer lists Kotlin or `gradle-project`: every build customizer is a `BuildCustomizer<MavenBuild>`, every template Java, the Dockerfile and Jenkinsfile run `mvn`. The framework turns a request naming either away. The two `configuration-file-formats` are still listed although the catalog always writes `application.yaml` — removing one changes what the framework itself writes, so it was left for a round that pins it.
- *Wizard templates (`templates/wizard/**`, `config/WizardTemplates`).* What the wizards and the multi-module controller write themselves is Mustache now, not `StringBuilder` code: `sql/{Entity,ViewEntity,IdClass,Repository,Service,Dto,Mapper,Controller}.java`, `openapi/{Record,Controller,Client,ClientConfig}.java`, `soap/{Endpoint,WebServiceConfig,Client,ClientConfig}.java`, `multimodule/parent-pom.xml`. `WizardTemplates.render(name, view)` reads `templates/wizard/<name>.mustache` (LF whatever the checkout) and renders through `CompiledTemplates`. The generators keep the parsing and build the view; irregular fragments are precomputed strings in the view (an entity member's annotation lines, a method's argument list) rather than template logic. Two things to know when editing one: a `${…}` placeholder beside a tag must be put in the view whole (`baseUrlPlaceholder`) — `${{{x}}}` reads as a triple brace — and a section tag alone on its line emits nothing, which is what the blank-line handling relies on. **Classpath files, not database rows:** a wizard template is written against a Java view-model an admin edit could not follow, and the entity-template tables, their sync and their admin screen are shaped for the fullstack sets. `openapi/SchemaRecordEmitter` is the half of the OpenAPI wizard that has nothing to do with HTTP (component schemas → records, the type mapping), kept apart for the events wizard.
- *How byte-equality was held.* The manifests were pinned on the `StringBuilder` emitters first; each generator was then moved and had to pass unchanged. For the SQL wizard the fixture was extended (a shared-key one-to-one, a composite foreign key to an unknown table, a schema-qualified table), pinned once more with the old emitter, and only then checked against the new one.

**Round 10, phase 3: wizard depth.** What the three wizards read and write, widened — and, for the first time, built. Every change to generated bytes here is deliberate and re-pinned.

- *Wizard output is built for real now.* None of it ever had been: the tests compared text. `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests` (`-Dsmoke.be=true`) gained `sqlWizardBackendCompilesAndCreatesItsSchema` (the library fixture, two API modes, Hibernate creating the schema from the entities), `sqlWizardBackendMigratesWithItsBaselineAndPassesItsTests` (Flyway + validation + generated tests, with `ddl-auto: validate` for the project's tests — the entities have to match the schema the DDL itself made) and `openApiWizard*` (below). `postAndExtractWizard` unpacks a `/starter-wizard.zip`; `writeProjectTest` adds a test class to the project before it is built. **Three SQL-wizard mappings did not start an application**, all fixed: a table without a primary key was an `@Entity` with no `@Id` (now written without `@Entity`, with a TODO, and no repository); a composite-key column that is also a foreign key was replaced by its association, leaving the `@IdClass` nothing to bind to; a key that is the whole key *and* a foreign key (a shared-key one-to-one) was `@Id @OneToOne @MapsId` on the association alone, which Spring Data cannot type. Both key cases are now a scalar `@Id` with a read-only association through the same column (`insertable = false, updatable = false`; `@OneToOne` for a whole key, `@ManyToOne` for a part) — `SqlEntityGenerator.isKeyColumn`.
- *SQL: what is read.* `ALTER TABLE … ADD [CONSTRAINT x] PRIMARY KEY | FOREIGN KEY | UNIQUE` (`applyAlterTable` — how pg_dump, SSMS and most exports declare constraints; ALTER used to be skipped whole, so such a script had no keys, hence no repositories, and no associations); a foreign key written on the column (`team_id BIGINT REFERENCES teams(id)`, `INLINE_REFERENCES`); `UNIQUE` — one column is `@Column(unique = true)`, several are the table's `@UniqueConstraint` (`TableModel.uniqueConstraints`); `CHECK (col IN (…))` as an enum (`<Table><Column>.java`, `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)`, `templates/wizard/sql/Enum.java`) — **only when every value is already a Java identifier**, because the database holds the values as they are and the enum is stored by its constants' names; anything else stays a `String`. `splitStatements` is comment-aware (a `;` or an apostrophe in a comment cut the statement in two). An unknown `apiMode` is a 400 (it fell back to `NONE` silently).
- *SQL: what the build adds* (`sql/SqlProjectFacts`, filled by the contributor from the selected dependencies):
  - `flyway` — the pasted script becomes `db/migration/V1__init.sql`, in place of the dependency's placeholder (the SQL contributor runs after the catalog's file contributor): every statement as written except the SELECTs. `sqlOptions.<dep>.flywayBaseline: false` keeps the placeholder; with several SQL payloads only the first is a baseline. The script is in the dialect it was pasted in — it runs on that database.
  - `validation` — `NOT NULL` → `@NotNull` (never on a key the database generates), a text column's length → `@Size(max)`, a required foreign key → a required id; on the DTO, or on the entity when it is exposed directly (`ENTITY_DIRECT`); controllers take `@Valid` bodies. Without the starter nothing names `jakarta.validation`.
  - `sqlOptions.<dep>.generateTests: true` — a `@WebMvcTest` per controller (`templates/wizard/sql/ControllerTest.java`; `@MockitoBean` from Boot 3.4).
- *OpenAPI: schemas* (`openapi/SchemaRecordEmitter`, `SchemaSet` — one per document, built with the DTO package and whether validation is on; `typeOf(schema, hint)` is the only type mapping):
  - a string `enum` → a Java enum that keeps each wire value (`@JsonValue`/`@JsonCreator`, `Enum.java`); written in a property it is named after its place (`Pet.status` → `PetStatus`). A **parameter** of an enum type stays `String`: Spring binds an enum by its constant's name, and `in-progress` cannot be one;
  - `allOf` → one merged record (it was an empty record with a TODO);
  - `oneOf`/`anyOf` over object schemas → a sealed interface (`Union.java`): `@JsonTypeInfo` by the discriminator (its `mapping` honoured; `EXISTING_PROPERTY` when every member declares the property) or `DEDUCTION` without one. Each member record carries `@JsonTypeInfo(use = NONE)`, so it is still read on its own — otherwise `{"id": 1}` asked for as a `Pet` failed as "cannot deduce". A choice between non-objects stays `Object` (the plan said 400; one exotic schema should not refuse a whole document);
  - a named schema that is not an object (a string with a format, an array, a map, a free-form object) is **not a type**: a reference to it is the type it stands for, constraints included (it used to be an empty record). `additionalProperties` → `Map<String, V>`; an object written in place → a record named after its place (a request body: `<Operation>Request`, a response: `<Operation>Response`);
  - a property whose Java name differs from its name on the wire gets `@JsonProperty` (`first_name` was read and written as `firstName`); a keyword is suffixed (`class_`). A schema named like a type the generated code uses (`List`, `Valid`, `HttpStatus`…) is renamed `<Name>Dto` (`RESERVED_TYPE_NAMES`);
  - `date-time` → `OffsetDateTime`; OpenAPI 3.1's `type: [string, 'null']` is read (`typeName`);
  - with the validation starter: `@NotNull` (required and not nullable), `@Size`, `@Pattern`, `@Email`, `@Min`/`@Max`, `@DecimalMin`/`@DecimalMax`, `@Valid` on nested types — on record components and on controller parameters (`constraintsOf`).
- *OpenAPI: operations.* Parameters are bound by the request's names (`ParamModel.wireName`): `X-Request-Id` was bound as `xRequestId`, so the header never arrived. A cookie is `@CookieValue` (it was `@RequestParam`). Date parameters carry `@DateTimeFormat(iso = …)` (Spring otherwise reads the server locale's short style). Path-level parameters are read. The declared success status — the lowest 2xx, then `2XX`, then `default` — is a `@ResponseStatus` where it is not 200, and decides the return type (a 204 used to lose to `default`, so a delete returned the error body's type). HEAD and OPTIONS are generated. A body is optional only when the document says `required: false`. Clients send the same names, expand path variables in the **path's** order, and use `fromUriString`.
- *OpenAPI: options.* `openApiOptions.<dep>.clientKind`: `restTemplate` (default) or `restClient` (`RestClientClient.java`, `RestClientConfig.java`; unknown → 400). `copySpec: true` keeps the document at `src/main/resources/openapi/<dep>.yaml|json` (written by the contributor).
- *The starters a wizard's code needs are added* (`WizardStarterController.toWebRequest`, only when the catalog has them — `ProjectRequestFactory.offers`): `web` for an OpenAPI payload without `web`/`webflux`, `openapi` (springdoc) beside generated controllers on `web`, `web-services` for a WSDL.
- *OpenAPI, built:* `openApiWizardBackendCompilesAndItsContractBehaves` (controllers + RestTemplate client + validation) and `openApiWizardRestClientCompilesAndCallsTheContract` build the Petstore fixture and run `OPENAPI_CONTRACT_TESTS` / `OPENAPI_REST_CLIENT_TESTS` inside the generated project: JSON round trips of the union, the enums and the renamed properties, the constraints through a `Validator`, controller binding through MockMvc (a bound call reaches the stub's `UnsupportedOperationException`), and each client against a `MockRestServiceServer`. **Add a method there for anything about generated wizard code a text assertion cannot prove.**
- *SOAP.* A WSDL that refers to another file — `wsdl:import`, or `xsd:import`/`include`/`redefine` with a `schemaLocation` — is a 400 naming each reference (`unresolvedImports`, on generate and on the preview). It is found by scanning the document **before** wsdl4j parses it, with a parser that takes no DOCTYPE: wsdl4j's reader opens a `schemaLocation` itself, relative to the server's working directory or by URL. An `xsd:import` of a namespace alone is fine. Multi-file WSDLs remain unsupported. (Since *Round 14, phase 6* a WSDL may come with the files it refers to; one document that refers to a file is still this 400.)
- *Not done from the plan:* a shared `SqlConstraintReader` (the fullstack DDL import keeps its own CHECK/FK reading in `SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter`; both read the same `TableModel`); multipart/form request bodies (still `@RequestBody` over the first media type); a real build of SOAP output (the JAX-WS plugin's artifacts are not known to be in the offline repository).

Pinned by `SqlEntityGeneratorTest` (63 cases), `OpenApiCodeGeneratorTest`, `SoapCodeGeneratorTest`, `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.wizardsAddTheStartersTheirCodeNeeds` / `sqlWizardRefusesAnUnknownApiMode` and 21 wizard golden manifests.

**Round 10, phase 4: the events wizard (AsyncAPI → Kafka).** A fourth wizard on `/starter-wizard.zip`, keyed by the `kafka` dependency. No existing manifest moved.

- *Wire.* `asyncApiByDep: {kafka: "<document>"}` and `eventOptions: {kafka: {subPackage?, payloadSubPackage?, groupId?, channels?: [{name, produce, consume}], generateTests?}}` on `WizardStarterRequest`. `POST /starter-wizard.detect-channels {spec}` → `[{name, address, sends, receives, messages, problem}]` for the drawer (400 `{error: "Invalid AsyncAPI document", messages}` for a document that cannot be used — the same body generation answers). `GET /metadata/events-capable-deps` → `["kafka"]`. `kafka` is added to the build when the caller named no such dependency.
- *Reading (`events/AsyncApiReader`).* 2.x and 3.x, YAML or JSON, read as a tree — there is no AsyncAPI library in the offline repository, and the wizard needs little of the specification. **The two versions say who sends in opposite words:** 2.x's `publish`/`subscribe` are a *client's* (what clients subscribe to, the application sends); 3.x's operations say `send`/`receive` from the application's own side. Both come out as `EventChannel.sends()`/`receives()` — what the described application does. A channel's `name` is its key under `channels`; its `address` is 3.x's `address` (else the key), 2.x's key. Messages: 2.x `message` or `message.oneOf`; 3.x the channel's `messages`, limited to those an operation lists. Refused, each with its reason: no `asyncapi` field, a version other than 2.x/3.x, no channels, a `schemaFormat` that is not JSON Schema (AsyncAPI's own, JSON Schema, OpenAPI — so Avro is a 400), a `$ref` out of the document or into nothing. A parameterized address (`users.{id}.inbox`) cannot be a Kafka topic: the channel carries a `problem`, is left out by default and is a 400 when asked for.
- *Which channels (`EventsCodeGenerator.plan`).* No `channels` in the options: the project **is the application the document describes** — a producer where it sends, a listener where it receives. With `channels`, exactly those, in the directions given (that is how the other party is generated; `{produce: false, consume: false}` leaves one out). An unknown name, a name twice, or nothing left to generate is a 400 (`WizardArgumentException`).
- *What is written* (`templates/wizard/events/{Producer,Listener,ProducerTest,ListenerTest}.java`, default packages `events` and `events.model`):
  - `<Channel>Producer` — `send(String key, <Payload> payload)` over `KafkaTemplate<String, String>`; several kinds of message on one channel give a method each (`send<Message>`).
  - `<Channel>Listener` — `@KafkaListener`, reads the payload and calls `handle(key, payload)`, a stub. Several kinds of message: nothing in a record says which, so it is handed the `JsonNode`. `groupId` is the option, else `${spring.kafka.consumer.group-id}`.
  - **Messages travel as JSON text.** The catalog's Kafka dependency configures `KafkaTemplate<String, String>` and String (de)serializers (`KafkaConfig`, `application-kafka.yml`); that is left alone, and the generated classes convert with the application's `ObjectMapper`. `eventsBuildCustomizer` adds `spring-boot-starter-json`, since a project with Kafka and no web starter has neither Jackson nor that bean.
  - A topic's name is a property, `app.events.topics.<channel-in-kebab-case>`, set to the channel's address in `application.yaml` (merged).
  - The payload types are the OpenAPI wizard's. Payload schemas are JSON Schema, which is what an OpenAPI 3.1 document's schemas are: `Payloads` puts the document's `components.schemas` — and each payload written in place, under its message's name — into a synthetic 3.1 document, parses it with swagger-parser and reads it with `SchemaRecordEmitter.read`. **A change to the type mapping there reaches both wizards.** Every schema the document names becomes a type, used by a chosen channel or not.
  - `generateTests`: per producer, the payload out through a mocked `KafkaTemplate` and back from the JSON; per listener, a record in. No broker. A test value is made where one is simple (`sampleOf`: a record with every component null, an enum's first constant, an empty list…); a union payload gets no round-trip test.
- *Plumbing.* `config/EventsSpecContext` is the **sixth** per-request ThreadLocal context: populated in `WizardStarterController.populateContexts`, cleared in its `finally` and by the `InitializrWebConfiguration` backstop (`GenerationContextCleanupTests` covers six). `validateEvents` runs the generator once before generation, so every refusal is a 400, never a half-written project. `eventsCodeContributor` (`@Order(100)`) writes the files.
- *Built for real:* `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.eventsWizardBackendCompilesAndPassesItsTests` — every channel of the 3.x fixture in both directions, with validation and the generated tests: the project compiles, its tests pass, its context starts (no broker is needed for that).
- *Limits.* Kafka only (the document's own bindings and servers are not read). JSON payloads only. Message headers, keys' schemas, correlation ids and traits are not read. A reference from inside a payload to somewhere other than `#/components/schemas/…` is not followed.

Pinned by `EventsCodeGeneratorTest` (the two fixtures, `wizard-fixtures/orders-asyncapi-v{2,3}.yaml`, describe the same events), `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.eventsWizard*` and two golden cases (23 wizard manifests).

**Round 10, phase 5: plain backends keep their model, and are updated from it.** What fullstack projects have had since Rounds 6 and 9, for `/starter.zip`, the wizards and multi-module projects. Every plain, wizard and multi-module golden case changed, deliberately; no fullstack manifest moved.

- *The model file (`config/BackendModel`).* `menora-model.json` at the project's root: `{format: "menora-backend-model/1", kind: "backend" | "multimodule", request, generator: {catalogHash}}`. `request` is a `/starter-wizard.zip` body (plus `modules` for a multi-module project) **as the generation resolved it** — the starters a wizard added, the datasource roles defaulted, each wizard's document with its options written back from the parsed record (`BackendModel.wire`: Jackson over the options records, whose component names are the wire names). Generating a model's own `request` gives the same bytes (pinned).
- *Who writes it.* `backendModelContributor` in `DynamicProjectGenerationConfiguration` (`@Order(LOWEST_PRECEDENCE)`), from the `ProjectDescription` and the per-request contexts — **not the endpoints**: `/starter.zip` is the framework's own controller, and its output has to stay byte for byte what `/starter-wizard.zip` generates without a wizard (still pinned by `WizardGoldenOutputTests`). The same contributor prepends `* text=auto eol=lf` to the framework's `.gitattributes` (`BackendModel.gitAttributes`) and normalizes every text file to LF — a patch made from generated files met CRLF ones on a Windows checkout. `modelHelpCustomizer` (a framework `HelpDocumentCustomizer`) adds *Reopening and updating this project* to `HELP.md`.
- *A project that is part of a larger one writes none.* `ProjectOptionsContext.setEmbedded(true)` — set by `FullstackProjectAssembler` before it generates its backend (the fullstack root has its own model) and by `MultiModuleController.assemble` around its modules — makes the contributor and the help section stand down. A multi-module project gets one model at its root (`MultiModuleController.modelOf`: the root's coordinates, `modules` = the templates that exist, in their order, `dependencies` = what every module was given beside its own) and a root `.gitattributes`. `ProjectOptionsContext.all()` is every selected sub-option as it stands; `clear()` resets the flag.
- *`POST /starter.patch` (`config/BackendPatchController`).* `{base, target, baseCatalogHash?}`; each side is a model file as it is, or just its `request`. A request with `modules` is assembled by `MultiModuleController.assembleModel`, any other generated by `WizardStarterController.generateProject` (the wizard endpoint's own pipeline, contexts cleared after each side). The difference comes back as `<artifact>-update.zip` from `ProjectUpdateBuilder` — the fullstack update's machinery; its `Info` now also carries the project's root in words and what the hashes are of ("templates" / "catalog"), with the fullstack notes word for word what they were. `menora-model.json` is replaced whole, never patched. A model that cannot be generated is a 400 `{error: "Invalid model", side: "base" | "target", detail, messages}`.
- *`generator.catalogHash`.* `BackendModel.catalogHash`: the SHA-256 of the file contributions and build customizations **of the model's own dependencies** (in a fixed order, line endings aside). Because it covers only those rows, an update compares the hash the project was generated with (the caller's `baseCatalogHash`, else the base model file's) with the hash of **the base model generated today** — not the target's, whose dependencies may differ — and says in `UPDATE.md` whether the catalog moved. As with fullstack, the patch is the change the *model* makes: both sides are generated with today's catalog.
- *Limits.* The model records the request, not the catalog: an update does not bring catalog changes. Sub-options of a multi-module project are recorded for the dependencies its modules use. `/starter.zip` answers with no header naming the hash, so a project generated in the editor (not imported) has no "generated with" hash until its file is imported.

Pinned by `BackendPatchTests` — the model and its round trip (wizard, `/starter.zip`, multi-module), the update zip and its notes, the 400s, and the whole journey with real git (generate, commit, edit by hand, change the model, `git apply --3way`).

**Round 10, phase 6: composition — child rows edited in their parent's form.** What Round 9 cut. Gated throughout: a request without a composition keeps its bytes (no fullstack manifest moved until the Orders example was given one).

- *Wire.* `composition: true` on a `ONE_TO_MANY` relation (`RelationDefinitionDto.composition`, `RelationDefinition.composition`). The rows of the target are then **part of this entity**: its form edits them, a save carries them, a row left out of a save is removed.
