- *What may be composed (`FullstackRequestValidator.checkComposition`, run where the link behind the list is settled; each a 400 at `…relations[i].composition` that says which rule).* Only a ONE_TO_MANY; never an entity's own rows; the parent writable; the child writable, with a **single generated key** (a new row has no id — that is how it is told from a kept one), its link to the parent **required**, no workflow, no FILE/IMAGE field (rules, show-when and computed fields were refused here too until Round 13, phase 7), no MANY_TO_MANY or ONE_TO_MANY of its own; and one owner per child.
- *Backend (`hasCompositions` on the parent, `isComposedChild` on the child; both backend sets).*
  - Parent entity: `@OneToMany(mappedBy, cascade = {PERSIST, MERGE, REMOVE})` — **without `REMOVE` when the parent is soft-deleted** (`cascadeJava`, from `softDeleteApplicable`): a row deleted softly can be restored, so its rows stay. No `orphanRemoval`: a removed row is deleted explicitly, so the two cases share one code path.
  - Parent DTO: `List<ChildDto> <list>` (`@Valid` with the validation starter — a row's message is keyed `lines[0].quantity`). `from()` fills it only where the collection is initialized (`Hibernate.isInitialized`), which the service does for **one row read by id** (`withRows`) — a page of rows carries `null`. `toEntity()`: no list at all is `null`, meaning "leave the rows as they are" to an update.
  - Parent service: `create` adopts the rows (`adopt<List>`: the row's link is the parent, its key is the database's, its other foreign keys become managed references — `childRefs`); `update` merges (`merge<List>`): a row with the id of a row the parent has is that row with its new values (`Child.copyFrom`), any other is new — **an id that is not one of this parent's rows is never trusted** — and a row not sent is removed. At most `MAX_ROWS` (200) rows in one save. Under optimistic locking a change to the rows force-increments the parent's version. A CSV import and the bulk and inline edits carry no rows.
  - Child: `copyFrom(other)` on the entity (its values and foreign keys, never its key or its parent); the DTO no longer requires the link (`dtoRequired` — inside its parent a row need not say whose it is), and the child's own `create` (and import) asks for it instead. The child's own endpoints and page are unchanged.
- *Frontend (both sets; new files, and small gated blocks in the form, the types, the validator, the wizard and each set's `EntityPage`).* `shared/ui/RowsEditor.tsx` (ships with `hasCompositionRows`): a small table with an input per cell, add and remove. Per composed child, `features/<child>-form/ui/<Child>Rows.tsx` (`EntityRows.tsx.mustache`, gated `isComposedChild`; `putRows`): a column per value a client may set and per foreign key but the link to the parent, a `RelationPicker` or a select per link. The parent's form renders it after its fields; `types.ts` gains `<list>?: Partial<Child>[] | null`; `validate<Parent>` runs `validate<Child>(row, true)` on every row (the `nested` flag skips the link) and keys the messages by place, which is where the server's land too. A row of a list has no rows of its own, so `EntityPage.openEdit` reads the record by id and merges the lists into the open form (a save before they arrive leaves them alone). A wizard gets a step per composed list after its own (`wizardHasRows`; the step owns every message under the list). Form pages and the record page's Edit read the record by id already.
- *Example.* Orders: `Order.lines` is a composition.
- *Limits.* One level (a composed child composes nothing). The rows are replaced as a set by whoever saves last unless the parent is versioned. No paging inside the form: 200 rows. The details still show the rows read-only (`RelatedRows`). The editor's canvas does not draw the rows editor or the wizard's extra step.

Pinned by `FullstackCompositionTests` (both backend shapes, both frontend sets, every 400) and `ReservedNamesGuardTest` (`RowsEditor`, `RowColumn` are reserved entity names now). Driven for real by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.liveApiOfAComposedParentBehaves` (both backend sets, with soft delete and locking: rows saved, read, changed, removed, checked by place, never taken from another parent, kept through a soft delete) and built by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*Composition*` (Orders layout on tailwind, classic Hebrew on Menora).

**Round 10, phase 7: rich fields and output.** An Excel export, an editor for Markdown fields, pictures on cards, a drop zone on the attachment field and printable record pages. Each is gated; the manifests that moved are the examples that were given the feature (Orders: the Excel option; Blog: `Post.body` is MARKDOWN; Approvals: the claim page prints) and Approvals again for the attachment field, which every project with a FILE or IMAGE field ships.

- *Excel export (`xlsxExport`, a `ScaffoldOption`, per entity like `csvExport`).* `GET /api/x/export.xlsx` beside the CSV endpoint — **the same parameters, copied from it** (`q`, the filters, `sort`, a workflow's `inbox` and user), so the workbook is the list on screen; the CSV endpoint's bytes are untouched.
  - `web/XlsxWriter` (`XlsxWriter.java.mustache`, gated on the project flag `hasXlsxExport` = any entity resolving the option on — `FeatureContext.putXlsxExport`, both render paths; the Lombok set borrows it). **No library**: an `.xlsx` is a zip of XML parts, written straight to the response with `ZipOutputStream` — five fixed parts, then the sheet, streamed row by row in the 500-row chunks the CSV export reads. Apache POI was decided against (a large dependency in every generated backend, for one sheet of cells).
  - Cells: a `Number` is a number cell, a `Boolean` a boolean, everything else **inline text** (`t="inlineStr"`), dates and enums included (ISO text and the constant, as in the CSV). Text is never a formula in SpreadsheetML unless it is written as one, so the CSV's apostrophe guard is not needed and not applied. The header row is bold (`styles.xml`, one extra font). A sheet name loses the characters Excel forbids and is cut to 31.
  - Columns (`putXlsxColumns` → `xlsxHeaders`, `xlsxCells`, `xlsxSheetName`, built in `buildEntityContext` only when the entity's option is on): every DTO value under **the label the UI gives it**, Java-escaped (`SqlNames.javaLiteral`) — a foreign key by the row it points at where that row has a name (`<field>Label`, else the id), a file by its name, a many-to-many by its names joined with `; `, the audit pair last. The cell expressions are Java built in Java; the template only lists them.
  - Frontend (both sets' `EntityPage`): an **Excel** button beside Export. The page builds the query itself from its `debouncedSearch`, `filters` and `sort` and downloads through `api.download` (imported as `sheetApi`, gated) — `useResource` keeps its bytes. `usesDownload` (CSV ∨ Excel) gates the one `useDownload` busy flag. Strings `exportXlsx`/`excel` on `hasXlsxExport`.
  - Either export counts as "an action to hide" for a list page's `hideActions` (`FullstackPageValidator.listSpec`, `listActionsConfigurable`); the `export` action hides both buttons.
- *Markdown editor.* A MARKDOWN field's form control is `shared/ui/MarkdownEditor.tsx` (ships with `hasMarkdownFields`, borrowed by the Menora set; the form imports it on the per-entity `hasMarkdownField`) instead of a bare textarea: Write / Preview, eight buttons (bold, italic, heading, bulleted and numbered list, callout, link, code — exactly the subset `Markdown.tsx` reads) and Ctrl+B / I / K. A line button toggles. **The preview is `Markdown.tsx` itself**, the component the details show the value with, so nothing typed is ever markup here either. A composed child's rows editor still takes long text in a plain input.
- *Pictures on cards.* `displayTs(IMAGE, …, Display.CARD)` is a 40px thumbnail (it was the file's name), so a board card and an inbox row show an IMAGE field as the table already did; `putCardFormatImports` imports `attachmentUrl` for it. A FILE on a card is still its name.
- *Attachment field.* `AttachmentField.tsx` takes a file **dropped** onto it (only a drag that carries files; an image field refuses another type before sending), shows the picked picture from the browser's own copy while it uploads, and says `dropHere` beside the button.
- *Printable record pages.* `printable: true` on a `record` page (`PageDefinitionDto.printable`, `PageDefinition.RecordSpec`; on another type a 400; absent and false are the same bytes). The screen gets a **Print** button (`window.print()`) and `print:hidden` on its own controls (back, previous/next, actions, the tab strip); the project flag `hasPrintablePages` appends an `@media print` block to both sets' `index.css.mustache`: the shell's scrolling, window-high boxes become ordinary flow (otherwise paper shows one sheet), and `aside`, `header`, `footer`, `nav`, dialogs and toasts are not printed. What prints is what the page shows — the open tab. **`PageDefinitionDto` has a chain of older constructors:** a new property goes on the canonical one, and only the constructors that call the canonical one pass a `null` more.
- *Limits.* The workbook has one sheet, no column widths, no date cells (ISO text sorts and filters correctly, but is not a date to Excel) and no composed rows. Print styles cover record pages only.

Pinned by `FullstackXlsxExportTests` and `FullstackRichFieldsTests`. Driven for real: `liveApiOfABackendWithEveryOptBehaves` reads the workbook back as a zip of XML from a built backend (parts, header, typed cells, escaping, sort and search); both frontend sets are built with the button alone and beside the CSV (`fullstackFrontend*Composition*`) and with the editor, the card pictures and a printing record page (`fullstackFrontend*RichFields*`). The writer's output was also opened once in Excel itself (2,001 rows; typed cells, bold header, Hebrew, a leading `=` shown as text) — by hand, not by a test.

**Round 10, phase 8: the shell's tools.** Three things a layout's header can carry beside its theme switch, each its own setting on the request's `nav` and off unless asked for — a layout that asks for none keeps its bytes. They need a page layout (`nav` does), and **both shells take them**: the Menora shell still ignores `style` and `collapsibleGroups`, not these.

- *Wire.* `nav.userMenu`, `nav.notifications`, `nav.commandPalette` (Booleans on `NavDto`, left out of the echoed request when absent; `PageDefinition.Nav`, `hasTools()`). `FullstackPageValidator.validateNav(raw, pages, entities)`: `notifications` without an entity whose workflow has an `assigneeField` is a 400 at `nav.notifications` — the bell would have nothing to count. An example's `settings.nav` takes the three keys (`FullstackExampleAdminController`).
- *Frontend (`src/app/shell/`, new files; one import and one element in each `App.tsx.mustache`).* `ShellTools.tsx.mustache` (gated `hasShellTools`) wraps the shell's own theme switch and holds what is generated: the palette's `PAGES` and `ENTITIES` and the bell's `BELL`. The three components are static files, borrowed by the Menora set, each on its own flag (`shellPalette`, `shellNotifications`, `shellUserMenu`; `dismiss.ts` — source `ShellDismiss.ts` — on `shellPopovers`), as are their strings. View-model: `PageContext.putShell` (frontend context only).
  - `CommandPalette` — a button and **Ctrl+K / ⌘K** (also while a field has the focus). Pages by name: the nav's pages and the hidden wizard/form pages that start a record (`palettePages`), through `canSee` when the layout has page roles. Records by name: every entity that is searched by text and has somewhere to open a match (`paletteEntities`, at most 8) is asked `?q=` at once, 200 ms after the typing stops, five matches each, a stale answer dropped; a match opens its record page, else the entity's list with the words (`_q`). With nothing typed it lists what was opened through it before (`localStorage` `palette-recent`, six records). Arrows, Enter, Escape (which closes the palette alone).
  - `NotificationBell` — **no table: it is derived from the workflows' own queues.** Per entity whose workflow assigns its tasks (`bellSources`): `?inbox=mine`, `?inbox=mine&<due>To=<yesterday>` (overdue, when the workflow has a due date; a date-time gets the end of that day) and `?inbox=unassigned`, each read as a count (`size=1`), on opening, every minute while the tab is in view and on focus. The number on the bell is "assigned to you"; it stands out while it is higher than this browser has seen (`localStorage` `bell-seen`, lowered again when the count falls). A line opens the entity's inbox page in the nav on that queue (`_queue`, when the page offers it), else the entity's home page.
  - `UserMenu` — the user id and roles of `GET /api/me` (`useMe`) and the keyboard shortcuts (`/`, `N`, and Ctrl+K with the palette). No sign-out: signing in and out are the gateway's.
- *Backend.* `web/CurrentUser`, `web/CurrentUserController` (`GET /api/me`) and the frontend's `shared/api/me.ts` were gated on a workflow; they are gated on **`usesCurrentUser`** now — a workflow, or a shell that shows the user. `buildProjectContext` sets it from the workflows; the frontend path adds the menu and the bell in `putPageContext`; the backend path never sees `nav`, so the controller hands it over through `EntityDefinitionContext.populate(…, shellUser)` → `shellUser()` (cleared with the rest). The API client sends the dev `userinfo` header for a user menu too (`sendsDevUser`). The files themselves are unchanged.
- *Example.* Approvals turns all three on (its five layout manifests moved: the shell, the five new files, the strings, the model file).
- *Not done from the plan:* `/api/me` gains no `name` — there is no directory lookup in a generated project to take one from, so the menu shows the id (and two letters of it on the button). The palette's "recent" are the records opened **through the palette**, not every record visited. The editor's canvas does not draw the tools.
- *Limits.* The bell counts; it does not list tasks or push — a minute's poll per workflow entity, three small requests each. The palette asks up to eight entities per pause in typing. Neither is offered in the classic shell.

Pinned by `FullstackShellToolsTests` (each tool alone, both shells, the palette's and the bell's targets, `/api/me` without a workflow with every other backend file unchanged, the 400). Built for real by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*ShellTools*` (Approvals on tailwind; on Menora in Hebrew with a page limited to a role).

**Round 10, phase 9: saved views.** A scaffold option, `savedViews` (a `ScaffoldOption`, per entity like `csvExport`): a list's search, filters, sort, page size, view and hidden columns kept **on the server** under a name, per user. The per-browser `listPrefs` stay as they are — this is the same idea, shared across machines and people. A request without it keeps its bytes.

- *Backend (`{{packageName}}.views`, gated on the project flag `hasSavedViews`; the Lombok set borrows it).* `SavedView` → table `app_saved_view` (`list_name`, `owner_id`, `view_name`, `shared_view`, `default_view`, `view_state`, `updated_at`; unique on list + owner + name — the DDL for a schema not created from the entities is in the class's Javadoc), `SavedViewRepository`, `SavedViewService` (the rules) and `SavedViewController`:
  - `GET /api/views?list=` — the caller's own views of that list and everyone's shared ones; `POST` `{list, name, state, shared?, defaultView?}`; `PUT /{id}` (what is sent changes, a `null` is left; `list` never changes); `DELETE /{id}`.
  - **A view belongs to whoever saved it** — the `userinfo` header, as everywhere else. Only the owner changes or deletes it: to anyone else `PUT`/`DELETE` answer 404, as for a view that is not there. `shared` offers it to every user of the list, read-only (`mine: false`). `defaultView` is each user's own choice among their own views, one per list (a new default takes over; someone else's shared default is `false` to you). Without a header every caller is the one owner `anonymous` — views still work on a setup with no sign-in, and are then everyone's.
  - `list` is the entity's name in kebab case, and must be one the project was generated with the option for (`savedViewLists` → the service's `LISTS`; anything else is a 400). The name is 1–80 characters, the owner's alone per list (409). At most 50 views per user and list.
  - **The state is the frontend's own JSON**: the server checks that it is an object of at most 4,000 characters, stores it as text and hands it back as an object. It does not read it, so the list page can remember more without a change here. (The plan said 8 KB; 4,000 fits a plain `varchar` on every database the catalog offers, and a list's settings are a few hundred characters.)
  - The package holds a JPA entity, so it is in `FeatureContext.supportPackages` — a hand-written datasource config (every database but the default H2) scans it. That method now takes whether the request's option is on (`DynamicProjectGenerationConfiguration` passes it).
- *Frontend (both sets).* `shared/ui/savedViews.ts` (`useSavedViews(list)` — every change reads the list again — and `fitViewState`, which drops a sort, a view or a page size the list no longer offers, since the model may have changed since the view was saved) and `shared/ui/ViewsMenu.tsx`: apply a view; name what is on screen to save it; for a view of one's own — update it with what is shown, rename, share, make it the default, delete (asked twice). Each `EntityPage.tsx.mustache` gains two gated blocks: `viewState`/`applyView` beside its own state, and the menu at the start of its header actions. An embedded list (a tab, a record's related rows) has no menu.
  - **What opens a list**, in order: what the route says; else the user's default view (applied once the views have loaded, and only when the list opened with nothing asked of it — `opensPlain`); else this browser's `listPrefs`; else the page's own props. Applying a view replaces the filters whole (a page's preset included — the view was saved with them).
  - On a list page that keeps its state in the route, an applied view lands in the URL like any other change — so a link to "this view" is the page's own address. That is why the plan's `_view=<id>` route parameter was not added: it would name the same state twice.
  - The API client sends the dev `userinfo` header when the project has saved views (`sendsDevUser`), so a view has an owner in development too.
- *Example.* Tickets turns it on.
- *Not done from the plan:* `_view=<id>` in the route (above); pinning a view to the nav (it would be runtime data inside both shells — cut in the plan itself); the `secured` hints and springdoc annotations on the views controller.
- *Limits.* A shared view is read-only to others, who can save it again under their own name. No per-role sharing. A view of a list is not offered on another page of the same entity with a different preset as a different thing — views are per entity, not per page.

Pinned by `FullstackSavedViewsTests` (what is generated and for which entities, both backend sets, both frontend sets, the datasource config). Driven for real by `liveApiOfABackendWithEveryOptBehaves` → `aSavedViewBelongsToWhoeverSavedIt` (ownership, sharing, one default, rename, every refusal, the anonymous owner) on a built backend; the menu is built by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*SavedViews*` (the Orders layout on tailwind; classic Hebrew on Menora).

**Round 10, phase 10: new visuals.** Each is gated; the manifests that moved are the examples given the feature.

- *Workflow move log (`workflow.trackMoves`).* **Opt-in per workflow** — the plan wrote it for every workflow, but that would have added a table to every existing workflow project on its next update. With it on:
  - Backend package `{{packageName}}.workflow` (gated on the project flag `hasWorkflowMoves`; in `supportPackages`): `WorkflowMove` → table `app_workflow_move` (entity, row id, from, to, transition, actor, when, and `seconds_in_from` — how long the row had been in the state it left, worked out when the move is written; the DDL is in the class's Javadoc), its repository and `WorkflowMoveLog`. The entity's `Service.create` calls `moves.created(…)` and `Service.transition` `moves.moved(…)` — **inside the same transaction**, so a refused or rolled-back move leaves no step. The actor is the request's `userinfo` header, read by the log itself (`RequestContextHolder`), so no signature changed.
  - `GET /api/x/{id}/moves` (a row's steps, oldest first; 404 for a row that is not there) and `GET /api/x/moves/time-in-state` (per state rows have left: the average stay in seconds and the number of stays — one grouped JPQL query over the stored durations, so it does not load the log).
  - Demo data: a logged workflow's demo rows are **spread over its states whatever the status' default** (`putWorkflow` overrides the status' `seedExpr`), and `DemoDataLoader` seeds each a short history (`moves.seed`), so the history and the numbers have something to show on a fresh start.
  - Frontend: `shared/ui/MovesList.tsx` (ships with `hasWorkflowMoves`) under a record page's content (`recordTracksMoves`): where the row started and every move since — transition, who, when — newest first, read again whenever the row changes. A section, not a tab: the plan's "Moves tab" would have needed the tab strip on records without related lists.
  - Not logged: a row created by a CSV import or directly in the database has no first step (its first move then has no duration). Claim, release and reassign are not moves.
- *Flow widgets (`widgets-flow.tsx`, shipped on `hasFlowWidgets` — a file of their own, so `widgets.tsx` keeps its bytes; borrowed by the Menora set).*
  - `funnel` — `groupBy` is an **enum** (named, else the workflow's status, else the first enum); its values in their own order are the stages. A stage's bar is **everything that got at least that far**: the rows in it now and in every later stage, with its share of the first — read from the ordinary `/stats?groupBy=` rollup, so it honours the widget's preset, the dashboard's period and its filters, and needs no move log. It assumes the stages are passed in order. A `presetFilter` naming some of the enum's values makes those the stages (`stagesTs`) — how a state off the main line (`REJECTED`) is left out. `agg` is `count` or `sum` (anything else does not add up across stages: a 400). A stage drills into the list like a bar.
  - `gauge` — a `progress` tile drawn as a half dial: `agg`/`field` against a required positive `target` (the full-scale value).
  - `timeInState` — an entity whose workflow logs its moves (else a 400 at the widget's `entity`), a title and a span, nothing else: bars of the average stay per state, in minutes, hours or days. It reads the whole log — the dashboard's period and filters do not limit it.
  - `WidgetKind` gains the three (so does the schema); `EntityScaffoldContext` puts `widgetIsFunnel`/`widgetIsGauge`/`widgetIsTimeInState`, `usesFlow`/`flowImports` per dashboard.
- *Gallery list view.* `gallery` joins `table`/`cards`/`kanban`/`calendar` in an entity's `listViews` (and a list page's `view`): the rows as tiles of their picture — the entity's **first IMAGE field** — with the name (the first column) and one more value under it, a tile opening the row. Asked for on an entity without an image field it is dropped, like a calendar without a date (`emittedListViews`). `shared/ui/GalleryView.tsx` ships on the project flag `hasGalleryViews` and is borrowed by the Menora set; each `EntityPage` gains an import, a switch entry (`viewGallery`) and a branch, all gated. It takes the table's columns, search and pager; editing stays on the row's page or the table. View-model: `viewGallery`, `galleryField`, `galleryLabelTs`.
- *Tree page* (`type: "tree"`, `entity`, optional `via`): an entity's rows as an outline, each under the row its **relation to itself** names — `via`, or the only such MANY_TO_ONE the entity has (none is a 400 at the page's `entity`; several without `via` a 400 at `via`; single key only). `via` is kept where a master-detail keeps its own (`PageDefinition.via()`), so no new wire field. `ScreenTree.tsx.mustache` (per page, gated `pageIsTree`; borrowed by the Menora set) reads **up to 500 rows in one call**, in name order, and builds the forest in the browser from each row's `<via>Id` — so the backend is untouched (the plan's `<field>Empty=true` root filter would have changed every self-referencing model's bytes). A row whose parent was not loaded, or is itself, stands at the top; a larger table says it shows the first 500. Branches open and close (Expand / Collapse all), a search keeps the rows that match and what leads to them, a row opens its record page (else the quick look), and New goes to the entity's create page when it has one. Default nav icon `Layers`; strings on `hasTreePages`.
- *Examples.* Approvals logs its moves, and its dashboard has a "Claims pipeline" funnel (Draft → Submitted → Approved → Paid) and a "Time in each step" card. Inventory's items have a `photo` and a gallery view. Project planning's tasks can be part of another task (`partOf`), with a "Work breakdown" tree page; its task list names six columns.
- *Limits of the tree.* 500 rows; a loop of rows that name each other is not drawn (neither is a root); no drag to re-parent — a row's parent is edited in its form.

Pinned by `FullstackWorkflowMovesTests`, `FullstackFlowWidgetsTests`, `FullstackGalleryViewTests` and `FullstackTreePageTests` (the gallery is built by `fullstackFrontend*RichFields*`, the tree by `fullstackFrontend*TreePage*`, each on both sets). Driven for real by `liveApiOfAWorkflowBackendBehaves` → `everyStepOfAClaimIsLogged` (the steps of a claim with their actors, a refused move leaving none, time in state over the demo histories, a 404) on a built backend; built on both frontend sets (`fullstackFrontend*ShellTools*` — Approvals as shipped — and `fullstackFrontendFlowWidgets…` with all three widgets).

### Adding or Modifying a Dependency

The DB is the source of truth. Use the admin API at runtime, or edit the catalog manifests for the initial seed:

1. **New dependency** — POST to `/admin/dependency-groups` + `/admin/dependency-entries`
2. **New file to inject** — POST to `/admin/file-contributions` with `dependencyId`, `fileType`, `content`, `targetPath`
3. **New build customization** — POST to `/admin/build-customizations`
4. **Hot-reload** — POST to `/admin/refresh` (no restart needed)

For a permanent change that survives a fresh DB (e.g. new deployment), edit the relevant manifest under `src/main/resources/catalog/` (drop any new content file under `static-configs/`/`templates/` and reference it via `contentResource`). See the catalog-manifest table above.

### Sub-Options (Optional Per-Dependency Files)

Some dependencies expose sub-options selectable by the user (e.g. `consumer-example`, `producer-example` for Kafka). URL convention: `opts-{depId}=opt1,opt2`.

`InitializrWebConfiguration` (the `@Order(MIN_VALUE)` servlet filter) calls `ProjectOptionsContext.populate(request)` before generation and `clear()` after. `DynamicProjectGenerationConfiguration` checks `optionsContext.hasOption(depId, subOptionId)` before writing sub-option-gated files.

Sub-options are managed via `/admin/sub-options`.

### Dependency Catalog in Metadata

`DatabaseInitializrMetadataProvider` (`@Primary` bean via `MetadataProviderConfig`) loads the dependency catalog from the DB. Non-dependency metadata (Java versions, Boot versions, packaging, types) still comes from `application.yml`.

The provider caches the metadata. Call `POST /admin/refresh` to invalidate the cache after DB changes.

`DependencyConfigService.getAllGroupsWithEntries(kind)` returns `GroupEntries(group, entries)`, with the group's entries of that kind filtered into a **new** list. Admin writes rebuild the metadata inside their own transaction, and `DependencyGroupEntity.entries` is an orphanRemoval collection. The rebuild used to `removeIf` cross-kind rows from it, which deleted them from the database whenever groups were reordered. Never mutate that collection on a read path. This is pinned by `GroupReorderKeepsCrossKindEntriesTests`.

A dependency that fails to build (bad `compatibilityRange`, bad `scope`, …) is skipped rather than taking the catalog down, but it then silently vanishes from `/metadata/client`. The skipped rows are recorded in `DatabaseInitializrMetadataProvider.getLoadFailures()` and returned by `/admin/refresh` as `{"message": …, "failed": […]}`; the admin UI shows them as an error toast.

### Dependency Version Compatibility Ranges

Each `DependencyEntryEntity` has an optional `compatibilityRange` field (column: `compatibility_range`). When set, the Spring Initializr framework automatically:

- Excludes the dependency from `/metadata/client` responses when the selected Boot version falls outside the range
- Includes `"versionRange"` in the metadata JSON for clients to display

**Range syntax** (interval notation):
- `[3.2.0,4.0.0)` — Boot ≥ 3.2.0 and < 4.0.0 (most common)
- `3.2.0` — Boot ≥ 3.2.0 (open upper bound)
- `[3.2.0,3.3.0]` — inclusive on both ends

A blank/null range means the dependency is compatible with all Boot versions (default behavior).

Set via the admin UI (Dependencies tab → edit → Compatibility Range field) or in `catalog/dependencies.json` (the `compatibilityRange` field on an entry) for fresh-DB seeds. The range is validated by `dep.resolve()` at metadata-build time — a malformed range throws immediately on refresh.

### Menora Digital design system (`design-menora-digital`)

The Menora Mivtachim customer-site brand (menoramivt.co.il), imported from the "Menora Mivtachim Design System" artifact. Unlike MUI/Chakra/Mantine it is **plain CSS, not palette-aware** (the tokens *are* the brand): `static-configs/frontend/design-menora-digital/` holds `tokens.css` (every colour/type/spacing/radius/shadow token as a `:root` custom property; `--font-sans: 'Almoni', 'Assistant', Arial`), `components.css` (the `.mn-*` classes — Button primary/header/outlined/text, NavLinks, ActionDisc, ServiceBubble, MagazineCard, CarouselArrow, ChatLauncher, Hero, plus the canvas's "proposed" set: SectionHeader, ActionPanel, Tabs, DropdownMenu, SearchField, ExpertTip, Chip, Footer, Table and the shared `.mn-panel` / `.mn-field` helpers), **17 typed React ports** of those components (`src/shared/ui/menora/*.tsx` + barrel; the source of truth is the "Menora Component Explorer" Claude Design canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/K6M7i6ug16tJbhvNzPekMk — one artboard per component with markup, Props and Rules). The ports live in a dep with no i18n module, so **every string they show is a prop** (the fullstack overlay passes `t('…')`; the bundle components keep their canvas defaults, e.g. ChatLauncher's `פנו אלינו`), a `src/index.css` override (sortOrder 10, keeps `@tailwind` directives when `style-tailwind` is co-selected), a Hebrew showcase `HomePage.tsx` (sortOrder 20, overwrites the `__common__` one) and the 150×46 header logo. The substrate's `fe-app-tsx.mustache` has a `{{#hasDesignMenoraDigital}}` shell branch (white header with logo + `NavLinks` + header pill, page ground, dark footer band, fixed `ChatLauncher`) and `fe-main-tsx.mustache` imports `@fontsource/assistant/{300..700}.css` — Almoni is licensed and **not bundled**, Assistant is the design system's sanctioned fallback (self-hosted via npm, never a CDN link). The logo is a binary, so `FrontendProjectGenerator.copyStaticAssets(targetDir, depIds)` copies it to `public/menora-mivtachim-logo.png` only when the dep is selected. It CONFLICTS with the other four design systems and is React-version agnostic. **Dark mode:** `tokens.css` carries the design system's dark theme (designed from the brand colours — the site has none) under `:root[data-theme="dark"], :root.dark`; the standalone shell ships `useMenoraTheme` + a `ThemeToggle` pill (sets `data-theme` and the `dark` class, persists to localStorage), and the fullstack set's `index.css.mustache` `.dark` block re-points the Tailwind tokens to the same values (purple lifts to `#a393ff`, grounds go slate, shadows black-based). Yellow and its `--on-yellow` label never change; the dark-purple PNG logo always sits on a white chip (`.mn-logo-chip`) so it survives dark grounds. `components.css` deliberately drops the source bundle's `direction: rtl` on `.mn` so `<html dir>` (the `rtl` flag) governs layout; the generator UI turns the RTL toggle on when this design system is picked. A `menora-digital` colour palette (`#684eed`/`#ffc700`) is also seeded so the palette-driven design systems and the default fullstack set can be brand-coloured.

### Department (`{{department}}`)

An admin-managed list (`initializer_department`, `V20`; `DepartmentEntity` — `departmentId` is a
lower-case slug because it lands in k8s names) shared by the Backend, Frontend and Fullstack screens.
Seeded with `lts` as the default by `DataSeeder.seedDepartmentsIfMissing` (table-scoped, so a
deleted row is not resurrected). Admin CRUD at `/admin/departments` (`DepartmentAdminController`,
single-default rule like palettes), public list at `GET /metadata/departments`, included in admin
export/import (`departments`, skipped when an older export lacks it).

**Transport.** The request's id rides on `ProjectOptionsContext.department()`: the filter reads the
`department` query param (so `/starter.zip`, `/starter.preview` and `/starter-multimodule.*` need no
controller change), and the JSON endpoints call `optionsContext.setDepartment(body.department())`
after `populate(opts)` (wizard, fullstack). `/frontend/starter.*` takes it as a `@RequestParam` onto
`FrontendProjectDescription.department`. `ProjectOptionsContext.clear()` resets it, so the filter
backstop covers it.

**Rendering.** `DepartmentResolver.putVars(ctx, id)` adds `department` / `departmentUpper` /
`departmentName` to every context (backend `buildBaseContext`, fullstack backend
`FullstackProjectGenerationConfiguration`, frontend `renderInto` and fullstack `renderFrontend`).
Unknown/blank → the default row → `lts`. Used by both `k8s-values.mustache` (namespace, department,
imageRepo, cert secret, Vault namespace), the log4j2 Kafka topic and the ldap-auth group prefix
(`PermissionService` + the two ldap `application.yaml` fragments — `YAML_MERGE` rows now honour
`substitutionType: MUSTACHE`, and the admin content validator renders them before validating).
Pinned by `DepartmentIntegrationTests`. **Upgraded DBs** keep the old hardcoded-`lts` template rows
until re-seeded or edited in admin (the department table itself appears automatically).

### Frontend Compatibility Rules (REQUIRES / CONFLICTS / RECOMMENDS)

Inter-dependency rules live in `dependency_compatibility` and are tagged by `project_kind`. The endpoint `/metadata/compatibility?projectKind=FRONTEND` returns FE-scoped rules (omit the param to get every row). FE seed rules are in `DataSeeder.feCompat(...)` and cover design-system conflicts, state-mgmt conflicts, and `design-shadcn REQUIRES style-tailwind`.

**Server-side enforcement.** `FrontendCompatibilityResolver` runs inside `FrontendStarterController.buildDescription` after the React-version filter. It auto-adds REQUIRES targets (or drops the source if the target is missing from the catalog) and drops the later-selected dep in a CONFLICTS pair, both with warn logs. This is the safety net for direct API hits (curl, IntelliJ); the UI surfaces the same rules as banners via `useCompatibility('FRONTEND')` so users see the issue before clicking Generate. RECOMMENDS never alter the selection — they only render as suggestions.

### InitializrWebConfiguration

`src/main/java/com/menora/initializr/config/InitializrWebConfiguration.java`

A `@Component`, `@Order(Integer.MIN_VALUE)` servlet filter (extends `OncePerRequestFilter`) that runs before all other filters and wraps every request with three responsibilities:

1. **`configurationFileFormat` default** — injects `configurationFileFormat=properties` when absent
2. **`X-Forwarded-Port` sanitization** — returns empty string if absent/unparseable/`"null"`
3. **Sub-option context** — calls `optionsContext.populate(request)` before and `clear()` after the filter chain

### Shared Generation Helpers

The generation endpoints (`WizardStarterController`, `FullstackStarterController`,
`MultiModuleController`, `ProjectPreviewController`, `FrontendProjectGenerator`) each used to
carry private copies of the same filesystem code. Two shared classes own it now — add to these
rather than re-introducing a private copy:

- `config/GeneratedProjectFiles` — `zipDirectory`, `readSafely`, `copyDirectory` (public, since
  `FrontendProjectGenerator` is in another package)
- `config/PreviewTreeBuilder` — `buildTree(sortedPaths)`, the flat-paths → `TreeNode` shaping
  behind every preview response

### Request performance

- **Open-in-view is off** (`spring.jpa.open-in-view: false`), so a generation no longer holds a pooled
  connection from its first query through rendering and zipping. The admin API still needs it — its endpoints
  return entities whose lazy `group`/`template`/`entries` Jackson reads — so `admin/AdminOpenEntityManagerConfig`
  registers the interceptor for `/admin/**` only. A new non-admin endpoint must return DTOs or fetch what it
  serializes.
- **Responses are gzipped** (`server.compression`, JSON + the Initializr media types, ≥ 2 KB): a fullstack preview
  shrinks ~5×. Tomcat skips responses with a strong ETag (`/metadata/client`), by design.
- **`config/CompiledTemplates`** is the one Mustache compiler (`escapeHTML=false`) with an LRU of compiled
  templates keyed by text — every generator renders through `CompiledTemplates.render`; don't add another
  `Mustache.compiler()`. `FullstackRenderer` builds each entity's context once per render, not once per file.
- **Generation is mostly filesystem time**, so `GeneratedProjectFiles` has `moveDirectory` (the fullstack/multi-module
  backend is renamed into place, not copied), `deleteLater` (temp trees are deleted on a background thread after
  the response; the framework's own `invoker.cleanTempFiles` stays synchronous) and `createParentDirectories`
  (one `isDirectory` check instead of `createDirectories`' failed create per file).
- The metadata is built on `ApplicationReadyEvent` (`MetadataProviderConfig.warmMetadata`), the team-model list
  reads a projection without the snapshot column, and `/metadata/starter-templates` / `entity-template-sets` load
  their child rows in one query.

### Per-Request ThreadLocal Contexts

Six `@Component` holders in `config/` carry per-request state into the generation child
context: `ProjectOptionsContext`, `SqlScriptsContext`, `OpenApiSpecContext`, `SoapSpecContext`,
`EventsSpecContext` (Round 10), `EntityDefinitionContext`. Controllers populate what they need **inside** their `try` and clear
it in the `finally`, but `InitializrWebConfiguration` (the `@Order(MIN_VALUE)` filter) clears
**all six** in its own `finally` as the unconditional backstop — no controller covers every
context, and an exception reaching `GlobalExceptionHandler` clears none. Without that backstop a
leftover context gets scaffolded into the next request served by the same pooled Tomcat thread.
Pinned by `config/GenerationContextCleanupTests`.

### Test Infrastructure

Tests use `src/test/resources/application.properties` which configures an in-memory H2 (`ddl-auto: validate`, schema managed by Flyway; `admin.password=test`). `DataSeeder` runs automatically at test startup and seeds the DB from the catalog manifests, so tests exercise the full DB-driven pipeline.

`src/test/java/com/menora/initializr/TestInvokerConfiguration.java` — a `@TestConfiguration` that provides a `ProjectGenerationInvoker<ProjectRequest>` bean. Test classes import it via `@Import(TestInvokerConfiguration.class)` to invoke project generation directly without HTTP.

**Always release an invoker-generated directory with `invoker.cleanTempFiles(dir)`, not `FileSystemUtils.deleteRecursively(dir)`.** `ProjectGenerationInvoker` is a singleton bean that registers every generated root in a private `temporaryFiles` map and only drops the entry in `cleanTempFiles` — a plain delete removes the files but leaks the map entry for the life of the JVM. `cleanTempFiles` deletes the tree too, so it replaces the delete rather than joining it. It only accepts paths the invoker produced (it NPEs on an unknown path), so directories from `Files.createTempDirectory` still use `FileSystemUtils.deleteRecursively`. Pinned by `InvokerTempFileCleanupTests`.

**Coverage:** `jacoco-maven-plugin` runs during `mvn test` → report at `target/site/jacoco/index.html`. A `check` execution gates the build at 78% instruction / 62% branch (bundle-wide) — set just under the measured 81.5% / 65.0%, so it catches a real regression without tripping on churn. Raise it as coverage rises; never lower it to make a build pass.

**Seeder / admin tests:**
- `db/DataSeederTest` — characterization test: asserts the observable seeded catalog (group/entry/file-contribution counts, representative content, compatibility/sub-option/template/palette/version counts). The regression oracle for catalog-manifest changes — together with `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests` (actual generated file bytes) it pins seeding behavior.
- `admin/AdminApiIntegrationTests` (`MockMvc`) — auth gate (login, 401), validation (400) and orphan-conflict (409) error paths, `/admin/refresh`.
- `admin/ConfigurationExportImportServiceTest` — export → import round-trip preserves row counts and content (runs `@Transactional` so the destructive import rolls back).

**Regression guards added alongside the fixes they pin:**
- `InvokerTempFileCleanupTests` — the invoker's `temporaryFiles` map is empty after `cleanTempFiles` (reflective; the map is private).
- `config/GenerationContextCleanupTests` — the filter clears all five ThreadLocal contexts, on both the success and the exception path.
- `MultiModuleGenerationTests` — `/starter-multimodule.zip` and `.preview` over HTTP, through the servlet stack rather than the invoker in-process, which is what makes them able to catch request-binding bugs. (The wizard tests in `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests` and every fullstack suite go over HTTP too.)
- `GlobalErrorHandlingTests.unknownPathReturns404NotAWrapped500` — the catch-all handler must not swallow `NoResourceFoundException`.
- In `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests`: `blankYamlMerge*` (blank `YAML_MERGE` is a no-op), `deleteContributionResolvesPackagePathPlaceholder` and `deleteContributionGatedOnUnselectedSubOptionDoesNotFire` (the delete pass gates and resolves paths like the write pass).

**Test coverage summary (`ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests`):**
- `metadataEndpointReturnsOk` — HTTP smoke test; checks `kafka` and `rqueue` appear in metadata
- `generatedProjectContainsArtifactoryRepo` — verifies Artifactory repos in generated `pom.xml`
- `generatedProjectContainsVersionDockerfileAndK8s` — checks VERSION content, Dockerfile artifact ID substitution, k8s/values.yaml group ID substitution
- `generatedProjectContainsLog4j2` — verifies `log4j2-spring.xml` present, `logback-spring.xml` absent, `spring-boot-starter-log4j2` in pom
- `generatedProjectContainsEditorconfig` — checks `.editorconfig` present, `application.properties` absent
- `kafkaDependencyInjectsConfigFiles` — checks `application.yaml` contains `bootstrap-servers`, `KafkaConfig.java` present
- `withoutKafkaDependencyNoKafkaFiles` — verifies kafka files absent when kafka not selected
- `securityDependencyInjectsSecurityConfig` — checks `application.yaml` + `SecurityConfig.java`
- `jpaDependencyInjectsJpaConfig` — checks `application.yaml` + `JpaConfig.java`
- `actuatorDependencyInjectsObservabilityConfig` — checks `application.yaml` contains `management`
- `rqueueDependencyInjectsRqueueConfig` — checks `application.yaml` + `RqueueConfig.java`
- `multipleDependenciesInjectAllConfigs` — combines kafka + security + jpa + actuator; spot-checks all files

### Key Version Properties

Two Spring Boot versions, independent of each other:
- **The application's own** — `pom.xml` `<parent><version>` (3.5.16 since the tool upgrade). Initializr 0.23.0 is built against Boot 3.5.x, so keep the parent on the line `<spring-initializr.version>` was built for.
- **What generated projects are offered** — the Versions table (seeded from `application.yml` `initializr.boot-versions`, edited in admin), with build customizations narrowed by their `compatibilityRange`. Moving the application's own Boot does not move these.

Initializr framework version is controlled solely by `<spring-initializr.version>` in `pom.xml`.

### Artifactory URL

The URL `https://repo.menora.co.il/artifactory/libs-release` appears in three places that must be kept in sync:
1. `pom.xml` `<repositories>` — where this app resolves its own dependencies
2. `application.yml` `initializr.env.repositories` — exposed in metadata to clients (IntelliJ)
3. `DataSeeder.seedBuildCustomizations()` — what is written into generated `pom.xml` files (as a `BuildCustomizationEntity`)

**Round 11 (2026-11): two things the generator's own UI asks of the audit.** The UI round (see *Round 11* in `ui/CLAUDE.md`) needed two small backend changes, both in `observability/`:

- *Popular dependencies.* `GET /metadata/popular-deps?kind=BACKEND|FRONTEND` (`config/PopularDependenciesController`) → `[{depId, count}]`, at most ten, most used first (ties by id), over the last 90 days. `GenerationAuditService.popularDependencies` counts **successful `.zip` generations only** — a preview is someone looking, a failure nobody's choice — and only requests that name `dependencies` as a parameter (wizard and fullstack generations send a JSON body, so they do not contribute). The frontend generator's endpoints are under `frontend/`, which is how the two kinds are told apart. Open like the other `/metadata/*` lists: ids and counts, nothing about users or artifacts. The picker's "Quick picks" row reads it.
- *Live previews are not activity.* The Backend tab's file dock refetches `/starter.preview` by itself as dependencies are ticked, with the header `X-Preview-Intent: live`. `GenerationAuditFilter.isLivePreview` skips the audit for such a request — otherwise every tick would add a row and skew the counts. **Only a `.preview` endpoint can opt out**: a `.zip` generation is recorded whatever header it carries.

`/starter.preview`, unlike `/starter.zip`, does not fill in what a caller leaves out (it must not call `request.initialize`, see `ProjectPreviewController`): a request without `bootVersion`, `language`, `packaging`… is a 400. Name every parameter, as the UI does.

Pinned by `PopularDependenciesAndLivePreviewTests`.

**Access gate (`config/UserInfoGateFilter`).** `menora.access.require-user-header: true` answers every request that lacks the SSO gateway's user header (`menora.audit.user-header`, `userinfo` unless renamed — the property the audit reads; a blank value counts as missing) with 403 `{error, code: "USERINFO_REQUIRED", detail}`. Off by default, and then the filter is not registered at all (`@ConditionalOnProperty`); switch it on per environment (`MENORA_ACCESS_REQUIRE_USER_HEADER=true`). `menora.access.open-paths` (comma-separated Ant patterns, default `/actuator/**`) is what stays answered without the header — Kubernetes probes do not pass the gateway. Order 0: before `AdminAuthFilter` (so `/admin/login` is refused too) and before `GenerationAuditFilter` (a refused call is not a failed generation in the audit). Open paths are matched against the path the container resolved (`getServletPath` + `getPathInfo`), never `getRequestURI()` — `/actuator/../metadata/client` is served as `/metadata/client` and must be judged as that. The header proves nothing by itself: the gate is as strong as the rule that only the gateway can reach this server. The UI reads the `code` and shows a "No access" screen (`AccessBlocked`, see `ui/CLAUDE.md`). Pinned by `UserInfoGateTests`.

**Round 12 (2026-11): what a request can make the server do.** Five defects from an audit of the generators and the service; no feature. The only generated bytes that changed are the four files of *Container ports* below.

- *Paths stay inside the project (`GeneratedProjectFiles.resolveInside`).* A generation writes into a temp directory on the server, and the paths it writes are put together from text somebody typed: a wizard's sub-package, a request map's key, a catalog row's target path, a template's path pattern. `root.resolve("../../x")` names a file outside it. The OpenAPI wizard's kept copy of the spec was `openapi/<key>.yaml` with the key taken from `specByDep` as sent — `{"../../../../../x": …}` with `copySpec` wrote the pasted document elsewhere on the disk, unauthenticated (the request was answered 200; `aWizardKeyCannotNameAFileOutsideTheProject` failed against the old controller). `resolveInside(root, relative)` normalizes and answers 400 (`WizardArgumentException`) for an absolute path, one that climbs out, or the root itself. It is used by every write whose path is not a constant: the file and delete contributors and the four wizard contributors in `DynamicProjectGenerationConfiguration`, `FrontendProjectGenerator` (contributions, deletes, the generated TS client's files) and `FullstackRenderer`. **A new write whose path comes from a request, the catalog or a template goes through it, never through `Path.resolve`.**
- *Plain names (`GeneratedProjectFiles.requirePlainName`).* Letters, digits, `_ . -`, not starting with `.` or `-`, no `..`, at most 128. Required of the **artifact id** in `ProjectRequestFactory.create` (it names the zip's root folder, a module's directory and the download; the framework cleans it for the pom, but the wizard, fullstack and multi-module endpoints used it as sent), of the frontend `projectName` (`FrontendStarterController`), and of every **key** of the wizard maps and their option maps (`WizardStarterController.validateKeys`, first thing in `validate(body)` — the one method the zip, preview and patch paths call before generating). `GlobalExceptionHandler` now answers a `WizardArgumentException` as 400 `{error, detail, path?}` for the endpoints with no handler of their own (`/starter.zip`, the frontend endpoints, the fullstack imports); it used to fall through to the 500.
- *A pasted document refers only to itself (`openapi/SelfContainedDocument`).* swagger-parser resolves references, and fetches the ones that are URLs or files — from this server, for whoever posted the document. Measured against 2.1.22 with a local HTTP server: a `$ref` that is not a fragment or a bare component name is fetched wherever it is written (schema, property, path item, parameter, response, example, callback), by the one-argument `readContents` too; a discriminator `mapping` value of that kind is fetched (3.0); and in 3.1 **a schema's `$id` is fetched even when every reference is internal**, after which the internal references are looked for in the answer and reported missing. `SelfContainedDocument.check(text)` reads the document the way the parser does (its own `DeserializationUtils`, its own fallback mappers, so the two cannot disagree), refuses the first two naming the place (`components.schemas.A.$ref refers to '…', which is not part of the document given`), and drops a textual `$id` — the document is then handed on as JSON; otherwise as pasted, the same `String`. One it cannot read is parsed with `resolve=false`, and the parser words the error. All three parse sites go through it: `OpenApiCodeGenerator.parseOrThrow` (`OpenApiParseException`, so the drawer's detection shows it), the events wizard's payload document (`AsyncApiParseException`) and `OpenApiTsGenerator.parse`. **A new call to the parser goes through `check`.** `externalValue`, `operationRef`, `$dynamicRef`, `$schema` and `jsonSchemaDialect` were measured too and fetch nothing. The WSDL side was already closed (*Round 10, phase 3*).
- *Admin login.* The password is compared as SHA-256 digests with `MessageDigest.isEqual`. `admin/AdminLoginThrottle`: after `admin.login.max-failures` (10) wrong passwords from one address within `admin.login.window` (5m) that address is answered 429 with `Retry-After` until the window has passed — the right password included; a success clears the count; `0` switches it off. The address is `getRemoteAddr`, never a header the caller sets, so behind a proxy without `server.forward-headers-strategy` all admins share one count. Per JVM. The controller logs a warning at start while the password is `changeme`. The admin UI shows the 429's message. Pinned by `AdminLoginTest` — without a Spring context, because a locked-out `127.0.0.1` in a shared test context would lock every other test class out.
- *H2 console off.* `spring.h2.console.enabled: false` in the shipped `application.yml`: it is a SQL prompt on the catalog database (`sa`, no password) outside the admin filter. On a developer's machine: `--spring.h2.console.enabled=true`. `ShippedConfigurationTest` reads the shipped file, since the tests run on their own properties.
- *Document size (`config/WizardDocumentLimit`).* `menora.wizard.max-documents-size` (5MB): the SQL, OpenAPI, WSDL and AsyncAPI documents of one generation request **together**, and the one document of each detect endpoint and of the fullstack `import-ddl` / `import-select`. Past it, 400 naming the property. It bounds what is parsed, not the request body — the body is still read into memory first.
- *Container ports.* The generated frontend's `nginx/nginx.conf` listened on 80 while its Dockerfile exposes `PORT` (3000) and `k8s/values.yaml` points the service and both probes at 3000. It now has `listen ${PORT};`, and `entrypoint.sh` exports `PORT` (3000 unless set) and substitutes it. The fullstack README and `.env.production` said `API_UPSTREAM=http://backend:8080`; the backend image listens on its `PORT`, 3000, so they say 3000. All 61 fullstack manifests moved in exactly those four files. 3000 is the port Menora's Helm chart expects of the frontend container (confirmed 2026-11-15); the change itself has not been run in a cluster. These are catalog rows and template files: on an upgraded database the old `nginx.conf` and `entrypoint.sh` rows are reported by the catalog sync (below) and taken with `POST /admin/catalog-sync/apply`; the template rows follow on start.
- *Multi-module with a wizard.* Not a backend change: the UI sends a project with a wizard to the wizard endpoint, which writes one module, and now says so beside the ticked modules (see `ui/CLAUDE.md`). Placing wizard output in a module is not done.

*Not done:* a wizard's document is still parsed twice per request (once to validate, once in the contributor); an admin-typed `targetPath` is checked when a project is generated, not when the row is saved; the request body has no size limit of its own.

Pinned by `GeneratedProjectFilesTest`, `SelfContainedDocumentTest` (every case against a recording HTTP server: nothing is fetched), `AdminLoginTest`, `ShippedConfigurationTest` and `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests` (`aWizardKeyCannotNameAFileOutsideTheProject`, `anArtifactIdIsAPlainName`, `aSpecThatRefersOutOfItselfIsRefused`, `aRequestsDocumentsHaveASizeLimit`).

**Catalog sync (`db/CatalogSync`, 2026-11): file contributions and build customizations follow the release.** The fullstack template sets have followed the shipped files since Round 9; the dependency catalog's files (Dockerfiles, nginx, datasource configs, the Vault rows) and build rows (BOMs, added and excluded dependencies) were seeded once, so every fix to one reached new databases only — the "upgraded databases keep the old rows" lines above. They follow now. Dependency groups and entries, sub-options and compatibility rules were **not** part of it then — seeded once — and joined it in *Round 14, phase 7* (at the end of this file), which also made the baseline per table and stopped a row being added for a dependency that is not there.

- *What is remembered.* `initializer_catalog_seed` (`V28`, `CatalogSeedEntity`): one record per row a release ships — `seed_key` (sha256 of the row's identity), `applied_hash` (fingerprint of the shipped version last written to this database, null when it never was), `kept`. A table of its own rather than a `seed_hash` column on the two catalog tables, because **a deleted row has to be remembered too** — the template sync puts a deleted file back on the next start; this does not.
- *Identity and fingerprint.* A file row is the same row from one release to the next by kind, dependency, target path, sub-option, Java version and Node version; a build row by kind, dependency, type, group and artifact, repository id, sub-option and Boot range (`CatalogSync.keyOf`; each is unique over the shipped manifests, pinned). Everything else is the fingerprint: content (line endings normalized, like the template sync), file type, substitution type (null = `NONE`, what the admin form saves) and sort order; version, exclusions, repository name and URL, snapshots, scope and sort order. An admin who changes an identity field has made another row: the shipped one counts as deleted.
- *On start* (`CatalogSync.sync`, called by `DataSeeder` — after seeding a fresh database, where every row is recorded as in step, or on an existing one), per shipped row: the stored row still has the fingerprint last applied → rewritten to the release's (**updated**); it has another → left alone (**differs**); there is no stored row but one was applied here before → an admin deleted it, left alone; the release ships it to this database for the first time → **added**. A record whose row the release no longer ships: the row is removed when it still has the applied fingerprint, kept otherwise, and the record goes either way.
- *The baseline: the first start of an existing database.* Nothing is recorded, so a row that differs may be an admin's edit or an older release's content, and a missing one may have been deleted or never there. **That start changes nothing**: it records every shipped row — in step where the stored one matches, never-applied where it differs or is missing — and logs one warning with the two counts. This was a choice (the template sync overwrote such rows once): an admin's edits to a Dockerfile are not ours to lose.
- *For the admin* (`admin/CatalogSyncController`, behind the admin login; there is no screen yet): `GET /admin/catalog-sync` → `{enabled, summary, rows: [{key, table, kind, dependencyId, label, status, rowId}]}` with `status` one of `IN_STEP`, `DIFFERS`, `MISSING` (never applied and absent), `KEPT`, `DELETED` — `?status=DIFFERS,MISSING` narrows the rows; `GET /admin/catalog-sync/{key}` → `{row, shipped, current}`, the two entities side by side for a diff; `POST /admin/catalog-sync/apply` `{keys: […]}` takes the shipped version of those rows (rewritten in place, a missing one added) and `{all: true}` of every row that differs or is missing — never one an admin kept or deleted, those are named to be taken; `POST /admin/catalog-sync/keep` `{keys: […], kept?: false}` marks rows as the admin's own (no longer reported, never replaced), or undoes it.
- *Switch.* `initializr.catalog.sync=off` leaves the catalog alone (a fresh database is still seeded).
- *The admin import* (`/admin/import`) replaces the catalog's rows and leaves these records: an imported row that equals what was last applied keeps following, any other is reported as differing.
- *Tried on a copy of a real database* (seeded several rounds ago): 311 rows in step, 10 differing, none missing — the five `*-config-primary` datasource templates (Round 10, phase 1), the three frontend Dockerfiles (Round 9) and Round 12's `nginx.conf` and `entrypoint.sh`. `apply {all: true}` wrote the ten.

*Limits.* A row saved from the admin form without a change counts as edited if the form alters anything beyond line endings and a missing substitution type. Two instances starting at once on one database both run the sync; there is no lock. Several stored rows with one identity (an admin's copy): the one equal to the shipped row is taken for it, else the first.

Pinned by `CatalogSyncTest` (each rule; "the release moved on" is staged by giving a row other content and recording that content as applied) and `CatalogSyncAdminTests` (the endpoints).

**Fullstack migrations (`fullstack/schema/`, 2026-11): with Flyway, the entity model is the first migration.** A fullstack project on PostgreSQL, Oracle, SQL Server or DB2 started with **no tables**: those databases' datasource configurations set `hbm2ddl-auto: none`, and the `flyway` dependency brought a placeholder `V1__init.sql` that created none of the entities' tables. Only H2 (`ddl-auto: create-drop`) made a schema. Now a fullstack request whose resolved dependencies include **`flyway`** gets its schema as `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__init.sql`, and an update of such a project gets what the model changed as a migration of its own. A request without `flyway` keeps its bytes — no golden manifest moved. Liquibase is not served; the plain SQL wizard's baseline (*Round 10, phase 3*) is unrelated and unchanged.

- *The schema (`Schema`, `SchemaBuilder`).* A database-neutral model — tables, columns (`ColumnType`: `VARCHAR`, `TEXT`, `BIGINT`, `INTEGER`, `BOOLEAN`, `DATE`, `TIMESTAMP`, `TIME`, `INSTANT`, `DECIMAL`, `MONEY`, `UUID`, `BLOB`), keys, unique constraints, foreign keys, indexes — built from the entity definitions **the way `Entity.java.mustache` is rendered from them**: names through `SchemaNames.tableOf` / `schemaOf` / `columnOf` / `joinColumnOf` and the join-table helpers `joinTableOf` / `ownerJoinColumnOf` / `inverseJoinColumnOf` (now public; the last three were inline in the view-model and are shared with it), and the audit columns, `version` and `deleted` from the per-entity context's `auditApplicable` / `lockingApplicable` / `softDeleteApplicable` — the flags the template itself is gated on. `SchemaBuilder.typeOf(FieldType)` is an exhaustive switch. A view (`@Subselect`) has no table; a MANY_TO_MANY has its join table (both columns the key); a FILE or IMAGE field is a plain UUID column (the entity maps no association, so no foreign key); `app_attachment`, `app_saved_view` and `app_workflow_move` are added on the project flags (`hasAttachments`, `hasSavedViews`, `hasWorkflowMoves`) and written out by hand to match their entity templates. **A change to how an entity is mapped belongs in `SchemaBuilder` too** — the smoke build below is what fails when they drift. Beyond what Hibernate would create: an index on every foreign key (none of the four server databases makes one) and on a workflow's status and assignee columns. Enum columns get no CHECK constraint (a new enum value would need a migration to change it). Constraint names are `pk_<table>`, `uk_`/`fk_`/`ix_<table>_<column>`, lower case, cut to 60 characters with a hash. Exact-case names (`"x"`, `` `x` ``, `[x]`) are written in double quotes, which all five databases read.
- *Per database (`SchemaDialect`: `H2`, `POSTGRESQL`, `ORACLE`, `MSSQL`, `DB2`).* The migration is written for the first of PostgreSQL, Oracle, SQL Server, DB2 among the project's dependencies, else H2 (`SchemaDialect.of`). The type names are **the ones Hibernate's own dialect writes** for the templates' mappings, and `SchemaDialectTest` holds the table to that without a database: it has Hibernate export `dialectfixture.DialectFixture` (one column per shape the templates emit) for each dialect — H2 2.2, PostgreSQL 14, Oracle 19, SQL Server 2019, DB2 11.5 — and compares every column. Two deliberate differences, both in the test: H2's long text is `text` (H2's name for its CLOB, and what the entity's `columnDefinition = "TEXT"` says), and PostgreSQL's identity is `bigint generated by default as identity` where this Hibernate still writes `bigserial`. Long text is `text` / `clob` / `varchar(max)`. A unique constraint over a nullable column is a plain constraint on H2, PostgreSQL and Oracle, and a unique index that leaves nulls out on DB2 (`exclude null keys`) and SQL Server (`where c is not null`), as Hibernate's dialects do. `create schema if not exists` is written for H2 and PostgreSQL only; elsewhere a comment says the schema must exist. **The fixture lives outside `com.menora.initializr`**: the application scans that package for entities, and one more `@Entity` there fails the catalog database's `ddl-auto: validate` in every Spring test.
- *The baseline (`SchemaSql.baseline`, `SchemaMigrations.writeBaseline`).* Tables first, each with its key and inline unique constraints; then unique indexes, foreign keys and indexes — so tables that refer to each other, or to themselves, need no order. Written by `FullstackProjectGenerationConfiguration` after the render (the contributor is `@Order(50)`, after the catalog's file contributor, so it replaces the dependency's placeholder), from Java, not a template row. The same call turns `ddl-auto` / `hbm2ddl-auto: create-drop` (the H2 configurations) into **`validate`** in `application.yaml`: with a migration there, Hibernate creating the tables as well would hide a migration that does not match the entities until the first real database. The server databases' `hbm2ddl-auto: none` is left alone.
- *Updating (`SchemaDiff`, `SchemaMigrations.carryBaselineOver`).* `POST /starter-fullstack.patch` generates both sides; the contributor leaves the schema it wrote in `EntityDefinitionContext.generatedSchema()` and `generateSide` takes it out before the contexts are cleared. When both sides have Flyway and the same database: the target tree's `V1__init.sql` is replaced by the base's (**it has run; it never changes**), and the difference between the two schemas is added as `db/migration/V<yyyyMMddHHmmss>__model_update.sql` — a timestamp, because the project may have hand-numbered migrations the generator cannot see (a hand-written one added later needs a timestamp too, or `outOfOrder`). No schema change, no file. One side without Flyway, or two different databases: nothing special — the baseline that appears, goes or changes is the update.
  - **Live** (runs as written): a new table with its constraints and indexes; a new column that may be null or has a default (`deleted`); a foreign key, an index and a unique constraint on columns this migration adds; `NOT NULL` dropped; a constraint or an index the model no longer has.
  - **To review** (commented out, each under a wrapped `--` note saying what changed): a dropped column — with the foreign key, unique and index on it first, which SQL Server needs — or table; a changed type or length; a column that became required; a unique constraint or a foreign key over values that are already there; a changed key (a note only). **A required new column is added without `NOT NULL`** (live) and the `NOT NULL` is the review item: the table may have rows. **A rename is a removal and an addition** — the model cannot say otherwise — and the note on the removal says to rename instead.
  - The `ALTER` spellings are per database (`SchemaDialect.addColumn`, `setNotNull`, `dropNotNull`, `alterType`, `dropIndex`). `UPDATE.md` gains a "The database" section when the update carries such a migration (`ProjectUpdateBuilder.notes`).
- *Built for real* (`GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests`, `-Dsmoke.be=true`): `flywayBaselineIsTheSchemaTheEntitiesExpect` builds seven backends with `flyway` — Orders with every option and Approvals with the move log (both with their live API tests), the field-types, many-to-many and one-to-many models, Course enrolments (composite keys) and Project planning — and each starts on the schema the migration made with Hibernate validating; a test added to each project reads `flyway_schema_history`. `anUpdatesMigrationTakesTheOldSchemaToTheNewEntities` writes the old model's baseline and the update's migration, as generated, into the new model's backend and builds it.

*Limits.* **Only H2 has been run with Hibernate validating.** The PostgreSQL, Oracle, SQL Server and DB2 output is held to Hibernate's own type names and reviewed by hand. Since 2026-11-19 the baseline's CREATE statements for every example have run on PostgreSQL, SQL Server and Oracle in Docker (`CreateTablesDockerTests`, see *Fullstack DDL and creating tables*), but no generated backend has started on them, and the update migrations' ALTER statements and DB2 have not run at all. Open points there: `lower()`/`LIKE` search over a `clob` (Oracle, DB2); `varchar2(4000 char)` for the saved views' state on an Oracle without extended strings; DB2 needs `REORG TABLE` after some of the review statements (the notes say so). A table or column named like one of a database's keywords (`order`, `comment`, `level`…) is written unquoted, as the entity maps it, and fails on that database in the migration as it would in Hibernate's own queries — the model has to quote it or name it otherwise. The update's migration is the difference between two models, not a look at the database: a schema changed by hand, or made by Hibernate rather than by the baseline (other constraint names), is not what it assumes. Both sides are generated by today's generator, so a change in how the generator maps something shows up in the next update's migration. An existing project that adds `flyway` gets the whole schema as `V1`, which the dependency's `baseline-on-migrate` marks as applied on a database that already has tables.

Pinned by `SchemaDialectTest`, `SchemaDiffTest` and `FullstackMigrationTests`.

**Enforced access (2026-11): with ldap-auth, every generated endpoint is checked.** Every generated CRUD endpoint was open: the `secured` option wrote `@RequiresPermission` lines **commented out**, and a page's `roles` only hid it in the frontend. Now a fullstack project whose resolved dependencies include `ldap-auth` or `ldap-auth-rest` — a default of both backend sets, so **every default project changed** (all 61 fullstack golden manifests, in the controllers, the security classes, `MeController`, `CurrentUser`, the attachment and saved-view controllers, the README and a new `application-local.yaml`) — carries a live check on every endpoint, at levels each entity sets. Without an ldap-auth dependency nothing is checked and nothing changed. The wizard manifests did not move.

- *The model.* `EntityDefinitionDto.access` — `{read, write}`, each `SIGNED_IN` (any caller the gateway identified), `USER` or `ADMIN`, matched ignoring case; a missing one is its default, **read `USER`, write `ADMIN`** (`EntityDefinition.Access`, `LEVELS` weakest first, `DEFAULT`). `FullstackRequestValidator.parseAccess`: an unknown level is a 400 (`…access.read`), and so is **write open to more people than read** — whoever may change a row has to be able to see it. Levels other than the default without an ldap-auth dependency are a 400 naming the entity (`FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts`), like page roles: the setting would otherwise be accepted and do nothing. The schema document gained `accessLevels` and `defaultAccess`.
- *The controller.* `Controller.java.mustache` (both backend sets share it): under `hasLdapAuth`, `@RequiresPermission(Constants.{{readLevel}})` on the list, a row, `/stats`, the exports, the move log and — because which move a user may make is the transition's own roles, checked by the service — the workflow's transition, claim and release; `{{writeLevel}}` on create, import, update, delete, restore and the bulk calls; `/assign` stays `ADMIN`. `AttachmentController` and `SavedViewController` ask `SIGNED_IN`. `/api/me` and `/api/me/roles` are open, as they answer who the caller is. `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` counts: no mapping without a check.
- *The `secured` option is retired.* `ScaffoldOption.SECURED` stays so a model that names it validates; no template reads `optScaffoldSecured`. The editor no longer offers it.
- *One place for roles (`security/PermissionService`, a catalog template of both ldap-auth dependencies — so plain and wizard projects with ldap-auth get it too).* `rolesOf(user)` is what the aspect, `CurrentUser` and `MeController` all ask, so they cannot disagree: **`ADMIN` includes `USER`** (the check used to be literal: a member of the admin group alone failed a `USER` check); a user's roles are remembered for a minute (every guarded call used to ask the directory); and **development roles** — `app.security.dev-roles.<user>: ADMIN` gives a user roles without the directory. `holds(user, required)` adds `Constants.SIGNED_IN`. `PermissionAspect` uses it: no `userinfo` header is a 401, a missing role a 403.
- *The local profile.* `src/main/resources/application-local.yaml` (a new catalog row of both dependencies) holds `app.security.dev-roles: {dev-user: ADMIN}` — `dev-user` is what the generated frontend's dev server signs its calls as. The fullstack README's run command is `mvn spring-boot:run -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=local` with ldap-auth, and it has a "Who may do what" section. **The setting is in that profile only, never in `application.yaml`**: it hands out roles without asking anyone.
- *Existing databases.* The changed security templates and the new `application-local.yaml` rows are catalog rows: the catalog sync brings them to a database whose rows were untouched, and reports them (differing, missing) on a database's first start after that release — see *Catalog sync*.
- *Built for real* (`GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.eachEntitysAccessLevelsAreEnforcedOverTheRealApi`, `-Dsmoke.be=true`): a backend with `ldap-auth-rest` — the first generated backend with it to be built; its only extra artifact is the AOP starter — and three entities at different levels, driven by tests added to the project as nobody (401 everywhere), a caller with no role, a user and an admin. The generated `@WebMvcTest` slices do not load the aspect and pass unchanged.

- *The frontend follows.* With ldap-auth (the frontend context's `hasLdapAuth`) the generated app offers what the caller may do; the backend still checks every call. `src/shared/api/roles.ts` (both sets, gated `hasLdapAuth`): `useCallerMay(level)` — the caller's roles from `/api/me/roles`, asked once a session, false until known so an action appears rather than disappears. **List pages** (`EntityPage`, both sets): the per-entity `writeChecked` (= ldap ∧ `mutable` ∧ write level not `SIGNED_IN`) puts `const mayWrite = useCallerMay('<level>')` in the page and joins it to the helper Round 7's `hideActions` introduced — `can(action)` is `!hideActions?.includes(action)`, `action === 'export' || mayWrite`, or both — so New, Edit, Delete, Import, the bulk bar, inline cells, the empty state's first record and a board's drag all go for a caller who may not write, in every view. `listActionsConfigurable` now means "the page has `can()`" (= `listHidesActions` ∨ `writeChecked`); the `hideActions` prop itself is `listHidesActions`. **Record pages**: `recordWriteChecked` / `recordWriteLevel` wrap Edit and Delete in `mayWrite` (Print and the workflow moves stay). **The nav and the routes**: a page's roles in `access.ts` are `effectiveRoles` — its own, and what its entity's level asks: the write level for a form, wizard or import page, the read level for any other page of one entity, nothing for a page over several (a dashboard, tabs, search). The shell admits a holder of any listed role and an admin is a user too, so the result is one role, the stronger of the page's and the entity's. So with ldap-auth a layout's entity pages ask `USER` by default and `access.ts` ships; without the dependency nothing changed. Built for real: four of the `fullstackFrontend*` smoke builds (classic, the Orders and Tickets layouts, the form pages), with the generated lint.

*Not done.* In the frontend, not following yet: the parent's New and Edit on a master-detail page, a calendar day's create, a board **page**'s drag and a list embedded as a tab or related list beyond what its `EntityPage` already hides — each ends in the API client's 403 sentence. The check is per entity, not per row or per field; there are still two roles; the levels are fixed at generation, not configurable at run time. A page's `roles` and an entity's `access` are separate settings — a page limited to admins over an entity users may read is hidden, not protected.

Pinned by `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` (`…enforcesEachEntitysAccessWithLdapAuth`, `…rejectsInvalidAccessLevels`, `…checksNothingWithoutLdapAuth`), `FullstackWorkflowTests`, `DataSeederTest` and the re-pinned golden manifests and schema.

**Generated tests (2026-11): a fullstack project's own tests, for real.** The `tests` option used to write one `@WebMvcTest` per entity with the service mocked — `GET` the list, expect 200 — so no SQL, no check an option adds, no access level was ever run by a project's own build. It now means tests that call the real application, and the editor turns it on for a new model (a request that leaves it out still gets none, so callers and saved models keep their output). Everything is computed in one class, `fullstack/GeneratedTests` (`putBackend`/`putFrontend` on the project contexts, `putEntity` from `EntityScaffoldContext.buildEntityContext`), from the entity definitions alone.

- *Backend.* `src/test/java/<pkg>/ApiTestSupport.java` (`hasApiTests`): an abstract `@SpringBootTest @AutoConfigureMockMvc @ActiveProfiles("test")` base — the context starts once for every test class — with `send`/`read`, and `body(entity)`: the JSON of a valid new row of any entity. A fresh row number per call (`nextRow`, from 1000) keeps unique columns apart; values respect `length`, `min`/`max` (`bounded`, `decimal`), enums (`pick`), e-mail; a key travels in a URL so a STRING key has no space (`key`); a required MANY_TO_ONE creates its parent first (`row.set("customerId", create("Customer").get("id"))`); a required FILE/IMAGE uploads a file first (`attachment`). The workflow's status and assignee are never sent. Per entity, `<controller pkg>/<Entity>ApiTest.java` (`apiTestApplicable` = the option as the entity resolves it, not on a SELECT view): the list answers a page, an unknown sort is a 400, a missing row a 404; for a writable entity create → read → PUT one text field → delete → 404 (→ restore, under soft delete), the search finds the row by its text, a required value left out is a 400 naming it (with the `validation` starter), the workflow's first move out of the initial state that asks for nothing — after a claim when there is an assignee — and a move that does not start there is a 409; with ldap-auth, nobody signed in is a 401 and each entity's read and write levels are checked with three callers.
- *A row the tests cannot make is said so.* A required field with a `pattern` and no default (nothing can invent a match), or a required link to an entity that cannot be made (read-only, itself, a cycle, or one with that problem — `reasonOf`): the entity's writing tests are generated `@Disabled("<why>")`, its case in `body` throws the same sentence, and the reading tests still run.
