- *The `test` profile* (`src/test/resources/application-test.yaml`): `spring.datasource` **and** every configured datasource's prefix (`postgresql`/`oracle`/`mssql`/`db2`, and `h2` for its own config class) point at `jdbc:h2:mem:api-test` with the H2 dialect; schema-qualified tables get `INIT=CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS …`; demo data is off; with ldap-auth `app.security.dev-roles` gives `api-test-admin` ADMIN, `api-test-user` USER and `api-test-nobody` nothing, so LDAP is never asked. Hibernate creates the schema — except on an H2 project with Flyway, where the baseline is H2 SQL: Flyway runs it and Hibernate validates. A project without the `h2` dependency gets H2 in test scope (`FullstackProjectGenerationConfiguration.testDatabaseCustomizer`), and the framework's own `*ApplicationTests` gets `@ActiveProfiles("test")` (`testProfileContributor`, late) — **before this, `mvn package` of a generated backend on any database but H2 failed at the context-load test** on a machine without that database.
- *Frontend.* `package.json` gains `"test": "vitest run"`, `"test:watch"` and Vitest 2, jsdom, `@testing-library/react` and `@testing-library/dom` (the versions the Frontend tab's catalog uses) — both sets' `package.json.mustache`, gated `hasFrontendTests`; `vitest.config.ts` merges the app's `vite.config.ts` (its aliases) with a jsdom environment. Per entity, `features/<e>-form/model/validate.test.ts` (`feTestApplicable`): a complete row passes `validate<Entity>` (left out when a required pattern has no default) and one case per rule it checks — each required field and relation, each `length`, `min`, `max` and e-mail; `pages/<e>/ui/<E>Page.test.tsx` (`feRenderTestApplicable`: the page opens on a table or cards and the entity has a plain text field to look for): the page renders over a stubbed `fetch` (the list holds one row, the caller is an admin) and shows the row. `shared/ui/csv.test.ts` with the CSV import (`feTestsCsv`), `app/route.test.ts` with a page layout (`feTestsRoute`). The tests import from `vitest` (no globals) and use no jest-dom, so `tsc -b` and the generated lint pass them as they are.
- The root README has a *Tests* section. The editor labels the option *Tests*.

*Existing databases.* The template rows follow on start (*Template sync*): the old `EntityControllerTest` row is removed and the new rows added, unless an admin edited them.

*Not done.* `mvn test` runs on H2 only; a dialect's own SQL and the server databases' baselines are run by the `db-tests` profile (*Local run*, below), not by the default build. No Playwright test, no wizard or plain-backend tests (the SQL wizard's `generateTests` is still a mocked slice). The render test checks that a row shows, not the form or the actions; a board, calendar or gallery opening view gets none. MANY_TO_MANY links, compositions' rows, bulk calls, exports, imports and saved views are not exercised by the generated tests (the smoke builds' `LiveApiTests` still are).

Pinned by `FullstackGeneratedTestsTests`; every `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests` build with `tests` runs them (`aBackendOnAnotherDatabasePassesItsOwnTestsOnH2` on a PostgreSQL + Flyway backend), and `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*TestsPass` runs `pnpm run test`.

**Local run (2026-11): `compose.yaml`, the `compose` profile and Testcontainers.** Nothing a generated project carried started a database, so running one on a laptop meant finding a server; and the fullstack Flyway baselines for PostgreSQL, Oracle and SQL Server had never been executed. Everything is in `localrun/LocalRun` (texts under `templates/localrun/`, written from Java like the wizards' output — what they hold follows the whole set of dependencies, which no catalog row sees) and `extension/localrun/LocalRunGenerationConfiguration` (registered in `spring.factories`).

- *Services* (`LocalRun.SERVICES`): `postgresql` → `postgres:16-alpine`, `oracle` → `gvenzl/oracle-free:23-slim-faststart` (an `appuser` in `FREEPDB1`), `mssql` → `mssql/server:2022-latest` (its health check creates `appdb`, so healthy means the URL works), `mongodb` → `mongo:7`, `redis` → `redis:7-alpine`, `kafka` → `apache/kafka:3.8.0` (one KRaft broker, PLAINTEXT, `localhost:9092` from the machine and `kafka:29092` from a container). Each has a health check and fixed host ports. **DB2 is IBM i through jt400 — there is no image**: a project with it gets a comment saying so and nothing else; `rqueue` (Redis Sentinel over TLS) and `h2` get nothing.
- *Images* (`localrun/ContainerImages`): Docker Hub images are pulled through `menora.containers.hub-prefix` (default `repo.menora.co.il/dockerhub/`, the proxy the frontend's Dockerfile already pulls nginx through; empty for Docker Hub itself), SQL Server's from `menora.containers.mcr-prefix` (default `mcr.microsoft.com/` — whether Menora proxies Microsoft's registry was not known).
- *Plain, wizard and multi-module projects* get `compose.yaml` at their root with the services alone, when a dependency has one (an H2-only project gets nothing; `/starter.zip` and `/starter-wizard.zip` stay identical since both run the contributor), and `src/main/resources/application-compose.yaml` — the **`compose` profile**, run with `mvn spring-boot:run -Dspring-boot.run.profiles=compose` (`compose,local` with an ldap-auth variant). The profile holds only what the catalog's defaults do not already give: Oracle's service URL and user, SQL Server's password and `trustServerCertificate`, `spring.kafka.security.protocol: PLAINTEXT`, and `<db>.hibernate.hbm2ddl-auto: update` when neither Flyway nor Liquibase is on the build (an empty database otherwise has no tables). Each value still gives way to its environment variable. HELP.md gets *Running it locally*. A multi-module project writes one `compose.yaml` over every module's dependencies (`MultiModuleController.assemble`) and the profile in the entry module only.
- *Fullstack projects* always get a root `compose.yaml` (`FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts`) with the services plus `backend` (built from `./backend`, the image's own command with `--spring.profiles.active=compose[,local]`, the URLs as environment variables naming the services, `8080:3000`, `depends_on` healthy) and `frontend` (built from `./frontend`, `API_UPSTREAM=http://backend:3000`, `3000:3000`); the backend gets the profile. The README gets *Run it all in Docker*. With ldap-auth the frontend container is given `DEV_USERINFO_ENABLED=on` / `DEV_USERINFO_DEFAULT=dev-user`, and the **frontend's `nginx.conf` (a catalog row) now reads them**: a `map` passes the caller's `userinfo` header through and, only when the flag is `on` and the call has none, sends `dev-user` — the entrypoint already substituted both variables (default off) and nothing used them.
- *The Kafka client* (`kafka-config.mustache`, a catalog row) hard-coded `SASL_SSL`; it reads `spring.kafka.security.protocol` now (default `SASL_SSL`, so a project's behaviour on the clusters is unchanged) and sets the SASL settings only for a SASL protocol.
- *Flyway's database modules* (`flywayDatabaseModulesCustomizer`): with `flyway` and the database on the build, `flyway-sqlserver` always (separate since Flyway 8), `flyway-database-postgresql` / `flyway-database-oracle` from Boot 3.3 (Flyway 10). Without them Flyway stops the application with "Unsupported Database" — a defect in every Boot 3.5 project with Flyway on those databases until now, hidden because only H2 had run.
- *The `testcontainers` dependency* (catalog, *Data* group, `org.testcontainers:junit-jupiter`, test scope; `V29` adds it to an existing database's Data group, since dependency entries are seeded once): the module of each database on the build (`postgresql`, `oracle-free`, `mssqlserver`), `src/test/java/<pkg>/ContainerDatabaseTests.java` — the application started with every PostgreSQL/Oracle/SQL Server datasource pointed at a container (`@DynamicPropertySource`), `@Testcontainers(disabledWithoutDocker = true)` so the image build, which runs the tests, skips it — and `src/test/resources/testcontainers.properties` with `hub.image.name.prefix` (not written when the prefix is empty). Plain projects and fullstack backends; not a module.
- *The fullstack `db-tests` Maven profile* (`FullstackProjectGenerationConfiguration.dbTestsProfileCustomizer`, with the `tests` option and PostgreSQL, Oracle or SQL Server — the migration's database, `LocalRun.dbTestsDatabase`): Testcontainers' `jdbc` and the database's module, and surefire `systemPropertyVariables` that point that datasource at Testcontainers' JDBC URL (`jdbc:tc:postgresql:16-alpine:///appdb`, `jdbc:tc:oracle:23-slim-faststart:///appdb`, `jdbc:tc:sqlserver:2022-latest:///appdb` — the driver starts the container on first use), its real dialect, and with Flyway `spring.flyway.enabled=true` and `hbm2ddl-auto=validate`, so **`mvn test -Pdb-tests` runs the generated API tests on the real database, on the schema the baseline makes**. Without Flyway Hibernate creates the schema as on H2. Other datasources stay on H2. SQL Server's licence is accepted in `src/test/resources/container-license-acceptance.txt`; its JDBC URL names `mcr.microsoft.com` whatever the prefix (Testcontainers' URL cannot carry a registry). The README's *Tests* section names the command.

*Existing databases.* `V29` adds the `testcontainers` entry. The `nginx.conf` and `kafka-config` rows follow on start through *Catalog sync* where nobody edited them (on a database's first start after the catalog-sync release they are reported, and `POST /admin/catalog-sync/apply` takes them).

*Not done / limits.* **Nothing of this has been run against Docker on the development machine** (Docker was not available): `LocalRunDockerSmokeTests` (`-Dsmoke.docker=true`) runs the API tests through `db-tests` on PostgreSQL (Boot 3.2 and 3.5, with and without Flyway), Oracle and SQL Server, starts a backend on its compose services (PostgreSQL with Kafka, Oracle, SQL Server) and checks the nginx header handling against an echo server — a first run on 2026-11-16 passed its three PostgreSQL cases (24 API tests each, the Flyway baseline included) and was then stopped for low memory during the Oracle case. Since then the SQL Server case passed (2026-11-19) and the Oracle case ran, failed on Oracle 23 and was fixed (2026-11-20, *After phase 11: the parked runs* — which also has a Boot 3.5 fault the run found and that is still open); the compose starts and the nginx check are still unexecuted. Run the cases one at a time (`-Dtest=LocalRunDockerSmokeTests#…`) on this machine. The compose files themselves are checked with `docker compose config`. The backend and frontend images are built from the projects' Dockerfiles, which pull from `repo.menora.co.il` — outside the Menora network `docker compose up --build` cannot build them. The plain project's `*ApplicationTests` still needs the real database (only the fullstack one moved to the test profile). No Kafka, Redis or MongoDB container in the tests; no compose `volumes` (data lives as long as the containers); fixed host ports clash with a database already running on the machine. Schema-qualified entities without Flyway on a server database: Hibernate does not create the schema.

Pinned by `LocalRunTest` (the texts, read as YAML) and `LocalRunGenerationTests` (where they land, through the endpoints); `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.theDbTestsProfileResolvesAndCompiles` and `theTestcontainersSampleTestCompilesAndWaitsForDocker` (`-Dsmoke.be=true`) build them without Docker.

**OpenAPI wizard: interfaces, forms and security (2026-11).** The wizard's controllers were concrete classes with `TODO` bodies, so an update of the spec (`POST /starter.patch`) patched the very files a team had filled in; a `multipart/form-data` body became a JSON `@RequestBody` record (a 415 at runtime); and the document's security schemes were ignored. All in `openapi/OpenApiCodeGenerator`, with `FormField`, `SecurityUse` and `OperationModel.bodyKind/formFields/security`.

- *`apiStyle`* (`openApiOptions.<dep>.apiStyle`: `classes` | `interfaces`; `OpenApiWizardOptions.ApiStyle`). **Absent is classes — every request and saved model that names no style generates the bytes it did** (no golden manifest moved); the Backend tab's OpenAPI drawer starts a *new* setup on interfaces. With `interfaces`, each tag is `<api>/<Tag>Api.java` (`templates/wizard/openapi/Api.java.mustache`): every mapping, binding, constraint, `@ResponseStatus` and security annotation, each method a `default` that throws `ResponseStatusException(NOT_IMPLEMENTED)` — and `<api>/<Tag>Controller.java` (`ApiController.java.mustache`), `@RestController @Validated … implements <Tag>Api {}`, which **names nothing of the document but the tag**: a changed spec rewrites the interface and leaves the class alone, so an update never patches the team's code, and an operation the spec adds answers 501 until overridden. Spring MVC maps an interface's annotated (default) methods and an override's parameters through the interface. An override declares the signature only — Hibernate Validator refuses an override that adds parameter constraints (the class Javadoc says so). A removed tag's class is deleted by the update like any generated file.
- *Form bodies.* With no `application/json` content, a `multipart/form-data` or `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body is read as its properties (`SchemaSet.propertiesOf`, references and `allOf` followed), each a parameter of its own — the openapi-generator way, and what binds a wire name that is not a Java name: a `format: binary` property is `@RequestPart MultipartFile` (an array of them `List<MultipartFile>`), an object in a multipart body a JSON `@RequestPart` of its record, anything else `@RequestParam` with its constraints and date format; the mapping gets `consumes`. No record is made of the body. A form-urlencoded object property travels as `String`. The client takes a `Resource` for a file and builds a `MultiValueMap` — files and JSON parts as they are, plain fields as their text (a date would otherwise go as a JSON part) — with the content type set (RestTemplate headers, RestClient `.contentType`). A property is required when the schema requires it and the body is not `required: false`.
- *Security* (`components.securitySchemes`; an operation's `security`, else the document's; `[]` or only `{}` alternatives = open). Server: the wizard adds springdoc with controllers, so the schemes are declared in `<api>/OpenApiSecuritySchemes.java` (`@SecuritySchemes`, every scheme of the document; OAuth2 without its flows) and each operation carries `@SecurityRequirement` per scheme it may be called with. Without springdoc (only through the generator's API) the method Javadoc says which schemes and that the gateway or the application enforces them. **Nothing is enforced by the generated code.** Client: each operation sends its **first** requirement's schemes from `<client>/OpenApiCredentials.java` — an API key in a header, a query parameter (RestClient `queryParamIfPresent`) or a cookie, `setBearerAuth` for HTTP bearer, OAuth2 and OpenID Connect (a token got elsewhere), `setBasicAuth` — each read from `<base-url property's parent>.security.<scheme-kebab>.api-key|token|username|password` (`${…:}`, empty by default and in the `application.yaml` stub; an environment variable or Vault gives the value) and **not sent while empty**. Digest and mutual TLS are documented, not sent.
- *Names.* `form`, `credentials` joined the reserved parameter names; `MultipartFile`, `Resource`, `MediaType`, `RequestPart`, `ResponseStatusException`, the springdoc security types and `OpenApiCredentials` the reserved type names (a schema called one gets `Dto`).
- *The editor.* The drawer's *Server code as* (Interfaces | Classes) shows with a server side; the entry keeps `apiStyle: 'interfaces'` only, the request sends it only with controllers, the model import reads the server's `INTERFACES`.

*Limits.* (Until Round 13, phase 9: OAuth2 flows were not declared for Swagger UI, the client did not fetch a token, and only the first security requirement was sent.) A form property that is an object (urlencoded) or a free-form map (multipart) is text. Header/cookie API keys and bearer tokens set by a parameter of the same name would be sent twice. The update of a project whose spec drops a tag deletes that tag's class.

Pinned by `OpenApiInterfacesTest` (fixture `wizard-fixtures/secure-forms.yaml`: a multipart upload with files, a JSON part and a date, a form, bearer, header and query API keys, basic and OAuth2, an operation open to anyone), three new golden cases (`openapi__both__interfaces`, `openapi__secure-forms__interfaces`, `openapi__secure-forms__classes`); built by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.openApiWizardInterfacesFormsAndSecurityWork` on Boot 3.2 and 3.5 (the team's class replaced by one implementing two operations: they answer, the rest 501; the multipart and form bodies bind; the RestClient client sends the forms and each requirement's credentials to a mock server; `/v3/api-docs` lists the schemes) and `openApiWizardSecureFormsAsClassesCompile`.

**Generation audit (2026-11): every endpoint, its kind, why it failed, kept a year.** `GenerationAuditFilter` read a request's parameters only, so the wizard, fullstack and patch endpoints (JSON bodies) were recorded without artifact, versions or dependencies — and the picker's *Popular* row never counted them; a failure was "HTTP 400"; the row was saved inside the request and never deleted; the detection calls (`/starter-wizard.detect-*`) were recorded as if generations.

- *What a row holds now* (`V30`: `kind`, `action`, `department`, `wizards`, `entity_count`, an index on `status`; existing rows backfilled from their endpoint). Only endpoints under `/starter…` or `/frontend/starter…` ending `.zip` (`action` **download**), `.patch` (**patch**) or `.preview` (**preview**) are recorded (`isGeneration`); a live preview still is not. `kind` is backend / wizard / multimodule / fullstack / frontend, from the endpoint — the wizard endpoint without a document is a backend, `starter.patch` is its model's kind.
- *JSON requests* (`observability/AuditDetails`, a request attribute): the controllers say what they resolved — `WizardStarterController.toWebRequest` the request and the wizards given a document (`sql`, `openapi`, `soap`, `events`), `FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts` the backend request and the entity count, `MultiModuleController.assemble` the root request with every module's dependencies and the kind, `ProjectOptionsContext.setDepartment` the department. The request object is held and read when the response is done, so dependencies a controller adds later count. A patch generates both sides; the target, second, wins. A request refused before it was resolved (a validation 400) keeps only what its URL had.
- *Why it failed*: the filter keeps a copy of the first 4 KB of the response (written through, so a ZIP is not held) and reads an error's `detail`, `messages` (with `side`) or `message`/`error` — `HTTP 400 — Unknown SQL wizard apiMode: 'NOPE'…` — else its text, else the status (`errorMessageOf`).
- *Writing* (`GenerationAuditService`): one background writer with a queue of 1000 (`menora.audit.async`, default true); a full queue drops the event and logs every hundredth. The admin reads wait up to 2 s for pending writes (`awaitIdle`), so a generation shows the moment the Activity tab is reloaded. *Retention*: a nightly `@Scheduled` job (`menora.audit.retention-cron`, 03:30) deletes events older than `menora.audit.retention-days` (365; 0 keeps all) — `AuditScheduling` turns scheduling on, the application's only scheduled job.
- *Reading* (`admin/ActivityController`, behind the admin login). `GET /admin/activity/summary?days=` — **generations (downloads and patches) counted apart from previews**: `totalCount`/`successCount`/`failureCount`/`successRate`/percentiles/top dependencies and Boot versions are the generations'; `previewCount`; `byKind` (generations and previews per kind); `perDay` (every day of the period, the server's time zone, generations by kind and previews); `byEndpoint`, `byDepartment` (`(none)`), `topUsers`; `failures` grouped by message with the latest occurrence; `retentionDays`. `GET /admin/activity/events?from&to&kind&action&status&department&username&q&page&size` — newest first, paged (200 a page at most); dates are days, `to` inclusive; `action=generation` is downloads and patches; `q` looks in artifact, user, endpoint and error; the pre-V30 `other` rows are left out unless asked for; a bad date or status is a 400. `GET /admin/activity/events.csv` — the same filter, 10 000 rows at most, UTF-8 with a BOM, formula cells led by a quote. `/recent` stays.
- *Found on the way*: `GET /starter.preview` with a parameter left out answered 400 "Text must not be null" (the preview binds the request without the framework's defaults, deliberately not `initialize()` which overwrites what was sent). It now fills only the missing fields from the metadata (`ProjectPreviewController.fillMissing`).
- *Tried on a copy of the real database* (5 events, 3–12 Nov): `V28`–`V30` applied; the two fullstack previews without artifact stayed so (nothing to recover); new generations through every endpoint came out as above.

*Limits.* The summary is computed in Java over the period's rows (fine for thousands, not millions). Day boundaries are the server's zone. Department is what the request named, not the user's. Remote addresses and user names are kept for the whole retention.

Pinned by `GenerationAuditCoverageTests` (every endpoint's row, kinds, actions, failure reasons, detection calls not recorded, the summary's split, the filters, paging, the CSV, retention) and the existing audit tests, which pass unchanged through the admin API.

**Admin identity and catalog history (2026-11).** The admin screens had one shared password and no record of who changed a catalog row, nor a way back. Now an admin is named, every catalog change is logged with who made it and the row before and after, and any change — or everything one request did — can be undone.

- *Signing in* (`admin/AdminAuthController`, `AdminDirectory`). `POST /admin/login/sso`: the SSO gateway's user header (`menora.audit.user-header`, `userinfo`) names the caller; they are an admin when one of their LDAP groups contains `admin.group`, as Menora's LDAP REST service answers — `GET {admin.ldap-rest.base-url}/employee/findGroupsByCn/{user}`, a JSON list of group names, the service and the contains-rule the generated apps' `ldap-auth-rest` use; an answer is kept a minute; 401 with no header, 403 not an admin, 503 the directory did not answer. `admin.dev-users` are admins without the directory (development, tests; a start-up warning says so). `POST /admin/login` (the shared password) stays while `admin.password-login` is true (default), its holder named "&lt;user&gt; (password)" when the header names them, else "password". `GET /admin/login/options` (open) says what the login screen may offer and who the header names; `GET /admin/me` who a token is. **The base URL and the group have no default** — set `ADMIN_LDAP_REST_BASE_URL` and `ADMIN_GROUP` per environment; until then only the password signs in. **Only a stub of the LDAP service has been tried** (`AdminDirectoryTest`); the real service's answer shape is taken from the generated `LdapService`.
- *Tokens* (`AdminTokenStore`) carry the admin's name in the signed payload (`<expiry>.<nonce>.<base64url name>`; an older two-part token reads as "password"). `AdminAuthFilter` puts it on the request (`AdminAuthFilter.USER_ATTRIBUTE`) and opens a `CatalogChangeContext` (actor = the name, source "admin", a request id) for the request.
- *The change log* (`V31`, `admin_catalog_change`; `admin/history/CatalogHistoryRecorder`). A Hibernate post-insert/update/delete listener on the session factory, for the catalog tables (`CatalogHistoryRecorder.TRACKED`: groups, entries, file contributions, build customizations, sub-options, compatibility, starter templates and their dependencies, module templates and mappings, entity template sets, files and default dependencies, palettes, versions, departments, fullstack examples — not the saved team models, not the audit, not the catalog-sync records). Each row: when, actor, source, request id, entity type, table, row id, CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE, a label (the row's depId, setKey, path, name…), the row before and after as JSON (references as ids, collections left out, normalized through JSON text), the changed fields; an UPDATE that changed nothing is not logged. Written with JDBC on the change's own connection, so it commits and rolls back with it. **Bulk JPQL and native SQL pass Hibernate's events by**: the import's `deleteAllInBatch` became row-by-row deletes and a flush (`ConfigurationExportImportService.clear`), the default-dependencies bulk delete a row-by-row default method (`EntityTemplateSetDefaultDepRepository.deleteBySetId`) — catalog code must keep it that way.
- *Sources* (`CatalogChangeContext`): "admin" for a signed-in request, narrowed by the endpoint — "import" (`/admin/import`), "catalog-sync" (`/admin/catalog-sync/apply`), "template-reset" (`/admin/entity-template-sets/reset`), "restore"; at start "release-sync" by "system" for what the syncs do to an existing database (one request); the first seeding of an empty database is not logged (suppressed). **A scope must enclose the flush**: `DataSeeder` flushes inside its scope because its own transaction commits after it; a scope set inside a `@Transactional` method would log its changes as "system".
- *Restoring* (`CatalogHistoryService`, `CatalogHistoryController`, behind the admin login). `POST /admin/history/{id}/undo` puts the row back as it was before the change — a created row removed, a deleted one re-inserted **with its own id** (JDBC insert from the logged columns, then logged as CREATE, so whatever referred to it finds it again), an update reversed; `POST /admin/history/{id}/restore` takes the row to the version the change made; `POST /admin/history/requests/{requestId}/undo` undoes a request's changes last-first in one transaction (an import of hundreds of rows back to the ids it replaced). Undo first checks that the row is still as the change left it; otherwise 409 `{detail, rows}` naming the rows and the fields changed since, unless `{force: true}`. Everything a restore does is logged as "restore" by whoever asked; the metadata cache is rebuilt after. `GET /admin/history` (filters: entityType, rowId, actor, source, requestId, from, to, q; paged), `/admin/history/requests` (grouped), `/admin/history/{id}`.
- *Tried on a copy of the real database*: V28–V31 applied; the start's template sync was logged as one "release-sync" request of 92 changes; an admin's edit, its undo and a forced undo as expected.

*Limits.* One admin role. The log is kept for ever (no retention). Rows the release ships are re-seeded by their own rules (the template sync puts a deleted shipped template file back on the next start regardless of an undo). A restore of a row whose referenced row is gone fails on the foreign key — undo the parent first, or the whole request. The UPDATE of a row saved from outside Hibernate's view (a detached entity without loaded state) is logged without "before" and cannot be undone.

Pinned by `AdminIdentityAndHistoryTests` (SSO sign-in and refusals, the password's holder named, an edit logged and undone by another admin, a conflict refused then forced, a deleted row back with its id and taken away again, a whole import undone to its original ids), `AdminDirectoryTest` (a stub LDAP service: member, non-member, unreachable, cached; dev users; the name in the token) and `AdminLoginTest`.

**SQL wizard from a live database (2026-11).** The SQL wizard and the fullstack DDL import took pasted DDL only. Both can now read the tables and views of a live PostgreSQL, SQL Server, Oracle or DB2 for IBM i database: through a **source** an admin set up, or a database typed in by hand.

- *Reading* (`liveschema/`). `LiveSchemaReader` uses JDBC `DatabaseMetaData` for tables, views, columns, the primary key, foreign keys (grouped by constraint, in key order) and unique indexes. It adds one catalog query per dialect for CHECK constraints (`pg_constraint`, `sys.check_constraints`, `ALL_CONSTRAINTS`, `QSYS2.SYSCST`/`SYSCHKCST`), Oracle's identity columns (`ALL_TAB_IDENTITY_COLS`) and PostgreSQL's enum types (`pg_enum`, read as a list of values). `CheckClauses` reads each database's spelling of `col IN ('A','B')` back as a list: PostgreSQL's `= ANY (ARRAY[…]::text[])`, SQL Server's OR chain of `[col]='A'`, and the text as written elsewhere. Anything else is not an enum. A connection is read-only, opened for one call and closed after it. Every statement has a 30 s timeout and the login 10 s. At most `menora.live-schema.max-concurrent-reads` (4) reads run at once; the rest wait 5 s, then get a 429.
- *What differs per database* (`DialectSupport`): the driver, the URL **built from parts** (host, port, database or service name, the DB2 library — never a URL from the caller, so no driver property such as a socket factory or an H2 `INIT` can be slipped in; `ConnectionSpec` checks each part), the timeout properties, the system schemas left out, the fold case, and how a driver's `TYPE_NAME`/`COLUMN_SIZE`/`DECIMAL_DIGITS` is written back as a type `TypeMappers` reads. Examples: `int8`→`BIGINT`, `bpchar`→`CHAR(n)`, `nvarchar` of 2^30-1→`NVARCHAR(MAX)`, SQL Server's `"bigint identity"`→`IDENTITY(1,1)`, Oracle's NUMBER without precision, `TIMESTAMP(6) WITH TIME ZONE`, DB2's `FOR BIT DATA`. An unknown type reads as text.
- *One path through text* (`LiveDdlWriter`). What was read is written as the script a person would have pasted: a `CREATE TABLE` per table (identity, NOT NULL, primary key, `UNIQUE (…)`, `CHECK (col IN (…))`), then the foreign keys **between the tables read** as `ALTER TABLE … ADD FOREIGN KEY` (so the script also runs as the Flyway baseline whatever the order; a key to a table not read is a comment, and the column stays a plain field), and a `SELECT <columns> FROM <view>` per view. A name is bare when it is a plain word in the fold case and not a keyword, otherwise quoted (`"OrderDate"`, `[User]`). The schema is kept (`hr.employees`, so `@Table(schema = …)`) unless `qualify: false`. The SQL wizard therefore generates exactly what that script pasted would, and the person sees and can edit it first.
- *Endpoints* (`config/LiveSchemaController`). Open, like the wizards; the access gate applies. `GET /metadata/live-schema/options` returns the enabled sources (name, dialect, description, default schema — not where they are), whether typed connections are allowed, which drivers are installed, the default ports and `max-tables`. `POST /metadata/live-schema/schemas` takes `{connection}` and returns `{schemas, defaultSchema}`: the source's default schema, else the one the connection opened in. `POST …/tables` takes `{connection, schema}` and returns `{tables: [{name, kind: TABLE|VIEW}], truncated}`, at most 5000. `POST …/ddl` takes `{connection, schema, tables, qualify?}` and returns `{dialect, sql, tables, views, warnings}` for the SQL wizard. `POST /metadata/fullstack/import-live` takes the same body and returns `{entities, note}`. Its tables go through `SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter.convert` over that DDL, so a key between two read tables becomes a relation and a CHECK list an enum. Each view comes through `convertSelect` with its fields **typed from their columns** (`SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter.fieldTypeOf`), and the note says the first column was made the key. `connection` is `{source}` or `{dialect, host, port?, database?, library?, user, password, trustServerCertificate?}`. Errors are `{error, detail}`: 400 for a bad part, an unknown table, a failed connect or a missing driver (the driver's words, with the password taken out); 403 when typed connections are off or the host is not allowed; 404 for an unknown source; 429 when busy. Each read is logged with the source or `host/database`, the schema, the count and the `userinfo` header — never the password.
- *Typed connections* (`menora.live-schema.typed-connections`, default true). Anyone may name a database, so the server connects wherever they say. `menora.live-schema.allowed-hosts` (comma-separated globs, `*.menora.co.il`) limits that, and is worth setting per environment. A typed password is sent with each call and kept nowhere, on the server or in the browser.
- *Admin sources* (`V32`, `initializer_schema_source`, `db/entity/SchemaSourceEntity`, `admin/SchemaSourceAdminController` at `/admin/schema-sources`). A source holds the parts plus the user. The **password is not stored**: `passwordRef` names the environment variable `SCHEMA_SOURCE_…` or the property `menora.live-schema.secrets.…` that holds it (`LiveSchemaService.PASSWORD_REF`; anything else is a 400, so a source cannot be pointed at `admin.password` or the datasource's). The list says `passwordAvailable`. `POST /admin/schema-sources/test` connects with a form, saved or not, and reports the schema count or why it failed. Sources are in the catalog history (`TRACKED`) and **not** in admin export/import (they name this environment's databases and secrets).
- *Drivers.* PostgreSQL, SQL Server, Oracle and DB2 for IBM i are runtime dependencies of the application (the first three at the versions the application's Boot manages; jt400 pinned at `net.sf.jt400:jt400:20.0.6`, the version the catalog's `db2` dependency uses). jt400 used to sit in a `db2-driver` Maven profile, on the belief that it was not in the offline repository; it is, and the profile is gone (2026-11). A driver a build leaves out is still offered as "no driver on this server". Code uses `java.sql` only.
- *Tests.* `menora.live-schema.allow-h2` (tests only) adds H2, an in-memory database by name. `LiveSchemaIntegrationTests` covers the endpoints over HTTP against such a schema, including the script generated into a project through `/starter-wizard.zip`, the fullstack import, the errors and the admin sources. `LiveDdlWriterTest` takes each driver's documented column descriptions through the writer and back through the SQL wizard's parser and the DDL import. `CheckClausesTest` and `LiveSchemaServiceTest` cover the typed-connection switch, the host list and the URL parts. `LiveSchemaDockerTests` (`-Dsmoke.docker=true`) reads real PostgreSQL 16, SQL Server 2022 and Oracle Free 23 servers in Docker and, on PostgreSQL, runs the script into an empty schema as a baseline — all three passed on 2026-11-16.

*Limits.* DB2 for IBM i has not been read from a server: there is no container to run it against (the driver, jt400, ships with the application). In the SQL wizard a view's fields are `String` (the wizard's SELECT views were always so); the fullstack import types them. A PostgreSQL enum column becomes a CHECK-limited text column in the baseline script, not the enum type. Writing to the real column then needs `stringtype=unspecified`. Indexes, defaults, comments, sequences and triggers are not read. Tables are read one round trip at a time (fine for tens, slow for hundreds; `max-tables` 300). (*Round 14, phase 5* changed three of these: indexes, defaults and comments are read, keys and indexes with one catalog query per schema, and the wizard types a view's fields from the casts of its SELECT.)

**Who changed what (2026-11).** The `audit` option names the user as well as the time. A new `history` option keeps every change to a row and lets an admin put the row back to an earlier version.

- *Who* (part of `audit`, every audited entity). The entity gets `@CreatedBy created_by` and `@LastModifiedBy updated_by`, both in both backend sets. `config/JpaAuditingConfig` gains an `AuditorAware` (`auditorAwareRef = "auditor"`) that reads `web/RequestUser.current()`: the request's `userinfo` header, `anonymous` for a request without one, and `system` outside a request (demo data). The DTO, CSV, XLSX, `SORTABLE`, the detail rows and the migration (`varchar(255)`) carry both. A soft-deleted row's native restore sets `updated_by` too. The list endpoint takes `changedBy` — `me` (the caller) or a user id — matching rows created **or** last changed by them (a `findAll(q, [filters,] changedBy, pageable)` overload, audit only). Frontend: *Created by* / *Updated by* columns, **hidden until asked for** (`WHO_HIDDEN`, the Columns menu; a list keeps only what differs from that), detail rows, and a **Changed by me** toggle in the toolbar (`withMine` adds `changedBy=me` to the data hook's filters). The activity feed names who made each change. An audited project's API client now sends the dev `userinfo` (`usesRequestUser` sets `sendsDevUser`). Field names `createdBy`/`updatedBy` are reserved under audit; page `columns`/`sort` accept them.
- *The change log* (`history`, a `ScaffoldOption`, per entity; writable, table-backed, single-key entities — `FeatureContext.historyApplies` / per-entity `historyApplicable`; project `hasHistory`, `historyEntities`). Its parts:
  - `history/ChangeLogEntry`: table `app_change_log` (`SchemaBuilder.changeLog`, in the Flyway baseline), with entity (kebab), row id, action (CREATE / UPDATE / DELETE / RESTORE), when, who, the changed fields and the row before and after as JSON (`LONG32VARCHAR`).
  - `ChangeLogRecorder`: a Hibernate post-insert/update/delete listener, the admin history's technique, written with JDBC on the change's own connection. It sees **every** path — save, bulk, transition, claim, import, composed child rows, DemoDataLoader.
  - What a snapshot leaves out: collections (many-to-many links are **not** logged), `@Formula`s, and `version`/`deleted`/the audit columns. A link is kept as the id it points at.
  - When an UPDATE counts: only when a person-made value changed, with numbers compared by value (40 vs 40.00).
  - `ChangeHistory`: `of(entity, id)`, newest first, at most 200. `restore(entity, id, changeId, latest, force)` sets the row's updatable properties to what that change left, through Hibernate's metadata and `DirectFieldAccessor`, with links via `getReference`. It leaves the workflow's status and assignee and any FILE/IMAGE field alone (`Tracked.keep`). It answers 409 (`ResourceConflictException`) when a change newer than the caller's `latest` exists unless `force`, and 400 for a DELETE entry. The restore is logged as RESTORE. `restored(...)` logs a soft-delete undo, which is native SQL Hibernate does not see.
  - Endpoints: `GET /api/x/{id}/history` at the read level, and `POST /api/x/{id}/history/{changeId}/restore {latest?, force?}`, ADMIN with ldap-auth, anyone without.
  - The datasource configs scan the `history` package (`supportPackages`). The tracked classes are written fully qualified (`entityClass`), so the recorder does not count as using an entity by its bare name (`ReservedNamesGuardTest`).
  - Frontend: `shared/ui/ChangeHistory.tsx` (`hasHistory`) and `features/<e>-form/ui/<E>History.tsx` (`historyApplicable`: field labels and enum labels; `useCallerMay('ADMIN')` with ldap-auth). The panel shows who, when and each field old → new, with **Restore this version** on earlier entries. A 409 re-reads the list and asks "*X* changed it since. Restore anyway?". It sits in the quick-look drawer and the side pane, and on the record page as a **Change history** tab (a section without tabs; `recordHasHistory`). Strings are gated `hasAudit` / `hasHistory`.
- *Bytes.* Projects without `audit` and `history` keep theirs. Every audited example moved (Blog, Approvals), and `widgets.tsx` (the activity feed) moved wherever widgets ship. Golden manifests were re-pinned.
- *Verified for real* (`-Dsmoke.be=true`). `LIVE_HISTORY_TESTS` runs in the Approvals backend, also on its Flyway baseline (Hibernate `validate`): who-columns from the header and "system" for demo rows; CREATE/UPDATE entries with only the changed field; a transition logged; a restore refused over an unseen change, then done, keeping the status; "changed by me"; a deletion logged and not restorable. The Orders every-option and baseline builds run with `history` on as well.

*Limits.* Many-to-many links and composed rows' membership are not in a parent's history (each composed row has its own). Values are shown raw (a link as its id, a date as ISO); old JSON keeps the property names of its time, so a renamed field shows under its old name and is not restored. The log is kept forever, with no retention. A restore sets values without the DTO's Bean Validation. The listener runs for every tracked class in the JVM, so tests that save rows see entries too.

Pinned by `FullstackChangeHistoryTests` and the golden manifests.

**Entity events and notifications (2026-11).** A generated fullstack app can tell the world that a row changed: a new `events` option sends each change to Kafka, to webhooks and to the open browsers, and a workflow transition can e-mail people (`notify`). Both leave through one outbox, written in the change's own transaction.

- *Wire.* `opts.scaffold: ["events"]` (a `ScaffoldOption`, per entity; writable, table-backed, single-key entities, like `history` — `FeatureContext.eventsApply`, per-entity `eventsApplicable`). A transition's `notify` (`TransitionDto` component `mailTo`, JSON `notify` — `notify()` is `Object`'s; `EntityDefinition.Transition.mailTo`): `assignee` (the workflow needs an `assigneeField`), `creator` (needs the `audit` option on the entity — checked in `FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts`, where the options are known), `ADMIN`, `USER`; unknown or repeated is a 400. `notify` works without the `events` option.
- *Context* (`FeatureContext.putEvents(ctx, entities, deps)`, both render paths, after `putHistory`): `hasEvents`, `hasNotifications`, `hasOutbox` (either), `outboxEntities` (class, kebab key, publishes?, status field, read level), `notifyTransitions` (`entity:transition` → assignee field, creator, roles, the entity's and the move's labels), `notifyStateLabels` (each state's label, `enumLabels` else humanized), `eventsKafka` (with the `kafka` dependency), `notifyLdapRest` (with `ldap-auth-rest`). It sets `usesRequestUser`. Per entity: `eventsApplicable`, `eventsRestores` (with soft delete), `transitionEvents` (a workflow, with events or a notifying move), `workflowNotifies`. The `events` package joins `supportPackages`; `app_outbox` joins the Flyway baseline (`SchemaBuilder.outbox`).
- *Backend* (`events/`, both sets — the Lombok one borrows):
  - `OutboxMessage` / `OutboxRepository`: table `app_outbox` — one row per change **per channel** (`kafka`, `webhook:<name>`, `mail`), the payload JSON, status PENDING/SENT/FAILED, attempts, `next_attempt_at`, `locked_until`, `last_error`.
  - `EventRecorder`: a Hibernate post-insert/update/delete listener (the change log's technique), writing with JDBC on the change's connection. An event is `{id, type: <entity>.<created|updated|deleted|restored|transitioned>, entity, rowId, occurredAt, actor, changedFields?, data, before?}`, plus `transition`/`from`/`to` for a move. The services mark a move with the static `EventRecorder.transition(entity, id, name)` (a thread-local cleared after the transaction) and report a soft-delete restore with `restored(row)` (native SQL Hibernate does not see). An update that changes only the framework's columns sends nothing.
  - `OutboxRelay` (`@EnableScheduling`, `@EnableConfigurationProperties`): every `app.events.relay.interval-ms` (5000) takes due rows, locks each with a conditional update (several instances never send one at once; a lock that runs out is sent again — **at least once**), hands it to the `OutboxSender` of its channel; a failure backs off 30 s → 1 h and is FAILED after `max-attempts` (10). A channel that fails is skipped for the rest of that pass, so a broker outage does not hold up webhooks and mail. Sent rows are dropped after `retention-days` (7).
  - `KafkaEventSender` (with `kafka`): topic `<topic-prefix><entity>` (default `<artifactId>.`), key = row id, through the catalog's `KafkaTemplate<String, String>`; `application-test.yaml` turns it off for the generated tests.
  - `WebhookSender`: a JSON POST with `X-Event-Id`, `X-Event-Type` and, with a secret, `X-Event-Signature: sha256=<HMAC-SHA256 hex>`; `events` patterns (`*.deleted`, `order.*`) choose what each webhook gets.
  - `MailNotifier` + `MailAddresses` (with a notifying move): `SimpleMailMessage` through Boot's `JavaMailSender` (needs `spring.mail.host`; `spring-boot-starter-mail` is added by `FullstackProjectGenerationConfiguration.notificationMailCustomizer` — the catalog has no mail dependency). Users are the row's assignee and/or creator, never the person who moved it; a role is the addresses in `app.events.mail.roles`. An address comes from `app.events.mail.users`, a user id that is one, **the LDAP REST service** (`GET {menora.ldap-rest.base-url}{app.events.mail.lookup-path}`, default `/employee/findEmailByCn/{user}` — the endpoint the company is adding; text, a JSON string or `{email|mail}`, kept an hour), else `<user>@<domain>`. Subject and body are templates with `{entity} {id} {transition} {from} {to} {actor}`, in English unless configured.
  - `LiveEvents` (with `events`): `GET /api/events/stream`, an `SseEmitter` per page (30 min, a comment every 25 s, `X-Accel-Buffering: no` for nginx) that gets `change {entity, rowId, kind}` after commit — only for entities the caller may read (`PermissionService.holds` at the entity's read level, with ldap-auth). Each instance tells its own pages.
  - `EventsProperties` (`app.events.*`) documents every setting; the root README gets an *Events and e-mails* section (`FullstackRootFiles.eventsReadme`).
- *Frontend* (with `events`): `shared/api/LiveUpdates.tsx` wraps the app in `main.tsx` (both sets render `main.tsx` through Mustache now, bytes unchanged without the option). It reads the stream with `fetch` (EventSource cannot send the dev `userinfo` header), groups changes 800 ms apart, waits while the tab is hidden, reconnects with growing delays, and bumps `RefreshTick` — the reload count every list, chart and widget already reads. A dashboard with a Refresh adds it to its own (`dashLive`), and the bell (`NotificationBell.tsx`, rendered through Mustache too) re-counts on it. The 60 s polling stays as the fallback.
- *Bytes.* Projects without the option and without a notifying move are byte for byte what they were (no golden manifest moved). `Outbox` is a reserved entity name.
- *Verified for real* (`-Dsmoke.be=true`): `liveApiOfAnEventsBackendBehaves` builds Approvals with `events`, Kafka on the build, soft delete and an approve that e-mails the creator and the admins, and runs `EVENTS_LIVE_TESTS` in it — a webhook server of its own (signature, created/updated/transitioned/deleted/restored events, a 500 left PENDING with its error while the other webhook gets its call), a caught mail (recipients, subject, the states' labels), and the stream over MockMvc. The mail starter of Boot 3.2 is not in the offline repository, so that build swaps in `spring-context-support` + `jakarta.mail-api` 2.1.1 + `angus-mail` 2.0.1 (`useOfflineMail`). The Approvals Flyway build runs with `events` too, and both Approvals frontends build and lint with it.

*Limits.* Kafka's channel is compiled but has not sent to a broker here. The LDAP REST address lookup is written against an endpoint that does not exist yet (its path is a setting). E-mails are plain text, in English unless the templates are configured, and link to the application, not the row. A change made on another instance reaches an open page at its next refresh. Many-to-many links are not in an event's `data`. Events are not ordered across a retry.

Pinned by `FullstackEntityEventsTests` and the smoke builds.

**Events wizard: Avro payloads (2026-11).** The events wizard (*Round 10, phase 4*) refused a message whose `schemaFormat` is Avro. It now writes Avro channels, with **no schema registry**: a message is the record in Avro's single-object encoding (a two-byte marker, the 8-byte fingerprint of the writer's schema, the binary record), so producer and consumer hold the same schema. No existing manifest moved; a document without Avro is written as before.

- *Reading (`AsyncApiReader`).* A message (2.x `schemaFormat` beside the payload, 3.x `payload: {schemaFormat, schema}`) whose format starts with `application/vnd.apache.avro` (`+json`, `+yaml` too) is `EventMessage.avro`; its schema may be written in place, a `$ref`, or its JSON text as a string. One channel is all Avro or all JSON — mixed is the channel's `problem`. `EventChannel.avro()`; `DetectedChannel.format` (`avro`/`json`) tells the drawer.
- *Schemas (`events/AvroSchemas`).* No Avro library in the offline repository, so the schemas are walked as trees. Sources: the Avro messages' payloads, and every `components.schemas` entry that is an Avro named type (`type: record|enum|fixed` with a `name`, or `{schemaFormat: avro, schema}`) — those are taken out of the JSON payload set. Each top-level schema is a file `src/main/avro/<full name>.avsc`; one without a namespace takes the payload package (so its class has a package); a named type defined twice the same way is written once (the later copy becomes its name); defined two ways, a payload that is not a record, a name nobody defines, a name Avro does not allow, or files that refer to each other in a circle are 400s with the reason. **The plugin reads every file with one parser and in no order of its own**, so the files others refer to are listed, dependencies first, as the plugin's `<imports>` (`avroImports`).
- *Build (`eventsBuildCustomizer` → `addAvro`).* With any Avro schema in the document: `org.apache.avro:avro` at `${avro.version}` (`EventsCodeGenerator.AVRO_VERSION`, 1.11.4 — the first 1.11 without CVE-2024-47561), and `avro-maven-plugin`'s `schema` goal at `generate-sources` over `src/main/avro` (`stringType` String, decimals as `BigDecimal`, the imports).
- *Code* (`templates/wizard/events/Avro*.java.mustache`): `AvroKafkaConfig` — `avroKafkaTemplate` (`KafkaTemplate<String, byte[]>`) and `avroListenerContainerFactory`, built from **the project's own `ProducerFactory` / `ConsumerFactory` settings** (the catalog `KafkaConfig`'s servers and SASL) with the value as bytes; the catalog's String beans stay for JSON channels. `<Channel>Producer` sends `payload.toByteBuffer()`; `<Channel>Listener` reads `Type.getDecoder().decode(bytes)`. Several kinds of message on one channel: the listener's `decode` tries each type's decoder — the fingerprint tells them apart (`MissingSchemaException` means "not this one") — and hands over a `SpecificRecord`. The classes are imported by simple name unless two of a channel's share one.
- *Tests* (`generateTests`): the producer's record out through a mocked template and back through the decoder; the listener reads one and refuses bytes that are not Avro. A test value is the record's all-arguments constructor with a simple value per field (`AvroSchemas.sample`: logical types as `Instant`, `LocalDate`, `BigDecimal` at the schema's scale…; a union with null gets `null`); a field type with no simple value (fixed, uuid) leaves that record without a round-trip test.
- *Verified.* `EventsAvroTest`, `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.eventsWizardWithAvroPayloadsAddsTheSchemasAndThePlugin`, the golden case `events__avro__tests` (fixture `wizard-fixtures/orders-avro-asyncapi.yaml`). The plugin is not in the offline repository, so here the check was by hand: the generated `.avsc` files parsed with Avro 1.11.3's own parser in the plugin's order, classes written to the SpecificCompiler's shape, and the generated sources and tests compiled and run against the real Avro runtime (round trips with every logical type of the fixture, dispatch by fingerprint, the template built from the catalog's settings). `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.eventsWizardAvroBackendCompilesAndPassesItsTests` builds it with the real plugin where Avro resolves: `-Dsmoke.be=true -Dsmoke.avro=true` — **not yet run**.

*Limits (Avro).* No schema registry (Confluent's or Apicurio's): the topic carries the fingerprint, not a schema id, so a consumer that must read an older schema adds it to its decoder by hand (the `AvroKafkaConfig` javadoc says how). Payloads must be records. Avro's protocol files (`.avpr`, `.avdl`) and `.avsc` files outside the document are not read.

**Deeper field model, phase 1 (2026-11): help text and unique keys.** Item 19 of the backlog, in three phases (help text and unique keys; an expression language and cross-field rules; show-when and computed fields). Projects without either are byte for byte what they were; the Projects example carries both (its golden manifests moved, deliberately).

- *Help text.* `FieldDefinitionDto.help` (NON_NULL; at most `MAX_HELP` = 300 characters, a 400 at `…fields[i]` past it) → `FieldDefinition.help` → `putText(fv, "help", …)` + `hasHelp`. The generated form passes it to `Field`'s existing `hint` (shown under the input while there is no error, and the input's `aria-describedby`); both frontend sets share `EntityForm`.
- *Unique keys.* `EntityDefinitionDto.uniqueKeys: [{fields: [...]}]` (NON_NULL) → `EntityDefinition.UniqueKey` (`FullstackRequestValidator.parseUniqueKeys`, each a 400 at `entities[i].uniqueKeys[k]`): two to eight members, each a non-key field or a MANY_TO_ONE link (case-insensitive, made canonical), no long text (Oracle and SQL Server index no LOB), no member twice, no two keys over the same set, at most ten keys, none on a SELECT-backed view, and **at least one member rows can differ in** — not all enums, yes/no fields and patterned texts, which the demo data and the generated tests cannot vary by row number. A composed child (rows edited in the parent's form) may have none: a save inserts the new rows before it deletes the removed ones, so a row replaced by one with the same key would clash (`checkComposition`).
  - The constraint is `uk_<table>_<col>_<col>…` (`SchemaBuilder.uniqueKeyName`, the same 60-character rule as every other name), in the entity's `@Table(uniqueConstraints = …)` (both backend sets; `uniqueConstraintsJava`) and in the Flyway baseline (`SchemaBuilder.tableOf` — the schema model already took several columns, so update migrations add and drop keys with no new code).
  - A clash: `ApiExceptionHandler` (gated `hasUniqueKeys`, from `FeatureContext.putUniqueKeys`, both render paths) looks for each key's constraint name in the driver's message, longest name first, and answers 409 with `detail` "Another Task has the same Project and Title" and `errors` on each member as a client knows it (a link by its `<field>Id`). Any other unique clash keeps its generic message. The frontend's api-client keeps a 409's `errors` for the form when the project has a key (`fieldErrors` was a 400's only), so the clash shows on the fields.
  - Demo rows: a member with a default value is varied by row number like a unique column (`seedExpression(…, distinct)`), and the loader leaves out a row that would repeat a key (a set per key, `java.util.Arrays.<Object>asList(row.getX(), …)`). The generated API tests already vary every value by row number.
- *Fixed on the way:* `EntityDefinition.withOpt` (an import page switches `csvImport` on for its entity) dropped the entity's `access`, so an entity with its own levels and an import page got the defaults. It keeps every property now (`FullstackFieldModelTests.anEntityWithAnImportPageKeepsItsAccessLevels`).
- *Schema limits* `maxHelp`, `maxUniqueKeys`, `maxUniqueKeyFields` join `GET /metadata/fullstack/schema`.
- *Verified.* `FullstackFieldModelTests` (the generated code, the 400s, the access fix); `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.liveApiOfAUniqueKeyBehaves` builds Projects on its Flyway baseline with demo data and the generated API tests, and drives it: a second task of one project and title is a 409 naming both, the same title elsewhere is fine, an edit into a clash is refused (H2 only).

*Limits (phase 1).* The clash is recognized by the constraint's name in the driver's message: H2, PostgreSQL, Oracle and SQL Server put it there; DB2 for i's SQL0803 may not, and then the generic 409 is all there is. Help text is in the form only (not the details or a table header). A soft-deleted row still holds its key.

**Deeper field model, phase 2 (2026-11): the expression language and rules.** Projects without rules are byte for byte what they were; the Projects example has three (its golden manifests moved, deliberately).

- *The language (`fullstack/expr`).* One line over the row's **own** fields: `Expr` (lexer + recursive-descent parser → `Lit`/`Ref`/`Unary`/`Binary`/`Call`; `or` < `and` < `not` < one comparison (they do not chain) < `+ -` < `* /` < unary `-`; `&&`, `||`, `!`, `=` are spellings of `and`, `or`, `not`, `==`; texts in either quotes; at most 500 characters), `ExprCheck` (types: number, text, yes/no, date, date-time, instant, time, enum; a name must be a field, an enum compares only with `==`/`!=` and with one of its own values, spelled as the constant in the output; `+` adds numbers, joins texts, or adds days to a date; `-` takes days from a date; UUID/FILE/IMAGE fields only inside `isEmpty`; functions `isEmpty today now daysBetween length abs round date('yyyy-mm-dd')`; each error says where, "(at character n)"), `ExprCode` (Java over `row` against the generated `rules/Expr.java`, TypeScript over `value` against `shared/ui/rules.ts`), `ExprEval` (the generator's own interpreter). **Semantics, the same in all three:** numbers are decimals (Java `BigDecimal`, so `2.00 == 2`), an empty value makes an operation empty and a comparison unknown, `and`/`or` are three-valued, division by zero is empty — and **a rule that comes out unknown holds** (asking for a value is Required's or `isEmpty`'s job). Phase 3 (show-when, computed fields) reuses it.
- *Wire.* `EntityDefinitionDto.rules: [{when?, check, message, field?}]` (NON_NULL) → `EntityDefinition.Rule` (`FullstackRequestValidator.parseRules`, a 400 at `entities[i].rules[k]`): `check` and `when` must be yes/no conditions; a message of at most 200 characters; `field` (where the message shows) one of the entity's fields, else the first field `check` reads (a check that reads none must say). At most 20; none on a read-only entity or a SELECT view (never saved), none on a composed child yet (the parent's save does not run them — `checkComposition`).
- *Backend (gated `hasRules`, both sets).* `rules/Expr.java` (the runtime), `rules/<Entity>Rules.java` (`violations(row)` → field → message, first per field; `check(row)` throws `web/RuleViolationException`), and `ApiExceptionHandler.handleRules` → 400 with `errors`, the same shape as bean validation, so the form shows it on the field. The service checks before **every save**: create, update, a workflow move, each row of a bulk change, and an import (each violation an `ImportError` of its row). Claim/release/assign (only the assignee changes) and the demo loader (straight to the repository) do not.
- *Frontend.* `validate<Entity>` runs the same checks after the field checks (`import * as rule from '@shared/ui/rules'`), a field's own error first.
- *The rows the generator makes must pass them (`RuleRows`).* The API tests' rows, the form test's valid row: for each field the rules read (and the generator may choose — not a key, unique, patterned or attachment field), candidates by type (numbers 1, 0, 10, 100… inside min/max; texts the usual and a long one; every enum value; yes/no; dates today ± 0/1/7/30/365 days; times; empty when optional), searched with backtracking until every rule holds — in every state the generated test puts the row in (none, the initial one, where its first move lands). Dates are counted from today (Java `LocalDate.now().plusDays(k)`, TS a `day(k)` helper in the test file), so a rule over `today()` holds on the day the tests run. **When no combination passes, the entity's API tests are blocked** with the reason naming the rule ("no row the tests can make passes its rule 2 (…)"), the existing mechanism for rows the tests cannot make — the project still builds; the form test's "passes a complete record" is left out. The update test never changes a field a rule reads. Demo rows keep their varied values (they bypass the rules); a date a rule reads is shifted by the chosen days, so two dates keep the order a rule wants.
- *Example.* Projects: a project ends on or after it starts; a task is due on or after it starts; a finished task (`when status == 'DONE'`) has an estimate.
- *Schema limits* `maxRules`, `maxRuleMessage`, `maxExpression`.
- *Verified.* `ExprTest` (parsing, every refusal, Java and TS output, the semantics), `RuleRowsTest` (a rule that must hold after the test's workflow move; the rule that stops a search), `FullstackFieldModelTests` (generated code, the blocked tests, the 400s); `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.liveApiOfTheFieldModelBehaves` (Projects with demo data, Flyway and its API tests — 21 tests, through the rules — plus a 400 on `endsOn`, an empty date holding, an estimate asked of a finished task on a create and an edit); `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontendRulesPass` (Projects' frontend lints, builds and passes its 28 tests).

**Deeper field model, phase 3 (2026-11): show-when and computed fields.** The last phase of backlog item 19. Projects without them are byte for byte what they were; the Projects example has one of each (its golden manifests moved, deliberately).

- *Wire.* `FieldDefinitionDto.showWhen` and `.formula` (NON_NULL), expressions in the phase-2 language → `FieldDefinition.showWhen`, `.formula` (`computed()`), `.requiredWhenShown`. `FullstackRequestValidator.checkDerivations` (once all the entity's fields are known; each a 400 at `fields[i]`): a show-when is a yes/no condition that does not read its own field; a formula comes out as the field's kind (`formulaType`: numbers, texts, yes/no, dates, date-times, instants, times — not enums, UUIDs or attachments) and reads no other computed field; a computed field is never required, has no default and no show-when; neither on a primary key, on a read-only entity or view, on a workflow's status or assignee (pass 2), or on a composed child (`checkComposition`). **A required field with a show-when becomes optional, `requiredWhenShown`**: no NOT NULL, no `@NotNull`, no client blank check — `EntityDefinition.allRules()` adds a rule `when <showWhen>: not isEmpty(<field>)` (backend message "must not be empty", the form's `t('required')`).
- *Backend.* `<Entity>Rules.derive(row)` — a show-when field cleared while its condition is not true, then each computed field set from its formula (`computedJava`: a whole number rounded half up, money to the cent, a text cut to its length — `Expr.toInteger/toLong/money/fit`) — runs before the rules: `apply(row)` = derive + violations, `check(row)` = apply + throw. The service's saves call `check`, the import `apply`, and **the demo loader `derive`** (so demo rows have their computed values; their rules still are not checked). A computed field's view is marked read-only like a workflow's status: no PUT, bulk or inline edit, no import column; a client's value is overwritten. The column is real (stored), so it sorts, filters, exports and charts like any.
- *Frontend.* The form: a show-when field is wrapped in `{rule.applies(<condition>) && (…)}` (with the wizard step's `show(...)` when there are steps — `guardOpen`/`guardClose`, put in `buildEntityContext` once `formHasSteps` is known); a computed field is a read-only input showing `rule.show(<formula>)` live (`computedTs`; a date-time as `rule.localMoment`, an instant as `rule.isoInstant`). `validate<Entity>` first clears hidden fields and computes computed ones in `value` (what the server will save), skips the field checks of computed fields, then runs the checks and rules.
- *Generated tests.* The update and search tests never use a computed or show-when field (the server may clear or overwrite it); the form test has no cases of their own. `RuleRows` never chooses a computed field, expands what a rule reads through formulas and conditions, and derives each scenario as the server would (`Derive`).
- *Example.* Projects: a project's *Days* is `daysBetween(startsOn, endsOn) + 1`; a task's estimate is shown once the task has started (`status != 'TODO'`).
- *Verified.* `FullstackFieldModelTests` (the generated derive, form guard, read-only computed input, validate, the required-when-shown rule, every refusal); `liveApiOfTheFieldModelBehaves` (days computed on a create and an edit whatever the client sends, an estimate cleared on a task to do and kept on one started — 22 tests in the generated project); `fullstackFrontendRulesPass` (lint, build, 26 tests).

*Limits (phase 3).* A formula reads entered fields only (no computed field reads another). A computed value is computed on save, so one over `today()` holds the day it was saved. A hidden field's own checks (length, range) are not run while it is hidden — it is cleared anyway. Show-when and formulas read the row's own fields; a composed child has neither yet.

*Limits (phase 2).* A rule reads the row's own fields only (not a linked row's). Messages are as written (one language). The editor's checker is a mirror (`ui/src/components/fullstack/expr.ts`); the server has the last word. Rules do not run on claim/release/reassign, on a composed child's rows, or on demo rows.

### Tool upgrade (2026-11)

The application itself, not what it generates: the generated projects' default Boot (3.2.1) and every generated byte are unchanged — no golden manifest moved.

- *Spring Boot 3.2.1 → 3.5.16* (`pom.xml` parent). Initializr 0.23.0 is built against Boot 3.5.10, so the tool had been running the framework on an older Boot than it was made for. It brings Spring 6.2, Hibernate 6.6, Flyway 11 and H2 2.3; nothing in `src/main` needed a change to compile, and `application.yml` needed none.
- *The jar keeps the catalog's dotfiles.* maven-jar-plugin 3.4+ (Boot 3.5's) leaves "default excludes" out of the jar — `static-configs/**/.gitignore`, `.dockerignore`, `.editorconfig` — and the start-up catalog sync then fails: "The shipped catalog could not be read". `maven-jar-plugin` `addDefaultExcludes=false` in the pom. **The test suite cannot see this** (it reads `target/classes`); only starting the jar does.
- *An upgraded install.* Started the jar on a copy of a real catalog database: H2 2.3 opens the 2.2 file and Flyway 11 continues Flyway 9's history (it was at 27 and applied the five newer migrations).
- *Schema table vs Hibernate 6.6* (`SchemaDialectTest`, which compares the migration's types with what the application's Hibernate exports). PostgreSQL: 6.6 writes the standard `bigint generated by default as identity`, which is what the migration already wrote, so the test's `bigserial` exception is gone. Oracle: 6.6 writes `generated by default as identity`, so `SchemaDialect.identityClause()` does too (it was `generated as identity`, i.e. ALWAYS; both insert the same way for IDENTITY ids, and BY DEFAULT also lets a person insert an id of their own). H2: 6.6 writes a native `enum ('A','B')` for an `@Enumerated(STRING)` column; the migration keeps `varchar(255)` with its CHECK, which 6.6's `ddl-auto: validate` accepts (tried) and 6.4 wrote itself — so one script fits projects on both Boot lines. The test records that as a known spelling, like H2's TEXT/CLOB.
- *Artifacts.* Eleven artifacts 3.5.16 needs were not in the local repository (the starters for cache, mustache, actuator and logging, spring-hateoas, logback, log4j-to-slf4j, jul-to-slf4j, commons-codec, ehcache 3.10.9, maven-clean-plugin 3.4.1). They came from Maven Central in one online run; **the company Artifactory has to carry them** before the tool builds there.
- *Not done.* Mockito warns that it attaches itself as an agent ("Please add Mockito as an agent"), which a later JDK will refuse; the fix (`-javaagent` through surefire's `argLine`, after JaCoCo's) needs maven-dependency-plugin 3.8.1, which is not in the local repository either. `wsdl4j` stays at 1.6.3: it is the last release there is.

### Structure (2026-11)

Two pieces of structure debt, paid without a feature.

- *`EntityScaffoldContext` by area.* It was 4,626 lines; it keeps the two entry points (`buildProjectContext`, `buildEntityContext`), the context keys they share, the seed order and the palette, and the rest moved — unchanged, member for member — into classes beside it in `fullstack/`:

  | Class | Holds |
  |---|---|
  | `EntityViewModel` | one entity's view-model (`of`, formerly `entityViewModel`), its workflow, form groups, stats fields, `emittedListViews` |
  | `FieldViewModel` | one field's (`of`, formerly `fieldViewModel`), and how each type is shown, imported, bulk-edited, defaulted and seeded (`displayTs`, `cellTs`, `inputType`, `seedExpression`, …) |
  | `RelationViews` | the child lists (declared ONE_TO_MANY and derived inverse collections), their columns, the entity summaries |
  | `FeatureContext` | the scaffold options' flags (`putAudit`, `putHistory`, `putEvents`, `putRules`, `putUniqueKeys`, `putSavedViews`, …), computed fields, `supportPackages` |
  | `PageContext` | the page layout: `putPageContext`, navigation and shell, each page type but dashboards and reports, `PageLinks` |
  | `DashboardContext` | dashboard widgets, report charts, their filters, ranges and drill-through |
  | `TemplateText` | text spliced into code: `putText`, `tsString`, the TS/JSX/Java escapes, the humanized labels, `labelOf` |
  | `SchemaNames` | tables, schemas, columns, join columns and tables (`tableOf`, `columnOf`, …) |

  Callers call the class that holds a member; only the classes with a public member are public. No generated byte changed (both golden suites unchanged).
- *One list page logic for both frontend sets.* The two `EntityPage.tsx.mustache` (react-tailwind-crud, react-menora-digital-crud) each carried the same ~520 lines of logic — state, URL and saved settings, loading, the form drawer, delete, inline edit, bulk actions, export, import, the board's moves — so every list feature was written twice. That logic is now one per-entity template, `EntityList.tsx.mustache` → `src/pages/<entity>/model/use<Entity>List.tsx` (the Menora set borrows it through `sourceSet`). It exports `use<Entity>List(columns, props)`, the page's props type (`<Entity>PageProps`), its column type (`<Entity>Column`, with `key` when a list page picks columns) and `ListAction`. Each set's `EntityPage` keeps its imports, its `columns` (the Menora ones carry `numeric`/`chip`), the Menora `VIEW_TABS`, and the JSX, which reads `list.<x>` (what the hook returns — every name under the gate it is defined under) and `props.<x>`; `const selectedRow = list.selectedRow` is taken once so the side pane's callbacks keep it narrowed. **A list feature's logic goes in the hook; only what it looks like goes in the two pages.** The hook is a `.tsx` because `inline()` builds `InlineCell`s.
  - Generated output: every fullstack frontend gains one file per entity and its page shrinks (61 golden cases re-pinned: only `pages/<e>/ui/<E>Page.tsx` changed and `pages/<e>/model/use<E>List.tsx` added). An existing project's next *Download update* carries that as a large hunk.
  - Tests read a list page through `ListPages.of(files, pagePath)` — the page followed by its hook — so an assertion about what the page does finds it in either.
  - Built for real: eleven `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests` (install, type check, `eslint --max-warnings 0`, build; both sets, layouts and the classic shell, side pane, workflow board, locking, inline edit, list presentation, saved views, composition, CSV import, the planning layout, and the generated Vitest tests including the list page render test) passed on 2026-11-18.

### Small ones (2026-11)

- *Compatibility rules hold on the server (`config/BackendCompatibility`).* The backend catalog's REQUIRES / CONFLICTS rows were shown by the UI and ignored by every backend endpoint, so `curl …/starter.zip?dependencies=web,webflux` generated a project that does not start. Now a dependency another REQUIRES is added (when the catalog offers it for the request's Boot version; requirements chain), and two that CONFLICT are a 400 `{error, detail, path: "dependencies"}` naming both and the rule's reason — and, when one of them was only added by a requirement, which dependency required it ("Spring Web ('web') is required by Spring Security ('security')"). Dropping one of a conflicting pair would generate something other than what was asked for, so it is refused, unlike the frontend resolver (`FrontendCompatibilityResolver`), which fixes a selection silently. RECOMMENDS changes nothing. Where it is applied:
  - `ProjectRequestFactory.create` — the wizard, fullstack and patch endpoints. `createModule` takes a module's list as it is: a multi-module project applies the rules once, in `MultiModuleController.assemble`, to the selection every module gets (a module's own template dependencies are the catalog's).
  - The endpoints the framework binds from query parameters (`/starter.zip`, `.tgz`, `/pom.xml`, `/build.gradle`, `/starter.preview`): `InitializrWebConfiguration` rewrites `dependencies` with what they require, and marks a conflict on the request; `BackendCompatibilityWebConfig`'s interceptor throws it before the handler, so `GlobalExceptionHandler` words the 400 and the generation audit records it like any refused request (a filter answering by itself would have bypassed both).
  - Consequence: a request the catalog's rules forbid now fails where it used to generate — e.g. `webflux,security`, since the catalog says security REQUIRES web, which CONFLICTS with webflux. The rules are admin-editable rows. No golden manifest moved. Pinned by `BackendCompatibilityTests`.
- *Team presets on the Backend tab* (`config/BackendPresetController`, `/metadata/backend/presets`, table `initializer_backend_preset`, `V33`). The Backend tab's presets were this browser's only; a team preset is the tab's whole state — form, dependencies, sub-options, modules, wizard documents — kept on the server for everyone, with the Fullstack tab's team-model rules: open reads and writes, names unique ignoring case (409), the snapshot a JSON object with `form` and `selected` kept verbatim (≤ 1 MB), `createdBy` from the SSO header, not part of admin export/import. The list carries `coordinates` (`groupId:artifactId`), `dependencyCount` and `moduleCount` (0 unless multi-module is on). Pinned by `BackendPresetControllerTests`; the UI side is in `ui/CLAUDE.md` (*Small ones*). The compatibility 400 and a preset's save and list were also tried on the jar over a copy of a real catalog database.
- *`tracing` catalog dependency* (Observability): `micrometer-tracing-bridge-otel` plus an `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp` build row (both Boot-managed), a REQUIRES rule on `actuator` (Actuator's observability auto-configuration sets tracing up), `management.tracing.sampling.probability: ${TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY:1.0}` merged into `application.yaml`, and — since every backend logs through log4j2's JSON layout, which names its MDC keys — `traceId`/`spanId` in `logFormat.json` under `{{#hasTracing}}` (`detailedLogFormat.json` writes every MDC key already). No OTLP endpoint is configured: on Boot 3.2+ the exporter starts only once `MANAGEMENT_OTLP_TRACING_ENDPOINT` is set, so a laptop without a collector logs no export errors. Pinned by `TracingDependencyTests`. **Not built for real**: the bridge is not in the offline repository. **Existing databases**: entries and rules are seeded once, so `V34__tracing_dependency.sql` adds the entry and its rule (V29's pattern: it finds the Observability group on an upgraded database and nothing on a fresh one, where the seeder adds them). The file and build rows (and the changed `logFormat.json`) come with the catalog sync — on a database's first start with it they are reported, and `POST /admin/catalog-sync/apply` takes them. Tried on a copy of a real catalog: V28–V34 applied, `tracing` offered with its rule, and after applying the three rows a generated project had the bridge, the exporter, Actuator, the sampling setting and `traceId` in its log layout.
- *Not done from item 25*: re-enabling `MultiModuleHappyPathIT` (in the `e2e` repo) and the stale docs (`README.md`'s "10 tables" and audit section, the bundle budget figure in `ui/CLAUDE.md`); static analysis (Checkstyle/SpotBugs) needs an "add plugin" build-customization type and plugins the offline repository does not have.

### Attachments (2026-11)

Generated fullstack backends with a FILE or IMAGE field: where the bytes go, who may read a file, and what happens to uploads nobody keeps. Gated on `hasAttachments` as before; both backend sets (the Lombok set borrows every file). The deliberate changes to generated bytes are the attachment package, the services of entities with a file field, `ApiExceptionHandler`, `DemoDataLoader` and the README — the Approvals and Inventory golden manifests, re-pinned; no frontend file moved.

- *One service, three storages.* `attachment/Attachments` is what the application talks to (store, describe, load, delete, the checks, the sweep); the services and `DemoDataLoader` take it instead of the storage. A file's row (`app_attachment`: name, type, size, `created_at`, and new `created_by` — the uploader) is always kept; `AttachmentStorage` is now the **bytes** only (`put`/`get`/`remove`), one of three picked by `app.attachments.storage`: `database` (default, `DbAttachmentStorage`, the row's `content` column — now nullable), `filesystem` (`FileSystemAttachmentStorage`, `<app.attachments.directory>/<first two chars>/<uuid>`, written to a temp file and moved into place) and `s3` (`S3AttachmentStorage`). Each is `@ConditionalOnProperty`; a team's own implementation marked `@Primary` still wins. Bytes are put inside the transaction that adds the row (a failure rolls the row back) and removed **after commit** (`TransactionSynchronization`), so a rolled-back delete keeps them. Settings are `attachment/AttachmentProperties` (`@ConfigurationProperties("app.attachments")`: `max-size`, `storage`, `directory`, `keep-unattached`, `s3.*`), registered by `AttachmentConfig`, which also sizes multipart and turns on `@EnableScheduling`. Changing the storage does not move files already stored.
- *S3 without an SDK.* `S3AttachmentStorage` speaks the S3 REST API itself — PUT, GET and DELETE of an object, signed with AWS Signature V4 (`x-amz-content-sha256`, `x-amz-date`, `host` signed) over `java.net.http.HttpClient` (HTTP/1.1, so the Host header it signs is the one the JDK sends) — because no AWS SDK or MinIO client is in the offline repository and a project should not need Artifactory to carry one. Path-style by default (MinIO); `path-style: false` for AWS. The bucket must exist. Missing settings fail at start, naming the property.
- *A file belongs to the record that holds it.* Nothing records an owner: `Attachments` asks each column that can hold attachment ids (`FeatureContext.putAttachments` → `attachmentOwners`, one native `select count(*) from <tableRef> where <column> = :id` per FILE/IMAGE field, names as the entity's own `@SQLDelete`/`@Formula` spell them). Native SQL, so a **soft-deleted** row still holds its file (a restore brings it back). Backend render path only.
