- *What a record may take* (`requireAttachable`, called by the generated service on create, on an update that changes the field, on a transition that `requires` a file field — `requires[].isAttachment` — and per row of a CSV import): a file that exists, that no record holds, and — with ldap-auth — that the caller uploaded. Otherwise `attachment/AttachmentException`, answered as a 400 whose `errors` name the field ("The file is no longer there: upload it again", "This file belongs to another record: upload a copy of it", "Upload the file yourself to attach it"). Without that check, pointing a record you may write at somebody else's file id would have handed it to your entity's readers. The replaced file is deleted as before.
- *Who may read a file* (ldap-auth only; `requireReadable` on `GET /api/attachments/{id}` and `/info`, after the `SIGNED_IN` check): a file on a record needs that record's entity read level (403 otherwise); a file on no record yet is its uploader's (404 for anyone else). Without ldap-auth nothing changes — the project knows no users. Downloads now say `Cache-Control: private, max-age=300`.
- *The clean-up.* `Attachments.sweep` (`@Scheduled`, hourly, first after five minutes) runs `removeUnattached()`: the uploads older than `app.attachments.keep-unattached` (24h; `0` keeps them) that no column holds — one native anti-join (`unattachedSqlJava`), 200 at a time, at most 50 rounds — deleted row first, bytes after commit. Several instances may sweep at once (`forget` answers 0 for a row another took). This also catches files a transition replaced. `app_attachment` gains an index on `created_at` (entity and Flyway baseline).
- *Generated README:* a section "Uploaded files" (`FullstackRootFiles.attachmentsReadme`) — the three storages with their environment variables and the volume for a container, who may read a file, the clean-up.
- Pinned by `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests.fullstackEndpoint_fileAndImageFields`, `FullstackMigrationTests.theGeneratorsOwnTablesAreInTheMigration` and the golden manifests. Built and driven for real by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.attachmentsFollowTheirRecordsOverTheRealApi` (`-Dsmoke.be=true`): a backend with ldap-auth-rest whose `AttachmentAccessTests` check the read levels, the uploader rule, the three refusals, a soft-deleted record keeping its file, a replaced file gone and the sweep, and whose `AttachmentFolderTests` run the `filesystem` storage and check the bytes on disk come and go — with the project's own API tests (which upload and attach through the same checks).
- **Not run against a real object store**: Docker Hub and quay.io both refused the MinIO image on this machine (an account policy; library images pull), and no S3 mock is in the offline repository. The S3 class is compiled by every smoke build, and its signing — key derivation, canonical request, string to sign — reproduces AWS's published Signature V4 example (`GET /test.txt`, signature `f0e8bdb8…`). Try it against MinIO before relying on it.
- *Not done:* virus scanning, versions of a file, moving files between storages. (The index on FILE/IMAGE columns and an update migrating `app_attachment` came in Round 13, phase 4; thumbnails in phase 5; several files in one field in phase 6.)

### Found while rewriting the Guide (2026-11)

- *Two catalog entries named artifacts that do not exist.* An entry with no Maven coordinates that is not file-only is a Spring Boot starter to the framework: it writes `spring-boot-starter-<depId>`. `mongodb` gave `spring-boot-starter-mongodb` and `mail-sampler` `spring-boot-starter-mail-sampler`, so neither project built. `mongodb` now names `spring-boot-starter-data-mongodb`; `mail-sampler` is file-only (its build customization already adds `spring-boot-starter-mail`). `catalog/dependencies.json` for a fresh database, `V35` for an existing one (only where the entry still has no coordinates and is still a starter; native SQL, so History does not show it). Pinned by `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.mongodbAndMailSamplerNameArtifactsThatExist` and `catalogEntriesWithoutCoordinatesAreRealBootStarters`, which fails for a new coordinate-less entry whose id is not on its list of real starters.
- *SQL wizard entities and Lombok.* `@Data` put every association into `equals`, `hashCode` and `toString`, which load the lazy row (a `LazyInitializationException` outside a transaction, recursion between two entities pointing at each other). Every association the wizard writes — many-to-one, the read-only one beside a key column, the composite one — now carries `@EqualsAndHashCode.Exclude` and `@ToString.Exclude` (`SqlEntityGenerator.lombokExcluded`), as the fullstack Lombok set does. Eight wizard golden manifests moved, in their entity files only.
- `docs/MUSTACHE_GUIDE.md` follows `ScaffoldOption` (every option, which render path reads it, `secured` retired) and documents `dsExtraPackages`.

## Round 13 (2026-11)

The plan is `~/.claude/plans/stay-on-the-same-jolly-kahan.md` (ten phases).

**Phase 1 — wizard internals.** No generated byte changed by the first half; the second widened what is read, deliberately.
- *Each document read and generated once per request (`gen/WizardMemo`).* A wizard request used to read its documents two or three times: `WizardStarterController` generated each one to check it (a bad one is a 400 with nothing on disk), the contributor generated it again, and a build customizer read it a third time (`avroImports`, `resolveWsdlFileNames`). The check now generates with what the project's own generation will use — the resolved request's package and starters, the options the contexts carry (`validateDocuments`, after `toWebRequest`; `validateShape` — keys and sizes — still comes first) — and the four generators keep their results per request: `OpenApiCodeGenerator.generate`, `SoapCodeGenerator.generate` and its parsed services (`servicesOf`), `EventsCodeGenerator.generate` and its parsed document (`read`), `SqlEntityGenerator.generate`. A key is every input, so a caller whose inputs differ (the framework cleaning a package name) generates its own; nothing is kept outside a request (`WizardMemo.open`/`close`, closed with the contexts in `clearAllContexts` and the `InitializrWebConfiguration` backstop), and a computation that throws is not kept. **The memo's values are shared: read them, never modify them.** The SQL check now generates instead of only listing tables, so a script that parses but cannot be generated is a 400 too. Pinned by `WizardDocumentsReadOnceTests` (one computation of each kind for a request with all four wizards — `WizardMemo.lastComputed()`) and the unchanged wizard golden manifests.
- *One reading of the constraints (`sql/SqlConstraintReader`).* `ALTER TABLE … ADD PRIMARY KEY | FOREIGN KEY | UNIQUE`, inline `REFERENCES`, `CHECK (col IN …)` and the rule for which values make an enum (`enumConstants`: every value already a Java identifier, no keyword, no repeat) moved out of `SqlEntityGenerator`, and the fullstack DDL import (`SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter`) reads the parsed `TableModel` instead of its own regex. New for both: `ALTER TABLE … ADD [CONSTRAINT x] CHECK (col IN …)`, and `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX … ON t (cols)` as a unique constraint (not a partial one — `WHERE …` allows duplicates outside it — nor one over an expression; a plain `CREATE INDEX` is still skipped).
- *Two import changes.* A UNIQUE over several columns becomes the entity's `uniqueKeys` (over the fields and many-to-one links the columns became; one taking in a key column or a TEXT is left out), carried to the editor as `EntityWire.uniqueKeys` (absent when none). And the enum rule is the wizard's: the values become constants **as the database holds them** (`'open'` → `open`); a value that is not a Java name leaves the column a string. The import used to upper-case them (`OPEN`), which the table's own CHECK then refused on every save. The live-database import goes through the same path.
- The wizard fixture `library-postgresql.sql` declares a CHECK by ALTER, a unique index and a partial one; the seven SQL golden cases were re-pinned (the `reviews` entity, DTO and service, a new `ReviewsVerdict` enum, the model file and the baseline). `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.sqlWizard*` build it. Pinned by `SqlEntityGeneratorTest.anAlterTableCheckAndAUniqueIndexAreRead` and `SqlToEntityDefinitionConverterTest.constraintsDeclaredAfterTheTableAreRead` / `checkInConstraintsBecomeEnumsOfTheValuesTheDatabaseHolds`.

**Phase 2 — the catalog sync has a screen** (admin *Catalog sync*, see `ui/CLAUDE.md`), over the existing `/admin/catalog-sync` API. And a file contribution's `targetPath` is checked when the row is saved (`POST`/`PUT /admin/file-contributions`) and when an export is imported, not only when a project is generated: `FileContributionContentValidator.targetPathProblem` puts sample values in for the placeholders and runs `GeneratedProjectFiles.resolveInside` — a path that leaves the project is a 400 (DELETE rows too: the delete pass resolves the same way). With the placeholders' sample values a path can pass here and still fail for a short package; generation keeps its own check. Rows the catalog sync applies are the release's and are not checked. Pinned by `AdminApiIntegrationTests.fileContributionLeadingOutOfTheProjectReturns400`.

**Phase 3 — generated snapshots: an update carries the generator's fixes** (`config/GeneratedSnapshots`, `observability/SnapshotStore`, `V36`).
- *Why.* An update generated both sides with the generator of the day, so its patch was the model's change only. Storing old templates would have carried few fixes: 102 of the 117 commits that touched `templates/fullstack` changed fullstack Java too.
- *The id.* Every project's `menora-model.json` ends with `generator.snapshot`: the SHA-256 of every other file (path, NUL, bytes, NUL, paths in order). `GeneratedSnapshots.stamp(root)` computes it once the tree is complete, rewrites the model file (`withSnapshot`: the key last in the `generator` block, pretty JSON, LF), and hands id and tree to `AuditDetails.snapshot`. It is called by the wizard controller (zip, preview, and `generateProject`, which the backend update uses), by `MultiModuleController.assemble` and at the end of `FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts`. The framework's `/starter.zip` is stamped by `ProjectPreviewConfig.StampingInvoker`: our own `ProjectGenerationController` bean (the framework's is `@ConditionalOnMissingBean`) over an invoker whose asset generator stamps after the last contributor. A project with no model file (a fullstack backend, a module) is not stamped. Content-addressed, so `/starter.zip` and the wizard endpoint without a document still give the same bytes, and two downloads of one project are kept once.
- *Header and storage.* `SnapshotHeaderAdvice` (a `ResponseBodyAdvice`, so the framework's zip too) sets `X-Snapshot` on every generated zip and, for a download or an update (not a preview — `GenerationAuditFilter.isGeneration`/`actionOf`), calls `SnapshotStore.keepCurrent` **before the body is written**: a project is kept before anyone holding it can ask for its update (the audit filter's `finally` ran after the client had the zip). Table `initializer_generated_snapshot` (id, kind, artifact, created/last used, file count, size, the tree zipped). One writer thread, a queue of 20 (`menora.snapshots.async`, default the audit's setting); a full queue drops the snapshot and its update falls back. `find` marks a row used and waits up to 2 s for a pending write. Retention `menora.snapshots.retention-days` (730) from last use, nightly at 03:45 (`menora.snapshots.retention-cron`); `menora.snapshots.enabled=false` turns it all off.
- *Updates.* `FullstackPatchRequest.baseSnapshot` / `BackendPatchRequest.baseSnapshot` (the backend also reads `generator.snapshot` from a whole model file it is sent). When the store has it, the base tree is the kept files; the base side is still generated now — for the migration's schema, for the same 400 when the old model no longer generates, and for its model file, which goes into the base tree **with the base's snapshot id** (otherwise identical models would leave the project's model file naming the old files, and the next update would start from them again). The update's result is stamped too (the fullstack one after `carryBaselineOver`): its `files/menora-model.json` and `X-Snapshot` name the project once applied, and an update request keeps it. `X-Update-Base: snapshot | regenerated` (`FullstackStarterController.UPDATE_BASE_HEADER`); `ProjectUpdateBuilder.Info.fromSnapshot` words `UPDATE.md` ("## Where it starts" instead of "What this is not").
- *Limits.* Only projects downloaded after this change have a snapshot, and only while it is kept; `/starter.preview` (the GET preview) is not stamped. The store grows by a compressed project per distinct download. A kept snapshot is trusted as it is: nothing checks its files still hash to its id.
- The golden suites mask `generator.snapshot` like the other hashes; every case's model file was re-pinned for the new key and nothing else moved. Pinned by `GeneratedSnapshotTests` (each kind's id is its files' and the header's, a download is kept and a preview is not, a backend and a fullstack update start from changed kept files and say so, an unknown id regenerates, retention) and `BackendPatchTests` (a model file on disk starts from its snapshot; without one, the catalog-hash wording).

**Phase 4 — the generator's own tables have versions** (`fullstack/schema/SchemaBuilder.CURRENT_VERSION`, now 3).
- *Why.* An update's migration compares two schemas built now, so a change to a table the generator itself adds (`app_attachment` gained `created_by`, a nullable `content` and an index in the Attachments round) never reached a project generated before it.
- *Versions.* 1 — before the Attachments round; 2 — the Attachments round; 3 — this round: an index on every FILE/IMAGE column (`ixa_<table>_<column>`, `ATTACHMENT_INDEX`), since `Attachments.ownersOf` counts rows by those columns on every read check. `SchemaBuilder.asOf(schema, version)` takes a schema built by this release back to an older version (the attachments table of that version, no `ixa_` indexes before 3; `null` reads as 1). **A change to a generator table (`app_*`) or to an index the generator adds beside an entity's columns is a new version**: bump `CURRENT_VERSION`, teach `asOf` the older shape, and pin it in `SchemaDiffTest`.
- *Where the version travels.* The fullstack `menora-model.json` writes `generator.schemaVersion` (beside `templatesHash`); the zip and the update answer `X-Schema-Version`; `FullstackPatchRequest.baseSchemaVersion` sends the project's back. `SchemaMigrations.carryBaselineOver(…, baseVersion)` diffs from `asOf(base, baseVersion)`, so the migration adds what the project's version lacks — as live statements when the version is known. **Unknown** (a model file from before versions, or a client that does not send it): read as version 1, and what would run on an `app_*` table goes "To review" in one note (`SchemaDiff.between(…, unsure)`, `SchemaBuilder.isGeneratorTable`) — that project's table may be version 1 or 2. Indexes on entity tables still run: no project from before had them.
- Plain projects have no generator tables; their model file carries no version.
- Golden: every fullstack model file moved (the new key); no golden case uses Flyway, so no baseline moved. Pinned by `SchemaDiffTest.anOlderVersionOfTheGeneratorsTablesIsBroughtUp` / `anUnknownVersionPutsTheGeneratorsTablesUpForReview` and `FullstackMigrationTests.anUpdateBringsTheGeneratorsOwnTablesUpFromTheVersionTheProjectHas`.

**Phase 5 — thumbnails** (generated apps with an IMAGE or FILE field).
- *Backend* (`spring-jpa-crud`, borrowed by the Lombok set): `attachment/Thumbnails.java` (new, gated `hasAttachments`) makes a picture's small version with the JDK's ImageIO — PNG, JPEG and GIF (the JDK reads no WebP), longest side 320 px, a JPEG stays a JPEG and the rest become PNG. The size is read from the header **before decoding**, and a picture over 40 megapixels is never decoded (a small file can describe an enormous picture). `GET /api/attachments/{id}/thumbnail` has the file's read check; `Attachments.thumbnail` makes it on the first request and keeps it in `app_attachment.thumbnail` (nullable, in the database whatever the storage — schema version 3), with empty bytes as "none can be made", so the original is not decoded again; without one the endpoint answers the file itself (`download`). Existing files get theirs on first view.
- *Frontend:* `format.ts` `attachmentUrl(id, thumbnail = false)`; a table cell, a card (board, inbox), the details' picture, the gallery tiles and the form's preview draw the thumbnail (`FieldViewModel.displayTs` IMAGE, `GalleryView.tsx`, `AttachmentField.tsx`), with `loading="lazy"` in cells and cards; the links still open the original.
- Golden: the attachment files of every case with files, `Thumbnails.java` added there, `format.ts` everywhere, and Inventory's picture cells, details and gallery. Pinned by `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests.fullstackEndpoint_fileAndImageFields`, `FullstackRichFieldsTests` and, built and driven, `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.attachmentsFollowTheirRecordsOverTheRealApi` (`aPictureHasASmallVersionAndOtherFilesAreServedAsTheyAre`: a 1200×800 PNG's thumbnail is 320×213, made once then kept, behind the same read check; a text file comes back as it is).

**Phase 6 — several files in one field** (`maxFiles` on a FILE or IMAGE field).
- *Wire and rules.* `FieldDefinitionDto.maxFiles` (1–`FullstackRequestValidator.MAX_FILES`, 50; left out of the JSON when absent) → `FieldDefinition.maxFiles` (a 25th component; `multiple()`). A 400 on any other type, outside the range, with `showWhen` (a save would clear a list to null), in a unique key, or in a workflow move's `requires`. A composed child takes no file field at all (as before). Schema `limits.maxFiles`; `AttachmentsField`, `CollectionTable`, `ElementCollection`, `OrderColumn` are reserved names.
- *Storage.* The list is the entity's `@ElementCollection(fetch = EAGER) @CollectionTable("<entity>_<field>", owner_id) @OrderColumn("file_order") @BatchSize(100) List<AttachedFile>` — `attachment/AttachedFile` (new, gated `hasMultiFiles`) is an `@Embeddable` of the file's id (`attachment_id`) and its name (`file_name`), **copied when the file is attached** (a file is never renamed), so a list needs no per-database aggregate to be shown. `SchemaNames.filesTableOf` names the table like a many-to-many's join table; `SchemaBuilder` writes it (owner and order as the key, a foreign key to the owner, an `ixa_` index on `attachment_id`) and no column on the entity's own table. The read check and the clean-up count the files tables like a column (`FeatureContext.putAttachments`).
- *Service.* `Attachments.attachAll(files, kept, field, max, required)` — at most `max`, at least one when required, none twice, a file not already on the record must be one it may take (`requireAttachable`), each named from `app_attachment` whatever the client sent — on create, update and the CSV import; `detachRemoved` deletes what an update left out; a delete deletes every file. **No list on an update keeps the files**, so a multi-file field carries no `@NotNull` on the DTO (the service checks "at least one"). `attachmentFields` are the single-file fields now, `multiFileFields` the others; `hasAttachmentFields` is either.
- *Shown.* `FieldViewModel.multiFileTs`: a cell the names as links (a picture: the first thumbnail and "+n"), a card the names or the first picture, the details every file (pictures as thumbnails opening their originals) — each reading `(v ?? [])`. CSV and Excel exports join the names with "; ". A gallery takes a single-picture field only. Rules' `isEmpty` counts an empty list as empty (both runtimes).
- *Form.* `shared/ui/AttachmentsField.tsx` (new, both sets): the files, each removable, more picked or dropped several at a time, "n / max". `validate.ts` checks the most and, when required, at least one (`maxFiles` string, gated `hasMultiFiles`). The demo rows give a required list one sample file (`DemoDataLoader.sampleFiles`); the API tests send one by id.
- *Example.* Approvals' claim takes `receipts` (up to 5) instead of one `receipt`.
- Golden: Approvals' backend and frontend, and the Projects rule runtime (`isEmpty`). Pinned by `FullstackMultiFileTests`, built and driven by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.severalFilesInAFieldOverTheRealApi` (Flyway baseline under `validate`; files in order and named, a file another record holds refused, a removed one gone, no list keeps them, the clean-up leaves them, too many and none refused) and `liveApiOfAWorkflowBackendBehaves`, and built by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*Approvals*` (both sets: lint, tsc, build).
- *Not done:* reordering files by drag in the form (the order is the upload order), a size total per field; the editor's canvas draws such a field like a single file.

**Phase 7 — rules, show-when and computed fields on composed rows.** `checkComposition` no longer refuses a child with row logic (nor does the editor's `rules.ts`).
- *Backend.* A composition's view carries `childHasRules` (`RelationViews`, from `EntityDefinition.hasRowLogic`). The parent's service then has `check<List>Rows(rows)`: each row through `<Child>Rules.apply` — hidden fields cleared, computed ones computed, then the checks — with every message keyed `<list>[i].<field>` in one `RuleViolationException` (the 400's `errors`, where the rows editor already looks). It runs on a create after the rows are adopted and on an update after they are merged, so a kept row is recomputed from its new values. Before the parent's own rules. A row saved on its own runs them as before.
- *Frontend.* `validate<Parent>` already ran `validate<Child>(row, true)`, which derives and checks. The rows editor (`EntityRows.tsx.mustache`, both sets) draws a computed cell read-only as it will be saved (`rowComputedTs`, `FeatureContext.computedTs(entity, f, "row")`) and a show-when cell as a dash while its condition does not hold (`rowShowWhenTs`); `rowsUseRules` gates the `rule` import. A computed column is never marked required.
- *Generated tests* are unchanged: a parent's are written without rows, and a composed child's create through its own endpoint with `RuleRows`' values, as before.
- *Example.* Orders' lines: `lineTotal = quantity * unitPrice` (computed) and the rule `unitPrice > 0` ("A unit price cannot be zero" — `min: 0` allows zero; the plan's `quantity > 0` would have repeated `min: 1`).
- Golden: the eight Orders cases (the line's files, the order's service, and the rules runtime Orders now ships). Pinned by `FullstackCompositionTests.theParentsSaveRunsTheRowsOwnRules`; driven by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.liveApiOfAComposedParentBehaves` (`aRowIsComputedAndCheckedByItsOwnRulesInItsParentsSave`: a client's `lineTotal` overwritten, a zero price refused at `lines[1].unitPrice` on create and on update, a kept row recomputed); built by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*Composition*`.
- *Not done:* a parent's rule that reads its rows (a sum over the lines); the editor's canvas still does not draw the rows editor.

**Phase 8 — a rule that reads a linked row** (`amount <= costCenter.budget`, in rules, show-when conditions and formulas alike).
- *Language.* `name.name` is one step through a MANY_TO_ONE to a field of the row it points at: the lexer reads it as one name (two steps are refused, and `x.y(...)` is no function), kept as one `Expr.Ref` (`isPath()`). `ExprCheck` resolves it through the entity's **links** and reports it in `Checked.paths` (`Path(relation, target, field)`), apart from `refs`, which stays the row's own fields. A linked field must be a short value: not long text, Markdown, a file or a UUID. An enum literal is checked against the linked field's values.
- *Model.* `EntityDefinition.links` (`Map<relation, LinkedEntity(name, fields)>`, a 20th component; the 19-argument constructor passes none). The validator builds it in pass 2 from the pass-1 fields, so `checkDerivations` and `parseRules` now run there (after the relations), with it. Every generation-time check reads the entity (`ExprCheck.check(text, entity, type)`).
- *Backend.* `ExprCode` writes a null-safe read (`(row.getCostCenter() == null ? null : row.getCostCenter().getBudget())`). The service loads the rows the rules read before they run — `withLinkedRows` (`entityManager.find` for each link in `linkedReads`), on a create, an import's rows and an update (a row from a request links by id alone, so a rule over a stub would always hold). A move and a bulk change read the stored row's links, which load lazily inside the transaction. `usesEntityManager` covers `readsLinks`.
- *Frontend.* `shared/api/linked.ts` (gated `hasLinkedRules`, both sets): `useLinkedRows([[path, id]])` in the form fetches each linked row once when its link is picked; `linked.value(path, id, field)` is what the TS expressions read (`validate.ts`, the form, a composed child's rows editor). Until a row arrives — or when the user may not read it — the value is unknown, the check holds, and the server decides. `rules.ts` is unchanged, so no other project moved.
- *Generated tests.* `RuleRows` treats a linked field like a field of the row: it picks a value (never empty) the rules hold for, and `ApiTestSupport` creates the parent with it (`create("CostCenter", parent -> { parent.put("budget", …); })`, a gated overload). A linked field that is a key, unique, patterned or computed leaves the entity's tests to the team, naming the rule.
- *Example.* Approvals: `when status == 'SUBMITTED': amount <= costCenter.budget` ("Over the cost center's budget"); `CostCenter.budget` gets `defaultValue: 10000` and a help text, since the demo rows (seeded from the default) were 1 to 8 and would have refused every realistic claim.
- Golden: the nine Approvals cases. Pinned by `ExprTest.aLinkedRowsFieldIsReadOneStepAway`, `FullstackFieldModelTests.aRuleReadsTheRowALinkPointsAt` / `whatALinkedReadCannotBeIsRefusedWhereItIs`; driven by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.liveApiOfAWorkflowBackendBehaves` (`aSubmittedClaimStaysWithinItsCostCentersBudget`: over budget refused on submit, within it moves, a save naming another center by id checked against that center) with its generated `ExpenseClaimApiTest` (the parent made with the solved budget); built by `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend*Approvals*`.
- *Limits.* One step; a parent's value the child's rule reads is not re-checked when the parent changes (lowering a budget leaves submitted claims as they are). The form's copy of a linked row is fetched once per session. If the parent's own rules read the same field the child's rule does, the solver does not know them, and the generated test may need adjusting.

**Phase 9 — OpenAPI wizard: OAuth2 that works** (`openapi/OpenApiCodeGenerator`, `SecurityUse`, `OperationModel`).
- *Declared.* An OAuth2 scheme's flows go to springdoc: `flows = @OAuthFlows(clientCredentials = @OAuthFlow(tokenUrl = …, scopes = {@OAuthScope(…)}), …)` for each of implicit, password, client credentials and authorization code (`oauthFlows`), so Swagger UI's *Authorize* works. OpenID Connect already had its URL.
- *A client-credentials flow* is `SecurityUse.Kind.OAUTH2_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS` (with the flow's `tokenUrl` and `scopes`). `<client>/OpenApiOAuth2Tokens` (new, `templates/wizard/openapi/OAuth2Tokens.java.mustache`, only when such a scheme is used): posts `grant_type=client_credentials` (and the scopes) to the token endpoint, the client authenticated by HTTP basic or — `client-authentication: post` — in the body; keeps each scheme's token until 30 s before `expires_in` (5 minutes when the answer has none), one fetch at a time per scheme; and, registered as a `ClientHttpRequestInterceptor` on the clients' `RestTemplate` (`additionalInterceptors`) or `RestClient` (`requestInterceptor`), answers a 401 to a request that carried one of its tokens by forgetting it and sending the request once more with a new one. **No new dependency**: it is a plain `RestTemplate` from spring-web (`tokenEndpoint()`, which a test binds a mock server to), so it builds offline on Boot 3.2 and 3.5.
- *Properties* `<base>.security.<scheme>.client-id | client-secret | scopes | token-url | client-authentication` (the last three default to the document's scopes, its token URL and `basic`, so `application.yaml` lists only the id, the secret and `token`). Without a client id the configured `token` is sent as before, so an existing configuration keeps working. `OpenApiCredentials.<scheme>()` returns the fetched token or the configured one; `<scheme>Configured()` says whether either is set.
- *Every requirement.* `OperationModel.clientAlternatives()` is each requirement the client can meet, up to the first that asks for nothing; with more than one, the client method declares `boolean auth0 = …; boolean auth1 = !auth0 && …;` and sends the first alternative whose credentials are all configured (`configuredExpr`); with one, the code is what it was. `clientSecurity()` is now every scheme the client may send (the credentials it reads).
- Golden: `openapi__secure-forms__classes` and `__interfaces` only. Pinned by `OpenApiInterfacesTest`; built and driven by `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.openApiWizardInterfacesFormsAndSecurityWork` (RestClient, Boot 3.2 and 3.5) and `openApiWizardSecureFormsAsClassesCompile` (RestTemplate), both with `OAuth2Tests`: one token fetch (basic client auth, the scope) for two calls, the bearer sent, a 401 answered with a new token and one repeat.
- *Not done:* the authorization-code, password and implicit flows still send a configured token; scopes per operation (`oauth: [read]`) are not asked for separately — the client asks for the flow's scopes (or the `scopes` property); the server side still enforces nothing.

**Phase 10 — events wizard: Confluent schema registry, keys, headers** (`events/`, `templates/wizard/events/`). A document and options that use none of it keep their bytes (the three events golden cases did not move).
- *What the reader takes* (`AsyncApiReader.addMessage`): a message's **traits** first (each trait's fields the message does not set, and its `headers` properties the message's own do not name; a 2.x `[trait, …]` pair's first element); its `headers` (an object schema, 3.x's `{schemaFormat, schema}` unwrapped) → `EventMessage.headers`; a `correlationId` at `$message.header#/x` → `correlationHeader` (one in the payload is not a header and is left alone); `bindings.kafka.key` → `key`.
- *Keys.* A key schema `{type: string, format: uuid}` makes the producer take and the listener hand over a `java.util.UUID` (sent as its text); any other key stays `String` (an enum or a number too — the plan's enum key type was left out). `/detect-channels` says `key: uuid`.
- *Headers* (`EventsCodeGenerator.Extras`). A message with headers or a correlation id gets `<payload package>/<Message>Headers.java` (`Headers.java.mustache`): a record of the headers, typed (`integer` → `Integer`/`Long`, `number` → `BigDecimal`, `boolean`, a `uuid` string → `UUID`, else `String`), the correlation header added as a `String` when the headers do not declare it. The producer keeps `send(key, payload)` (now `send(key, payload, null)`) and gains `send(key, payload, headers)`, which builds a `ProducerRecord` and writes each header that is set as UTF-8 text — a correlation id left empty is a new UUID. A listener of a channel with one kind of message reads them back (`headersOf(record)`, parsed by type) and calls `handle(key, payload, headers)`; a channel with several kinds hands over no headers (which kind's would they be?). Tests check the record and the generated correlation id. `/detect-channels` lists the header names.
- *Confluent schema registry* (`eventOptions.<dep>.schemaRegistry: confluent`, any case; anything else a 400; absent = the single-object encoding as before). For Avro channels: `AvroKafkaConfig` (`AvroRegistryKafkaConfig.java.mustache`) builds a `KafkaTemplate<String, SpecificRecord>` with `KafkaAvroSerializer` and a listener factory with `KafkaAvroDeserializer` + `specific.avro.reader`, from the project's own Kafka settings; producers send the record (`AvroRegistryProducer`), listeners receive the generated class (several kinds of message arrive as their own classes); tests write and read through an in-memory registry (`mock://`). `application.yaml` gets `app.events.schema-registry.url` (`SCHEMA_REGISTRY_URL`, default `http://localhost:8081`), `.auto-register` (`SCHEMA_REGISTRY_AUTO_REGISTER`, default false) and `.user-info` (`SCHEMA_REGISTRY_USER_INFO`, `user:password` for basic auth), which `AvroKafkaConfig` puts into both serializers' settings itself — **not `spring.kafka.properties`**: the catalog's own `KafkaConfig` builds the factories without them, and the first real build failed on `Missing required configuration "schema.registry.url"`. JSON channels are written as without it.
- *Build.* `io.confluent:kafka-avro-serializer` at `confluent.version`, by Boot line (`EventsCodeGenerator.confluentVersion`: 3.2 → 7.6.1, 3.3 → 7.7.1, 3.4 → 7.8.0, 3.5 → 7.9.0 — the release on the Kafka client that line manages; the project's own kafka-clients wins), from the `confluent` repository `menora.events.confluent-repository` (default `https://packages.confluent.io/maven/` — point it at the Artifactory remote).
- *Local run.* With the registry and Kafka, `compose.yaml` adds `schema-registry` (`confluentinc/cp-schema-registry:7.7.1` through the hub prefix, its store in the compose broker, port 8081) and the `compose` profile points the application at it with `app.events.schema-registry.auto-register: true` (`LocalRun.SCHEMA_REGISTRY`, a pseudo-dependency `LocalRunGenerationConfiguration` adds).
- *The drawer* (`EventsWizardDrawer.tsx`): an *Avro through* select (no registry | Confluent schema registry) beside the Avro note, saved as `EventsWizardEntry.schemaRegistry`, carried by `eventsBody`/`eventsOf`; each channel shows its UUID key and header names.
- Golden: two new cases over the new fixture `wizard-fixtures/orders-avro-registry-asyncapi.yaml` (`events__headers-keys`, `events__avro__confluent`); none moved. Pinned by `EventsHeadersAndRegistryTest` and `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.eventsWizardThroughTheSchemaRegistryAddsTheSerializerAndTheRegistryContainer`. **Built:** `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.eventsWizardJsonKeysAndHeadersCompileAndTravel` (offline: a JSON channel's headers out and typed back, a new correlation id, a UUID key) and — run once online on 2026-11-19, since the avro-maven-plugin and Confluent's jars are not in the offline repository — `eventsWizardKeysHeadersAndRegistryBackendCompilesAndPassesItsTests` (`-Dsmoke.be=true -Dsmoke.confluent=true`), both modes: compiled, every generated test passed (a record through the `mock://` registry) and the application context started on the registry settings. Those jars are now in the local `~/.m2`; Artifactory must carry them for anyone else.
- *Not done:* an enum or numeric key type; headers on a listener of several kinds of message; schema-registry basic auth beyond the properties a team adds; Apicurio or another registry; the registry's subject naming strategies (TopicNameStrategy, Confluent's default).

**Fullstack DDL and creating tables (2026-11).** A model's tables could only be had by generating a project with `flyway` and opening its `V1__init.sql`. Now the editor can export that script for any of the five databases, or create the tables on a database an admin set up as a schema source and allowed it for. No generated byte changed.

- *One schema path.* The project context `SchemaBuilder.build` reads used to be assembled inline in the contributor lambda; it is now `extension/fullstack/BackendScaffoldContext.build(Inputs, entities, optionsContext, departmentResolver)`, which the contributor calls and the new endpoints call too. `FullstackProjectAssembler.buildArtifacts` split its checks out into `resolve(body)` → `Resolved` (entities with their pages' implications, sets, locale, the backend request with its dependencies, ldap, shell user; the options context populated), so a model the generator refuses is refused by the new endpoints in the same words. `schemaOf(body)` = `resolve` → `BackendScaffoldContext.build` → `SchemaBuilder.build`. `FullstackDdlEndpointTests.theScriptIsTheProjectsBaselineForEveryExample` generates every example with `flyway` and compares the zip's `V1__init.sql` with the endpoint's `sql` byte for byte — the guard against the two drifting.
- *`POST /starter-fullstack.ddl[?dialect=D]`* (body: the fullstack request) → `{dialect, label, sql, tables, projectDialect, flyway}`. `sql` is `SchemaSql.baseline` — the V1 text. `D` is a `SchemaDialect` name or a dependency id (`SchemaDialect.ofDependency`); absent, the model's own database (`SchemaDialect.of`). Flyway is not needed. An unknown dialect is a 400 at `dialect`.
- *Writable sources.* `V37` adds `allow_create_tables` (default false) to `initializer_schema_source`; `SchemaSourceEntity.allowCreateTables`, the admin `SourceView` and `/metadata/live-schema/options` (`SourceSummary.createTables`) carry it. `LiveSchemaService.writableSource(name)` resolves **only a source**, enabled and allowing it (403 otherwise, 404 unknown) — a typed connection never writes. `withConnection(spec, readOnly, work)` and `LiveSchemaReader.connect(spec, readOnly)`: with `readOnly` false, `DialectSupport.url/properties(spec, false)` leave out PostgreSQL's `readOnly=true`, SQL Server's `applicationIntent=ReadOnly` and DB2's `access=read only`, and `setReadOnly(true)` is skipped. Same semaphore, timeouts and password masking as reads. The entity's dialect pattern admits `H2` for the tests' in-memory sources; `specOf` still refuses H2 unless `menora.live-schema.allow-h2`.
- *`POST /starter-fullstack.create-tables`* `{request, source, schema, check?}` → `{dialect, label, schema, sql, tables, statements, created, checked, transactional, projectDialect, flyway}` (`fullstack/schema/SchemaInstaller`):
  - The dialect is the **source's** (`SqlDialect.depId()` → `SchemaDialect.ofDependency`); the answer names the project's so the UI warns when they differ.
  - The schema must be one the source lists (ignoring case; 400 otherwise). `SchemaSql.inSchema(schema, "\"<listed name>\"")` puts every unqualified table, and the foreign keys to them, in it — quoted as the database stores it, so a mixed-case PostgreSQL schema and an upper-case Oracle one both resolve; SQL Server ignores `Connection.setSchema`, which is why the names are qualified rather than the session switched. Tables the model qualifies itself keep their schema.
  - `SchemaSql.statements(schema, dialect, existingSchemas)`: the baseline's statements without comments or semicolons (Oracle refuses one), `create schema if not exists` only for a model schema that is not there (H2, PostgreSQL), and on Oracle and DB2 an index name qualified with its table's schema (those make an index in the session's schema otherwise; PostgreSQL forbids a qualified one).
  - Clash check first: every target schema's tables and views (`LiveSchemaReader.tables`), compared ignoring case. Any of the model's there → **409** `{error: "Tables exist", detail, existing: ["SCHEMA.table"…]}` (`LiveSchemaException.existing`), nothing run. `check: true` stops after it and returns the SQL.
  - Run: PostgreSQL and SQL Server in one transaction, rolled back on a failure ("Nothing was created"). Oracle, DB2 and H2 commit each CREATE, so a failure drops the tables that run made, newest first (`cascade constraints` / `cascade`), and the 400 names any that would not go. Each statement has the 30 s timeout. A log line per call: check or run, source, schema, counts, the `userinfo` header. Not in the generation audit (it covers `.zip`/`.patch`/`.preview`).
- *UI*: see *Fullstack DDL and creating tables* in `ui/CLAUDE.md`. *Agent skill*: `mi tables ddl|create`.

*Run for real* (`CreateTablesDockerTests`, `-Dsmoke.docker=true`, one database per run with `-Dsmoke.db=POSTGRESQL|MSSQL|ORACLE`): PostgreSQL 16, SQL Server 2022 and Oracle Free 23 in Docker, through the endpoint and an admin source — every seeded example plus the field-types, many-to-many and one-to-many models, each in a schema of its own (42 tables per database), then a second run refused with the four tables listed, then a run that fails at its third CREATE (`pk_posts` already taken) rolled back on PostgreSQL and SQL Server and its two tables dropped again on Oracle. All three passed on 2026-11-19 (about 40 s, 50 s and 80 s). That is the first time the baseline's statements have run on those databases — CREATE only: Hibernate has not validated entities against them there.

*Limits.* DB2 has not run the statements (no container). The run checks that the statements are accepted, not that the tables are what Hibernate validates on that database. A table whose name is a keyword fails as it does in the baseline. The clash check looks at table names only — an index or constraint name already used elsewhere in the schema makes the run fail (and roll back / drop). A schema the model names on Oracle, SQL Server or DB2 must exist. Nothing is recorded in the target database (no Flyway history): a project with Flyway marks V1 as applied on its first start (`baseline-on-migrate`), the drawer says so. The source's user needs CREATE rights; the check does not test them.

Pinned by `FullstackDdlEndpointTests`, `liveschema/FullstackCreateTablesTests` (an H2 source, in `LiveSchemaIntegrationTests`' context), `SchemaSqlStatementsTest`, `DialectSupportWritableTest` and, opt-in, `liveschema/CreateTablesDockerTests`.

## Round 14 (2026-11)

Eleven follow-ups picked from the "Not done" notes above; the plan is `~/.claude/plans/i-want-you-to-giggly-wreath.md`.

**Phase 1 — the biggest classes, split by area.** No behaviour and no generated byte changed: both golden suites passed without a re-pin, and the suite runs the same 1,078 tests before and after. Members moved unchanged, as in *Structure (2026-11)*. The pieces of a static class static-import each other (`import static …PageChecks.*`), so no call site changed; a private member another piece names became package-private, one used only in its own piece stayed private.

- *`fullstack/FullstackPageValidator`* (2,323 → 615 lines) keeps the entry points (`validateAndConvert`, `applyPageImplications`, `validateNav`), the page loop, the page basics (roles, type, nav group and icon, `rejectForeignProps`) and **every constant `FullstackSchema` reads**. Beside it, package-private:

  | Class | Holds |
  |---|---|
  | `PageListRules` | a list's columns, sort, view, detail, page size, hidden actions (`auditApplies`, `columns`, `sort`, `listSpec`, …) |
  | `PagePresets` | preset filters and date ranges (`presetFilter`, `relationIds`, `parseDateRange`) |
  | `DashboardWidgetRules` | `widgets` and each widget kind, dashboard filters, `drillTo`, the aggregate and bucket parsers |
  | `ReportChartRules` | a report's charts |
  | `PageStructureRules` | tabs, wizard steps, header stats, tree, master-detail, child tabs, calendar, board, inbox, content, search |
  | `PageChecks` | `fieldOf`, `requireEntity`, `checkLength`, `trimToNull` |

- *`sql/SqlEntityGenerator`* (1,951 → 202 lines) keeps the bean and its public API (`generate`, `detectTableNames`, `parseTablesForImport`, `parseSelectForImport`, `SelectProjection`, `SqlParseException`, `FLYWAY_BASELINE_PATH`). The rest is static (none of it read state): `SqlSelectReader` (a SELECT's projection; `parseSelectForImport` delegates to its `parseSelect`), `SqlScriptParser` (the dialect clean-up, `splitStatements`, `parseTables`, `toTableModel`, `IGNORABLE_STATEMENT`), `SqlEntityRenderer` (entity, enums, id class, repository), `SqlRestRenderer` (service, DTO, mapper, controller, its test), `SqlJavaNames`.
- *`config/FullstackStarterController`* (1,185 → 339 lines) is the HTTP: the five endpoints, their records, the two exception handlers, the update's response. **`config/FullstackProjectAssembler`** (a bean) builds the project — `resolve` → `Resolved`, `buildArtifacts`, `renderFrontend`, `schemaOf`, `generateSide`, `templatesHash`; `FullstackModelFile` is `menora-model.json` (`MODEL_FILE`, `modelJson`, `templatesHashOf`, `GENERATOR_KEY`, `SCHEMA_VERSION_KEY`); `FullstackRootFiles` the README's sections and the root `.gitignore` / `.gitattributes`. The controller still clears the per-request contexts.
- *`FullstackStarterIntegrationTests`* (4,215 lines, 95 tests in one class) is now eight classes on `FullstackEndpointTestSupport` (`generateZip`, `postFullstack`, `unzip`, `pkField`, `bodyWithField`), each `@IntegrationTest @Tag("slow")`: the project as a whole stays in `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` (17), and `FullstackFieldsGenerationTests` (18), `FullstackRelationsGenerationTests` (11), `FullstackScaffoldOptionsTests` (17), `FullstackThemeGenerationTests` (10), `FullstackAccessGenerationTests` (3), `FullstackDatasourceConfigTests` (5) and `FullstackSchemaImportTests` (14) take a model's parts. **A test name earlier sections give under `FullstackStarterIntegrationTests` may now be in one of these** — search by the method name.

**Phase 2 — a fix sweep over generated code.** Defects recorded in earlier "Limits" notes, and four more found while reading for them. Each has a pin in `FullstackFixSweepTests`; what a text pin cannot prove is run in `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests` (`liveApiOfTheFixSweepBehaves`, `keywordNamedTablesAndColumnsBuildAndBehave`, `-Dsmoke.be=true`).

- *SQL keywords as names.* A field called `order`, `user`, `value` or `key` — or a table called `values` — was written bare in the mapping, in the SQL the templates splice and in the Flyway baseline, so the project did not start (on H2, the default database, for all four).
  - *The rule.* `SchemaNames` renders a name that is a keyword **on the request's database** quoted, **in the case that database folds bare names to**: `"ORDER"` on H2, Oracle and DB2, `"order"` on PostgreSQL, `[order]` on SQL Server (`SqlNames.quoteFolded`). A name the database takes bare stays bare — nothing about a working project's columns changes.
  - *How.* `SchemaNames.forDatabase(SqlDialect)` (a thread-local, set by `FullstackProjectAssembler.namesFor` at the end of `resolve`, cleared with `EntityDefinitionContext.clear()`); every name `SchemaNames` renders goes through `pin`. Nothing downstream changed: a quoted name was already supported for imported DDL — `SchemaBuilder.name()`, `SqlNames.javaLiteral`, and `QuotedNamesNamingStrategy`, which `rendersQuotedName` now also ships for a quoted join table or files table. With no database said (unit tests, the validators) names are rendered as before. The model file keeps the user's bare names.
  - *The project's own tests run on H2*, whatever its database. A name that database takes bare and H2 reserves (`key`, `value`, `year` on PostgreSQL) stays bare, and the test connection tells H2 the word is no keyword: `;NON_KEYWORDS=KEY,VALUE` on `testDbUrl` (`SchemaNames.freedOnH2`, in `GeneratedTests`). H2 frees every word that way but `CONSTRAINT`, `FOREIGN`, `PRIMARY`, `SET` and — as a table — `VALUES`, so those five are on every database's list.
  - *The words.* `sql/SqlKeywords` reads `sql/reserved-words.txt`: a list per database (`[h2]`, `[postgresql]`, `[oracle]`, `[mssql]`; DB2 and MySQL take every word listed — quoting a word a database does not reserve is harmless, missing one is the old failure) and the columns every table already has (`[system:postgresql]` `ctid`, `xmin`…), which no column can be named: a 400 at `entities[i].fields[j].name`. `SqlKeywordsTest` asks an in-memory H2: a word H2 refuses bare, among every listed word and the names models commonly use, must be on its list; each must work quoted; and each word another database takes bare must be freed by `NON_KEYWORDS`, as a column and as a table. `FullstackSchema` publishes both (`names.sqlReservedWords`, `names.sqlSystemColumns`, by dependency id).
  - ***A `@Formula` cannot name another table's quoted name.*** Hibernate reads a quoted identifier in a formula as a column of the entity itself and puts the entity's alias before it (`select t.b1_0."KEY" from b1_0."VALUES" t` — found by the real build). Two formulas name another table: a link's label and a parent's count of its child rows. So: **the label** of an entity is its first text field *a formula can name* (`SchemaNames.formulaCanName`; a quoted column is passed over, and an entity whose table or key is quoted has no label — a link to it shows the id); **a child count** over a child whose table or link column has to be quoted is a 400 saying which name to change (`checkCountedTables`: a declared ONE_TO_MANY, a composition, or `inverseCollections`). Both hold for exact-case names from imported DDL too, which had the same fault. The entity's own quoted columns are fine in its formulas (the alias is theirs).
  - *`.ddl?dialect=D` and create-tables* render the names for the database they write for (`FullstackProjectAssembler.schemaOf(body, dialect)`, `SchemaInstaller.dialectOf(source)`), not the project's.
  - *Pasted DDL.* `SqlNames.physical(name, quoted, dialect)` keeps the quotes of a quoted keyword (`"order"` on PostgreSQL was rendered bare, as "what the database stores anyway") — the SQL wizard and the DDL import. `LiveDdlWriter` quotes every word `SqlKeywords` knows.
  - *Not covered:* a SELECT view's own labels (the SELECT has to alias them quoted); DB2's words are every other database's, not its own list.
- *A soft-deleted row holding a unique value.* Deleting only marks a row, so its unique values stay taken and a new row with them was refused as "A record with these values already exists" — about a row nobody sees. On an entity with soft delete and a unique field or key (`deletedHoldsKeys`), `Service.create`, `update` and each `importAll` row first ask, per constraint, for a deleted row with the same values (`requireNoDeletedHolder`: a native query in `FlushModeType.COMMIT`, so the row being saved is not written first; a null member skips it; `FeatureContext.deletedProbes`). A hit is `web/DeletedHolderException` → 409 with `detail` "A deleted Task has the same Project and Title", `errors` on those fields and `deleted: {id}`. The list page's save offers **Restore it** (the existing `POST /{id}/restore`, then the drawer closes); form and wizard pages show the message on the fields. `conflictErrors` (= `hasUniqueKeys` ∨ `hasDeletedHolders`) is what makes the API client keep a 409's field errors. The constraint itself is unchanged — no column, no schema version. Two saves racing past the question get the database's own refusal.
- *History restore* (`ChangeHistory.restore`) set a row's fields directly and flushed. It now takes the entity's own say: `Service.checkRestored` — the DTO's Bean Validation (`restoreValidates`, with the validation starter) and `<Entity>Rules.check` — run on the row before the flush, and what it throws leaves the row as it was (400). It answers the fields the old version recorded that the row no longer has (renamed or removed since), which used to be dropped silently: the endpoint's body is now `{row, skipped}` (`Controller.Restored`), and the history panel says "…except X, which this record no longer has".
- *Rules on claim, release and reassign.* They changed the assignee and saved; a rule may read it. Each now runs `checkRules` (the linked rows loaded, then `<Entity>Rules.check`), like a transition.
- *Demo rows.* An entity with rules skips a demo row a rule is broken by (`hasRuleChecks`: `if (!Rules.apply(row).isEmpty()) continue;`) — the app would refuse to save it, so it could never be edited — and a child whose parent got no rows that way is skipped too (`targetMayHaveNoRows`). The rows already take values the rules accept where the solver finds them (`RuleRows`), so this is the net under it.
- *A hidden field's leftover value.* A value typed into a field that a later choice hid was still posted, and the server's `@Valid` refused it on a field the user could not see. `<Entity>Form` now empties a field as its condition hides it (its `onChange` goes through the show-when conditions in the model's order, as `Rules.derive` clears them on save).
- *A computed field over the clock.* `daysOpen = daysBetween(openedOn, today())` was stored at save and read stale ever after. A computed field whose formula calls `today()` or `now()` and reads no linked row (`FeatureContext.readsClock`) gets `<Entity>Rules.<field>Now(row)`, and `Dto.from` answers that instead of the stored column. Filters, sort and exports' SQL still read the stored value (the last save's).
- *Money in charts.* Bars, lines, donuts, stacked bars, pivots, ranked lists and a report's rows and total printed a sum of money as a plain number. `BarRows`, `LineChart`, `DonutChart`, `StackedBars`, `Legend` and the cards over them take `currency` (`widgets.tsx`), passed by the dashboard and report screens when the aggregate is over a MONEY field (`hasAggCurrency`, now set for report charts too). A count stays a number.
- *Exports.* `export.csv` and `export.xlsx` now take `changedBy` like the list (both ignored "Changed by me"), and the Excel button sends the list's own filters — its scope (a master-detail child list exported every parent's rows) and "changed by me". `XlsxWriter`: a `LocalDate` and a `LocalDateTime` are date cells (Excel's formats 14 and 22), each column has a width from its heading, and a sheet stops at Excel's 1,048,576 rows (`full()`).
- *Entity names a selected dependency also writes.* `User` with data-jpa's `sample-entity`, `Mail` with the mail sampler, `Resilient` with resilience4j: the same file or the same bean name. `FullstackProjectAssembler.checkSampleClassClashes` compares each entity's classes (`X`, `XRepository`, `XDto`, `XService`, `XController`, `XRules`, `XId`) with the Java files the selected dependencies and sub-options write: a 400 at `entities[i].name` naming the dependency and option.
- *Soft delete on SQL Server and Oracle.* The generated SQL said `deleted = true` / `false`, which SQL Server reads as a column ("Invalid column name 'true'" — every read of a soft-deleted entity failed there; found by running the generated API tests on a real SQL Server). It now says it as the project's database does (`SchemaDialect.flag` / `flagIs`, through `SchemaNames.booleanLiteral` / `booleanTest`; the view keys `softDeleteTrue`, `softDeleteFalse`, `softDeleteLive`): `SET deleted = 1` and, where it is compared, `cast(deleted as integer) = 0` on SQL Server and Oracle — the cast because the project's own tests run that same SQL on H2, which compares a boolean with neither a number nor a text. H2, PostgreSQL and DB2 keep the words, byte for byte. Pinned by `BooleanLiteralsTest` (H2 reads every spelling).

*Run for real (2026-11-19).* `-Dsmoke.be=true`: `liveApiOfTheFixSweepBehaves` (Projects with soft delete, history and the Excel export: a deleted task holding its key and its restore, a version put back, a date cell), `keywordNamedTablesAndColumnsBuildAndBehave` (H2 by Hibernate and by the Flyway baseline; PostgreSQL with its tests on H2 through `NON_KEYWORDS`), and the workflow, every-option, field-model, Lombok, composition and seven Flyway-baseline builds — all passed. `-Dsmoke.docker=true -Dsmoke.db=mssql`: the generated API tests of Orders (soft delete, locking, audit) on SQL Server 2022 over the Flyway baseline, Hibernate validating — 24 tests passed, the first time a generated backend has run on SQL Server. **Run on 2026-11-20** (see *After phase 11: the parked runs* at the end of this file): `aBackendOnAnotherDatabasePassesItsOwnTestsOnH2` for `postgresql`, `mssql` and `oracle` passed (their soft-delete SQL on H2); the keyword model through `CreateTablesDockerTests` passed on PostgreSQL, SQL Server and Oracle; the generated API tests on a real Oracle failed — the application did not start on Oracle 23 (a boolean column's type), fixed the same day. DB2 has no container.

*Closes* these earlier notes: keyword-named tables and columns (*Fullstack migrations*, *Fullstack DDL*); a soft-deleted row holding its key (*Deeper field model, phase 1*); rules on claim/release/reassign and demo rows, a hidden field's checks, a formula over `today()` (*phases 2–3*); a restore skipping validation and dropping a renamed field silently (*Who changed what*); charts printing money as plain numbers (*Round 10, phase 1*); the Excel export's dates and widths (*phase 7*).

*Golden re-pin:* 52 of the 61 fullstack manifests — `widgets.tsx` in every layout case, plus Approvals' `ExpenseClaimService`, controllers and `DemoDataLoader`, Orders' controllers, `XlsxWriter`, `useOrderList`/`useOrderLineList`, Projects' `TaskForm` and the dashboards with a money chart. No wizard manifest moved. `fullstack-schema.json` gained the two word lists.

### Phase 3: row-level access

An entity's rows can belong to users. Beside `read` and `write`, `access` takes `owners` and `rows`:

```json
"access": { "read": "USER", "write": "USER", "owners": ["createdBy", "assignee"], "rows": "OWN" }
```

- **`owners`** (1–4): `createdBy` — whoever created the row, which turns the entity's `audit` on (an explicit `audit: false` is a 400) — and/or stored STRING fields holding a user id (not the key, not computed, no show-when). A row is a caller's when any of them names the caller. **Admins are never limited**, and neither is work no request asked for (demo data, scheduled jobs).
- **`rows`**: `OWN` (the default with owners) — a user sees and changes only their own rows; to anyone else a row is not there (404). `OWN_WRITES` — every user sees every row, only its owners change it (403).
- It needs an ldap-auth dependency like any non-default access. A request without the keys keeps its bytes (checked: both golden suites passed unchanged before the Approvals example took the setting).
- 400s (`fullstack/RowAccessRules`, run after the compositions are resolved; paths `entities[i].access.owners[k]` / `.rows`; mirrored in the editor): an owner that is no field, not a STRING, the key, computed or shown on a condition; an owner named twice; more than four; `rows` without `owners`; an unknown mode; on a SELECT view; on a composed child (it follows its parent); `read: ADMIN`; `OWN_WRITES` with `write: ADMIN` or on a read-only entity; `createdBy` on a read-only entity; the workflow's assignee as the only owner (a new row never has one, so nobody could own what they create).

**Model.** `EntityDefinition.Access(read, write, owners, rows)` (`rowLimited()`, `ownRowsOnly()`, `ownWritesOnly()`; the two-argument constructor remains). `FullstackSchema` publishes `rowAccess: {modes, defaultMode, creator, maxOwners}`.

**View-model — `fullstack/RowAccessContext`.** `rulesOf(entities)` gives each limited entity its `Rule(holder, link)`: its own owners, or — a composed child — its parent's, read through the composition link. From that one description "is this row the caller's" is written four ways: a criteria predicate (`rowMineSpecJava`), a Java test on a loaded row (`rowMineJava`), JPQL over an alias (a link to such a row) and native SQL over an alias (a soft-deleted row, a file's holder). `putProject` (in `buildProjectContext`, so both render paths) sets `hasRowAccess`, `hasOwnWrites` and turns `usesCurrentUser` on; `putEntity` sets `rowLimited`, `rowsOwnOnly`, `rowsOwnWrites`, `rowsViaParent`, `rowOwnsOnCreate` + `rowFillOwners`, `rowRestoresOwned` + `rowOwnedSqlJava`, `ownLinks` / `ownSets` (links to `OWN` entities), `usesRowAccess`, and adds to `usesEntityManager`.

**Generated backend** (`spring-jpa-crud`; the Lombok set borrows). `web/RowAccess` (gated `hasRowAccess`): `caller()` → `Caller(id, limited)` from the request's `userinfo` header and `CurrentUser`'s roles, worked out once per request; unlimited outside a request; `RowAccess.as(caller, work)` carries a caller onto a thread that serves no request. `web/RowAccessException` → 403 from `ApiExceptionHandler`. In `Service.java.mustache`:

| Path | Under `OWN` | Under `OWN_WRITES` |
|---|---|---|
| the three `findAll`s (so the list, "Changed by me", the inbox queues, the CSV and Excel exports, widgets) and `stats` | `limited(specOf(…))`: the caller's rows | everything |
| `findById` — and so get, update, delete, a transition, claim, release, the move log | `.filter(this::isMine)`: 404 | `requireMine(findById(id))` on update, delete, transition and release: 403 (claim stays open — it is how a row becomes somebody's) |
| bulk edit and bulk delete | somebody else's row counts as missing: 404 naming it, nothing changed | 403, nothing changed |
| create, each imported row | `own(entity)`: with `createdBy` among the owners nothing to do; else the first owner field left empty (never the workflow's assignee, which `create` empties) is filled with the caller, and a row made out to somebody else is a 403 / that row's import error | the same |
| soft-delete restore | `requireOwnedDeleted`: a native count (the restriction hides the row) — 404 | the same |
| `GET /{id}/history` | the controller reads the row first for a limited caller: 404 | open |
| time in state | `@RequiresPermission(ADMIN)`; the dashboard draws its card for admins | as before |
| a composed child (`rowsViaParent`) | its predicate goes through the link (`root.get("visit").get("createdBy")`); `requireParentMine` on create, update and import: 400 "visitId: no Visit 7" | 403 |
| a link to a row of an `OWN` entity the caller cannot see (`requireVisibleLinks`, MANY_TO_ONE and MANY_TO_MANY) | 400 in the words of an unknown id; a link the row already had is left alone | — |

- *Exports.* The rows are read while the response is written, after the request's thread has gone: the controller takes `service.caller()` on the request thread and reads each chunk inside `RowAccess.as(caller, …)`.
- *Files* (`Attachments`): an owner column of an `OWN` entity carries `mineSql`, and `requireReadable` also asks `seesRow` — the same count over the caller's rows (through `owner_id` for a field of several files), or the ADMIN role.
- *Live events* (`LiveEvents`): `Tracked.ownRows`; a non-admin listener gets such an entity's change without its `rowId` (the frontend only refreshes).
- *Schema* (`SchemaBuilder`): an index on `created_by` and on each owner column (the workflow's assignee already had one).
- *Demo data*: a third of the rows carry `dev-user` in an owner field. A demo row's creator is `system`, so an entity owned only by its creator shows a non-admin dev user no demo rows.
- *Generated tests*: a fourth caller `api-test-other` (USER; `application-test.yaml`, `ApiTestSupport.OTHER`, both gated `hasRowAccess`) and `<Entity>ApiTest.aUsersRowIsTheirOwn` — a user makes a row, the other user gets 404 (or reads it and gets 403 on a change), an admin does both. Left out where a user cannot make a row here: only admins write, the row links to a row only its owner sees, its rows are its parent's, a composite key.

**Generated frontend.** Under `OWN` nothing per row is needed: every row a user sees is theirs. Under `OWN_WRITES` (`listOwnWrites` on the entity view, also read by the pages):
- `entities/<e>/model/types.ts` gains `isMine<Entity>(row, me)` (an admin: always; else `row.<owner> === me.id`), and `me.ts` / `GET /api/me` ship with any row access.
- The list hook (`use<Entity>List`): `mine(row)`; Edit and Delete (`ifMine`), a cell edited in place and a kanban move refuse somebody else's row with a toast (`notYours`, in both languages, gated `hasOwnWrites`); `writeChecked` is on for such an entity even when anyone signed in may write.
- Hidden per row: Edit and Delete in the side pane and Edit in the quick look (both sets' `EntityPage`), the record page's Edit and Delete, the workflow's moves in `<Entity>Transitions` (Claim stays), the master–detail parent's Edit; a board card is not moved. The shared `Table` / `CardGrid` still draw a row's buttons — they refuse with the toast.
- **Write actions that were shown to callers who may not write** now ask `useCallerMay` (with ldap-auth, and only where the entity's write level is not SIGNED_IN): the master–detail parent's New and Edit, a calendar day's New, a board's move. This moved the bytes of every ldap project with those pages.

**Example.** Approvals' `ExpenseClaim`: `{read: USER, write: USER, owners: [createdBy, assignee], rows: OWN}` — a user sees the claims they made or hold; admins approve. (It had the default write level, so only admins could create a claim.)

**Golden re-pin:** 25 of 61 fullstack manifests. Approvals (9): `ExpenseClaimService`, `ExpenseClaimController`, `Attachments`, `ApiExceptionHandler`, new `RowAccess` / `RowAccessException`, `useExpenseClaimList` (asks for USER), `access.ts`, the claim's record screen, the home dashboard (the time-in-state card for admins), `menora-model.json`. Blog, Orders, Tickets (4 each): the master–detail screen. Projects (4): the board and calendar screens. `fullstack-schema.json` gained `rowAccess`. No wizard manifest moved.

**Pinned by** `FullstackRowAccessTests` (12: both modes, the owner field, composed rows, links, files and live changes, the generated tests, the baseline's indexes, every 400, the Approvals example). **Run for real** (2026-11-19): `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests.rowsBelongToTheirUsersOverTheRealApi` builds `FullstackExampleRequests.rowAccessBody` with ldap-auth-rest in both modes and drives it as two users and an admin — lists, exports read on another thread, bulk calls, import, soft-delete restore, history, statistics, a composed child, a link to an unseen row — a client's files read by its owner and refused to another user — with the project's own generated tests in the same build; `approvalsWithItsOwnedClaimsBuildsAndPassesItsTests` builds the Approvals example as it ships (ldap-auth-rest, roles, owned claims) and runs its generated tests; `GeneratedFrontendBuildSmokeTests.fullstackFrontend[Menora]RowAccessInstallsAndBuilds` install, lint, build (and, on the tailwind set, test) a layout with a side-pane list, a record page, a master–detail page, a board and a calendar under `OWN_WRITES`.

**Limits.** A linked row's label and a child count (both `@Formula`) show whatever the caller may see, and a rule over a linked row still reads it on the server. No ownership by LDAP group or team, no field-level access, no ownership through a relation other than a composition. Under `OWN` a user sees the unassigned queue only for rows that are otherwise theirs (the editor says so). A deleted row's history is not shown to a limited caller. The shared table and cards draw Edit/Delete on every row under `OWN_WRITES`. The Approvals smoke builds that drop ldap-auth drop the example's `access` too.

*Closes* from *Enforced access*'s "Not done": the parent's New and Edit on a master–detail page, a calendar day's create and a board page's drag follow who may write; the check can be per row. Still open there: a list embedded as a tab beyond what its `EntityPage` hides, two roles, levels fixed at generation.

### Phase 4: relations and rows depth

Four things the relation model left out, each gated: a request that uses none of them keeps its bytes (the golden suite showed it — see *Golden re-pin* below for the one shared file that moved).

**A many-to-many from both sides.** `mappedBy` on a MANY_TO_MANY makes it the other side of a set its target declares:

```json
{ "name": "Tag", "relations": [ { "type": "MANY_TO_MANY", "fieldName": "posts", "targetEntity": "Post", "mappedBy": "tags" } ] }
```

- It stays in `EntityDefinition.collections` (so the DTO's `<field>Ids` / `<field>Labels`, the form's picker, the list column, the details, the filter and the CSV export come for nothing) and `ownedCollections()` is the subset that holds a join table — what `SchemaBuilder` and `SchemaNames` walk. One join table, the owner's.
- 400s (`parseRelations`, then `resolveInverseSets` once every entity's sets are known; path `entities[i].relations[j].mappedBy` / `.joinTable`): a `mappedBy` that is not a MANY_TO_MANY of the target to this entity holding its own join table (the candidates are named); `joinTable` / `joinColumn` / `inverseJoinColumn` beside it; two second sides of one set; an owner whose rows belong to users (`access.owners`) — a change from this side changes the owner's rows.
- Entity (both backend sets): `@ManyToMany(mappedBy = "tags", fetch = LAZY)` + `@BatchSize`. Service: `link<Field>(row, wanted)` makes a change in the owners' own sets — rows told apart **by key**, never by `equals` (a Lombok entity's hash moves with its values) — called by `update`, by `linkedBack(saved)` after a create or an import (after the save: an assigned-key row is merged into another instance), and by `unlinked(row)` before a real delete, single or bulk (`unlinksOnDelete`; a soft-deleted row keeps its links for a restore). Deleting a row that sets point at used to be the join table's foreign-key failure.
- Not from this side: a bulk change of the set. Saving the second side moves the owner rows' versions (their set changed).

**A many-to-many where it was skipped.**
- *Bulk edit* (`bulkSetsApplicable`: the option, and a set the entity owns): `PATCH /api/x/bulk {ids, field: "tags", value: [ids]}` puts the same rows on every picked record — `stubs<Field>(value)` → `resolveLinks` once (an unknown id is a 400, nothing changed), `null` or `[]` empties the set, a set of rows only their owners see goes through `requireVisibleLinks`. The list's bulk bar offers the set with a `MultiRelationPicker` (both sets' `EntityPage`; the ids ride in the bar's value, comma-joined).
- *In place* (`inlineLinks` = inline edit ∧ a set, either side): the list column carries `links: '<field>'` (the per-entity column type gains `{ links?: string }`), `INLINE_LINKS` says where a row keeps the ids and what the picker reads, and the cell is `shared/ui/InlineLinks.tsx` (gated `hasInlineLinks`): a pencil opens the picker, Save writes the ids with the row's PUT.
- *Wizard*: a step may ask for a set by its field name (`PageStructureRules.steps`; its messages are keyed `<field>Ids`), and steps a wizard does not name end with the sets — before this a wizard could not set one at all.
- *Demo rows*: `buildSeedOrder` puts a set's target before its owner, and each demo row gets two links (`seedSets`).

**A one-to-many past ten rows.** `RelatedRows.tsx` loads ten and offers *Load more (n left)* (the board's string), page after page.

**Composition.**
- *A parent reads its rows.* Five functions in the expression language: `sum(lines.total)`, `min`, `max`, `avg` over a number field of a composed list, and `count(lines)`. An empty value is left out; no rows sum to 0 and have no min, max or avg; a list that was not read at all is unknown (so the rule holds). `EntityDefinition.LinkedEntity` gains `rows` (the entity's `links` hold its composed lists beside its MANY_TO_ONEs — the names cannot clash) and, for those, the rows' own `rules`. `ExprCheck` (`AGGREGATES`, `Checked.rows` / `.lists`, `Rows(fn, list, child, field)`) refuses `lines.total` or `lines` outside a function, a field the rows do not have, one that is not a number, and says which lists there are; `ExprCode` writes `Expr.sum(row.getLines(), each -> Expr.num(each.getLineTotal()))` and `rule.sum(value.lines, 'lineTotal')`; `ExprEval` runs them over a list of row maps. The Java runtime's functions are gated `rulesReadRows`; `rules.ts` is a static file, so its five functions reach every project with rules.
- *A rule over rows alone* shows its message on the list (`field` may name a composed list, and is the first list read when nothing else is): the form draws it under the rows editor (`showsMessage`).
- *The form follows the rows.* The rows editor of a child whose parent reads it keeps each row as it will be saved (`rowsDeriveLive`: hidden fields cleared, computed ones computed — `derived(row)` in `<Child>Rows.tsx`), so `value.lines[i].lineTotal` is current and the parent's own computed field and checks follow each edit. A row of a list page carries no rows: the functions answer unknown there, and the server decides.
- *A row changed on its own.* A composed child whose parent reads its rows (`parentReadsRows`) re-runs the parent's rules after every change through its own endpoints — create, update (the parent it left too), delete, restore, import and both bulk calls: `recheckParent` flushes, refreshes the parent and runs `<Parent>Rules.check`, which also saves a recomputed total. A change that breaks a parent's rule is a 400 in the parent's words, and rolled back.
- *The version check.* A parent with a composition gets `optimisticLocking` unless its own `opts` say `false` (`resolveChildLists` → `withOpt`), so two people saving the same rows get the stale 409 instead of one silently replacing the other's. A field named `version` on such an entity is the option's 400. An existing project's update therefore adds the `version` column to such a parent — review that migration like any other.
- *Details.* A composed list is drawn from the record's own rows (`rows={value.lines} whole` on `RelatedRows`), whole; read from a list row, which has none, it fetches up to 200.
- *Tests and demo rows.* `RuleRows` makes the test record with **one row of each list its rules read**: the row's fields the parent adds up and the fields the rows' own rules read are choices of the same search (`<list>.<field>`), and the rows' rules are checked on that row (`RowScope`). `GeneratedTests` writes the row into `ApiTestSupport.body` (`row.putArray("lines").addObject()`, with a new row of each entity it must link to) and into the frontend's valid row. A rule one row cannot pass (`count(lines) >= 2`), or rows whose own expressions read a linked row, leave the entity's tests to the team, naming the rule — as before. `DemoDataLoader` makes composed rows before their parent, two to each, and saves them with it (`seedComposed` / `seedRows`), so a parent's total and "needs a row" rule have rows to read; composed rows are no longer saved on their own.

**Examples.** Orders: `Order.total` is `sum(lines.lineTotal)` and an order needs a line (`count(lines) > 0`); the order is versioned. Blog: `Tag.posts` is the other side of `Post.tags`, and its options gain `seedData`, `bulkUpdate` and `inlineEdit`.

**Limits.** One row per list in a generated test record. A composition two levels deep, more than 200 rows in one save and a many-to-many in the CSV import stay out. An import of parents carries no rows, so a parent that needs one cannot be imported. A parent's computed field over its rows is right after every save through the API; rows written straight to the database do not recompute it. The second side of a set is not a bulk change. A child's computed cell that reads a linked row is taken into the parent's live total only at the next edit of that row.

**Golden re-pin:** 33 of 61 fullstack manifests and no wizard manifest. `fullstack-schema.json` gained one reserved entity name, `InlineLinks` (the new shared component — `ReservedNamesGuardTest` asked for it). Approvals (9) and Projects (8): `shared/ui/rules.ts` only — the static runtime gained the five row functions. Orders (8): `Order`, `OrderDto`, new `OrderRules`, `rules/Expr`, `OrderService`, `OrderLineService`, `ApiExceptionHandler` (the stale 409), the order's `types.ts` / `validate.ts` / `OrderForm` / `OrderDetail` / list hook and page, `OrderLineRows`, `RelatedRows`, `rules.ts`, new `StaleNotice`, `strings.ts`, `menora-model.json`. Blog (8): what its three new options add (`DemoDataLoader`, every controller and service, every list hook and page, `InlineCell`, `InlineLinks`) and `Tag`'s second side (`Tag`, `TagDto`, `TagService`, its `types.ts`, form and details, the tags screen), `menora-model.json`. Tickets, Inventory, Course enrolments and Sales reporting did not move.

**Pinned by** `FullstackRelationsDepthTests` (10), `ExprTest` (three cases for rows), `RuleRowsTest` (two). **Run for real** (2026-11-19, `-Dsmoke.be=true`): `aManyToManyIsReadAndChangedFromBothSidesOverTheRealApi` on both backend sets (read, filter and save from either side, a tag made with its posts, an unknown id refused, a bulk change, a tag deleted out of its posts singly and in bulk, the demo rows' links, with the project's generated tests); `liveApiOfABackendWithEveryOptBehaves` (Orders as it ships: an order without a line refused on `lines`, the total following lines added, changed, bulk-changed, deleted and restored through their own endpoints, the last line's delete refused and rolled back, the stale 409, the demo orders' lines and totals; the generated `OrderApiTest` makes its order with a line); `liveApiOfAComposedParentBehaves` on both sets (compositions that read nothing, as before). `-Dsmoke.fe=true`: Blog on both skins with a wizard, bulk edit and cells edited in place; Orders with its own generated tests; the Orders layout, a one-to-many model and the Menora composition.

### Phase 5: SQL wizard depth

What the SQL wizard reads from a script, and what the live-schema reader takes from a database. Nothing on the wire changed; a script without a view or an index keeps its bytes.

**Views (`SqlScriptParser`, `SqlSelectReader`).**
- `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [MATERIALIZED | FORCE | …] VIEW [IF NOT EXISTS] name [(columns)] AS <query>` is read (`CREATE_VIEW_LEAD`, then `AS_QUERY`; `WITH CHECK OPTION` / `READ ONLY` / `[NO] DATA` cut off). It was dropped — or a parse error where JSqlParser could not read it. The view is a `TableModel` with `viewQuery = "SELECT * FROM <name as written>"`: the entity reads **the view itself**, by name, so it follows whatever the view's query becomes and takes the names the database gives the columns. Its entity is named after the view (`author_stats` → `AuthorStats`; a number is added when a class already has the name).
- **Typed at the end of the script** (`typeViews`, over `PendingView`s — a dump puts a view before the tables it reads): `SqlSelectReader.project(select, tables)` gives each column of the query a `SqlType` — the type of the table column it selects (a table of the script, or an earlier view by its own name), of a `CAST(x AS type)`, `BIGINT` for `count(…)`, and what `min` / `max` are taken over. `*` and `t.*` become the columns of the tables they stand for. A column list names the columns and the query types them by position. What nothing types has `rawType == null`: a `String` with the TODO, as every view field was. **`ColumnModel.rawType` may be null now, on a view only**; a text column of a view carries no length (a read-only field needs none, and the DTO would get a `@Size`).
- A bare `SELECT` is typed the same way (its labels are still read where the statement is, by `toViewTableModel`); its query is the SELECT itself, as before, and it is still left out of the Flyway baseline. A `CREATE VIEW` stays in the baseline.
- A query JSqlParser cannot read keeps the text-scanned labels, and `castTypes` reads its `CAST(… AS type) AS label` items the same way.
- 400s (`SqlParseException`, naming the view and the statement): `SELECT *` over a table the script does not create and no column list; an expression without a name and no column list; no column found at all. Each says to name the columns (`CREATE VIEW v (a, b) AS …`).
- `ViewEntity.java.mustache` takes `typeImports`, and per column `type` and `untyped` (the TODO line only then).
