- **Fullstack DDL import** (`SqlToEntityDefinitionConverter.toViewEntity`): a view of the script — a `CREATE VIEW`, or a SELECT among the statements, which used to become an ordinary writable entity of strings — is a read-only entity with `viewQuery`, its fields named as the query names its columns (a view's `@Column` is the field name) and typed like a table's. `POST /metadata/fullstack/import-ddl` answers the "first column was made the key" note when there is one.

**Indexes (`SqlConstraintReader.applyIndex`, `TableIndex`).**
- `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX [CONCURRENTLY] [IF NOT EXISTS] [name] ON [ONLY] table [USING m] (keys) …`, the key list read to its closing parenthesis. Over plain columns (`name [COLLATE c] [ASC|DESC] [NULLS FIRST|LAST]`): a unique one is the table's UNIQUE (as before), any other one of `TableModel.indexes` → `@Table(indexes = {@Index(name = "…", columnList = "a, b DESC")})`, the columns in the form `@Column` has them (`sqlName`), so Hibernate finds them — a quoted mixed-case column included (built for real).
- **Said, not dropped:** a partial index (`WHERE`), one over an expression, and one of another kind (`USING` anything but btree, `BITMAP`) are a sentence in `TableModel.notes`, written as `// …` lines above the entity's annotations. A unique index over an expression used to fall through the old pattern silently.
- An index on a table the script does not create is none of the reader's business (like an `ALTER`).

**Live database (`liveschema/`).**
- **One catalog query per schema** for primary keys, foreign keys and indexes (`DialectSupport.primaryKeysQuery` / `foreignKeysQuery` / `indexesQuery`; row shapes in their javadoc), written for PostgreSQL (`pg_constraint`, `pg_index`), SQL Server (`sys.*`), Oracle (`all_constraints`, `all_indexes`), DB2 for IBM i (`QSYS2.SYS*`) and H2 (`INFORMATION_SCHEMA`). It was three driver calls per table. A dialect without a query, or a database that refuses one, is asked through the driver table by table as before — per kind, logged at WARN, and listed by `LiveSchemaReader.refusedQueries()`. `menora.live-schema.catalog-queries=false` turns the queries off altogether (the way out should one answer differently from the driver on some server).
- **Indexes** (`LiveSchemaModel.Index`): `sortIndexes` makes of each what it is to the script — the primary key's own (dropped), a unique constraint, a plain index, or one the script cannot carry with the reason (`skipped`). A key column the table does not have is an expression (PostgreSQL's driver reports the expression's text, Oracle a hidden `SYS_NC…$` column — a `DESC` key on Oracle is one too). **A partial unique index is no longer read as a UNIQUE constraint** (it allows duplicates outside its condition), and an index with one expression key is no longer read as an index over its other columns.
- **Defaults and comments** (`Column.defaultValue` / `comment`, `Table.comment`): from the driver's `COLUMN_DEF` and `REMARKS` — read in the result's own column order, since Oracle streams the default — and from `commentsQuery()` where the driver's remarks are empty (SQL Server's `MS_Description`, Oracle's `all_tab_comments` / `all_col_comments`).
- **The script (`LiveDdlWriter`)** gains, after the foreign keys: `CREATE INDEX name ON t (a, b DESC);` per plain index and a `-- Index … is not in this script: it is partial.` line per other; `DEFAULT` on a column when the script can carry it (`defaultLiteral`: a number, a text, true/false, the current date or time — out of `((0))`, `'open'::character varying`, `N'x'`, `now()`, `getdate()`), and otherwise a comment after the column (`-- default in the database: gen_random_uuid()`), never on an identity column; `COMMENT ON TABLE | COLUMN … IS '…';`, or plain script comments where that is not a statement (SQL Server, `commentOnStatements()`) and for a view (the script does not create it).
- **A view's SELECT casts its columns** (`typedSelectOf`): `SELECT CAST(id AS BIGINT) AS id, CAST(name AS VARCHAR(80)) AS name FROM hr.v` — a cast to the type the column already has, which is how the wizard types the fields. Not cast: binary columns and large objects, and a column whose type the reader did not know and wrote as text (`castableText`: only when the driver itself calls it a character type) — those stay a `String` with the TODO. The fullstack import still uses the plain `selectOf` and types the fields from the catalog.

**Golden re-pin:** 7 wizard manifests (the six `sql__postgresql__*` cases and `wizards__sql-openapi-soap`), no fullstack manifest; `sql__mssql__mapstruct-dto` did not move. `library-postgresql.sql` gained five indexes (plain, several columns with a `DESC`, unnamed, on a quoted column, plus an expression and a GIN one) and two views (`author_stats`, `publisher_list` with a column list over `SELECT *`). What moved: `Books`, `Loans`, `OrderItems` (`indexes = …`), `Authors`, `Reviews` (notes — `reviews_one_unrated` is the partial unique index the fixture already had), `Authors2` and its DTO (`bookCount` is a `Long` now), the new views' entity / repository / service / DTO / mapper / controller, the baseline in the Flyway case, and `menora-model.json` (it holds the script).

**Pinned by** `SqlEntityGeneratorTest` (8 new: views, a view before its tables, the 400s, the baseline, a typed SELECT, casts read from text, indexes), `SqlToEntityDefinitionConverterTest.aViewInTheScriptIsAReadOnlyEntityWithTypedFields`, `LiveDdlWriterTest` (indexes / defaults / comments, `defaultLiteral`, the casts of each database's types through the wizard), the new `LiveSchemaReaderTest` (an in-memory H2: both ways of reading agree, and the script runs on an empty database) and `LiveSchemaIntegrationTests`. **Run for real** (2026-11-19, `-Dsmoke.be=true`): `sqlWizardBackendMigratesWithItsBaselineAndPassesItsTests` — the baseline creates the view and the index, Hibernate validates, and an injected `ViewReadTests` reads the view through its entity (a `Long` count, a `LocalDateTime` max, a `BigDecimal` cast); `sqlWizardBackendCompilesAndCreatesItsSchema` on two API modes — Hibernate creates every `@Index` of the library fixture.

**Run on 2026-11-20:** `LiveSchemaDockerTests` (`-Dsmoke.docker=true`), extended for this phase — indexes, defaults, comments, the casts, no refused query, the driver agreeing — passed on PostgreSQL 16, SQL Server 2022 and Oracle 23 (3 tests, 96 s): the first time the three catalog queries ran on their servers. DB2 for IBM i has no image and stays unrun.

**Limits.** A view's first column is its key. A sub-query in `FROM` is not followed for types, nor are `sum` / `avg` / `coalesce` and other expressions (a `CAST` types them). An index's method, `INCLUDE` columns and name on a unique index are not kept. The fullstack import ignores indexes and notes. The live reader does not read sequences or triggers, and a default that is an expression stays a comment.

### Phase 6: SOAP — a WSDL in several files, and its first real build

A WSDL that imports other files used to be a 400 whatever came with it. It can now come with them. A request with one document keeps its bytes (the three `soap__*` manifests and `wizards__sql-openapi-soap` did not move).

**Wire.** `wsdlFilesByDep: {<dep>: {<relative path>: <text>}}` on `/starter-wizard.zip` / `.preview` (and so on `/starter.patch`, whose sides are the same record) — the documents the dependency's `wsdlByDep` entry refers to, each under its path relative to that main document, as the reference to it reads. `POST /starter-wizard.detect-services` takes `{wsdl, files?}`. Files for a dependency with no WSDL are a 400 (`wsdlFilesByDep.<dep> names files, but wsdlByDep has no WSDL…`); the keys are checked like every wizard map's; the files count toward `menora.wizard.max-documents-size` (`WizardDocumentLimit.flatten`, and `check(document, files)` for the detection).

**`soap/WsdlFiles`** — the main document and its files (a record, so it is the `WizardMemo` key of both `soap` and `soap-parse`):
- `of(main, files)` tidies the names (`\` → `/`, a leading `./`) and refuses, as a `SoapParseException` (the drawer shows its `messages`): a name that is not a path of plain segments (letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`; no `..`, nothing absolute) ending `.wsdl` / `.xsd` / `.xml`; an empty file; two names a case-ignoring disk cannot tell apart; more than `MAX_FILES` (100).
- `problems()` reads every document with the parser that takes no DOCTYPE (the main one as before; a file's failure names the file), requires a file's root to be `wsdl:definitions` or `xsd:schema`, and follows each `wsdl:import` / `xsd:import|include|redefine` **from the folder of the document that makes it**: an address (a scheme, a leading `/` or `\`) is always refused; so is a path that climbs out of the main document's folder; a path that is none of the files is "not among the files given" (one document: "not part of the document given", today's words with a new remedy). An `xsd:import` of a namespace alone is still fine.
- `locator()` is the `WSDLLocator` wsdl4j reads imports through — the files, by resolved path, under `mem:/` URIs. **Given a locator, wsdl4j's reader opens nothing itself**; `importDocuments` is on only with files, so one document is read exactly as before.

**`SoapCodeGenerator`** takes the files on `generate`, `detectServices` and `resolveWsdlFileNames` (the old signatures delegate with none). A service of an imported WSDL takes its own namespace (the service QName's), not the main document's.

**Generated.**
- The files go to `src/main/resources/wsdl/<path>` beside the main document, which keeps its name (`<first-service-kebab>.wsdl` — a file with that name, ignoring case, is a 400). The JAX-WS plugin is still given the main document alone and follows its imports on disk.
- With endpoints, `WebServiceConfig` gains a bean per `.wsdl` file (`SimpleWsdl11Definition`) and per `.xsd` file (`SimpleXsdSchema`), **named by the file's base name**: `MessageDispatcherServlet` answers `GET …/<name>.wsdl|.xsd` by the last path segment alone, whatever folder the URL names, so a relative reference in a served document (`port/country-port.wsdl`, `../schemas/countries.xsd`) leads to the right bean. Two files of one base name, or a file named like a service bean, therefore cannot both be served: a 400 in `ENDPOINTS` / `BOTH`, fine in `CLIENT`. A `.xml` file is in the project and not served.
- `menora-model.json` keeps `wsdlFilesByDep` (sorted, only for a dependency with files), so a project reopens and updates with them.

**SOAP output is built for real now** (2026-11-19, `-Dsmoke.be=true -Dsmoke.offline=true`, Boot 3.2.1 — the offline repository holds Spring-WS 4.0.9 only, so not on 3.5):
- `soapWizardBackendBuildsItsPayloadsAndAnswersOverSoap` — the Countries fixture in `BOTH`: wsimport makes the payload classes, the project compiles against the names the wizard assumed, and an injected `SoapTests` starts the application, fetches `/ws/countryService.wsdl` (the address rewritten to the server's own), posts a SOAP request (the stub endpoint answers a fault naming its TODO) and makes the same call through the generated client.
- `soapWizardBackendBuildsFromAWsdlInSeveralFiles` — the split fixture: wsimport follows the import to the port type and on to the schemas, and the application serves each file where the reference before it points (`/ws/port/country-port.wsdl`, `/ws/schemas/countries.xsd`, `/ws/schemas/types.xsd`); the types of the included schema are classes too.
- Both passed first time: the single-file output, never built before, needed no fix. `runMvn` takes `-Dsmoke.offline=true` (the nested build gets `-o`), as `LocalRunDockerSmokeTests` does.

**Fixture and golden.** `wizard-fixtures/countries-split/`: `countries.wsdl` (binding and service; imports `port/country-port.wsdl`), which imports `../schemas/countries.xsd`, which includes `types.xsd`. New case `soap__multi-file` (`BOTH`); no existing manifest moved.

**Pinned by** `SoapCodeGeneratorTest` (5 new: read through the imports, one document unchanged, what the files do not hold, a file held to what the main document is, files that cannot be written or served side by side), `ProjectGenerationIntegrationTests.soapWizardTakesAWsdlInSeveralFiles` / `soapWizardRefusesFilesItCannotRead`, `WizardGoldenOutputTests`.

**Limits.** The main document is the root of the set: a reference that climbs above it (`../xsd/types.xsd` from the main document) cannot be satisfied — the WSDL has to be given from the folder its references stay under. A file that imports the main document back is not resolved (the main has no name in the request). A served file's bean is named after the file, so a schema called like another bean of the application (`dataSource.xsd`) would stop it starting. An `xsd:import` with a query or fragment in its location is looked up as written. Not run on Boot 3.5.

### Phase 7: the catalog sync covers dependency groups, entries, sub-options and compatibility rules

A release's change to one of those four used to need a hand-written data migration (`V29`, `V34`, `V35`), and reached nothing an admin might have wanted to keep. They follow the release now, by the rules of *Catalog sync* above; no generated byte changed.

**Structure.** `db/CatalogSyncTables` holds the six tables, each a `Table<E>`: how its shipped rows are read from the manifests, its key, its fingerprint, its parent, how it is added, updated, deleted, and how the admin API shows it. `db/CatalogSync` holds the rules, the same for every table, and runs them through a `Pass` — the catalog as one run sees it (the records, which stored group stands for which shipped one, which dependency ids exist), kept current as the run writes. `CatalogSeedEntity.RowTable` is in the order rows are written.

| Table | Identity | What may change (the fingerprint) | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEPENDENCY_GROUP` | kind + the shipped name; the stored row by the record's `row_id` | name, sort order | — |
| `DEPENDENCY_ENTRY` | the dependency id (unique over both kinds in the table, so the kind is in the fingerprint) | group, kind, name, description, coordinates, version, scope, repository, sort order, Boot range, starter | its group |
| `SUB_OPTION` | kind, dependency, option id | label, description, sort order | its dependency |
| `COMPATIBILITY_RULE` | kind, source, target, relation | description, sort order | both dependencies |
| `FILE_CONTRIBUTION`, `BUILD_CUSTOMIZATION` | as before | as before | their dependency (`__common__` always exists) |

- **Groups (`V38`: `initializer_catalog_seed.row_id`).** A group has only its name, which an admin may change. Its record remembers the row that stands for it, so a renamed group is still the shipped group: `DIFFERS`, kept as it is, and an entry a release ships for it lands in it. A `row_id` that names no row (the admin import re-inserts every row under a new id) falls back to the name, and is corrected on the next start. An entry's fingerprint holds its group as the *shipped group's key*, so a rename does not make every entry of the group differ, and an entry moved to another group — by a release (it follows when untouched) or by an admin (kept) — does.
- **A row and what it needs (`Status.PARENT_GONE`, with a `reason`).** A shipped row whose parent is not in the catalog is neither added nor updated — **this changes file contributions and build customizations too**, which used to be added for a dependency an admin had deleted. Rows are written parents first (groups, entries, options, rules, files, build rows), so a dependency a release adds arrives with everything of its, and `apply` of a dependency together with its rows writes them in one call. `POST /admin/catalog-sync/apply` answers `{written, skipped, summary}`; `skipped` are the rows that could not be written, each with its `reason` (`CatalogSync.applyRows`; `apply` still answers the count).
- **Rows no longer shipped** go the other way — build rows, files, rules, options, entries, groups — an entry only when nothing names it (`Table.removable`: no option, rule, file, build row, starter-template dependency, module mapping or template-set default), a group only when it is empty. One that stays is the admin's from then on, as before.
- **The baseline is per table** (`Result.baselined`): a table no record knows yet is looked at for the first time — recorded, none of its rows changed. An existing database has been syncing files and build rows since Round 12, so its first start on this release baselines the four new tables alone, and its files keep following in that same start.
- **One reading of the manifests.** `DataSeeder.seedCatalog(kind)` saves what `CatalogSync.shippedDependencyGroups` (a group with its entries — saving it saves them), `shippedSubOptions`, `shippedCompatibilityRules`, `shippedFileContributions` and `shippedBuildCustomizations` read: its own loaders are gone, so a fresh seed and "the shipped row" cannot drift.
- **The metadata follows.** `CatalogSyncController.apply` / `keep` refresh `DatabaseInitializrMetadataProvider` (they did not), after flushing and clearing the request's persistence context: the provider reads groups with their entries, and a group the request had already loaded answered with the entries it had then.
- **Detail views.** `GET /admin/catalog-sync/{key}` shows a group and an entry as a map of their own fields (an entity would drag the group's entries along); an entry's `group` is the name the database has for it on both sides when it is the same group. Rows carry `table`, and `reason` for a parent that is gone.

**Tried on a copy of the real catalog database** (2026-11-20, schema at v35): `V36`–`V38` ran, the start logged "first look at [DEPENDENCY_GROUP, DEPENDENCY_ENTRY, SUB_OPTION, COMPATIBILITY_RULE] — recorded, none of their rows changed", and all 501 shipped rows were in step (19 groups, 60 entries, 65 options, 32 rules, 227 files, 98 build rows); a second start logged nothing. Over HTTP: an entry renamed through the admin API showed as `DIFFERS` and `apply {all: true}` took the shipped name back, the generator offering it at once; a dependency deleted with `?force=true` showed as `DELETED` with its two files and build row `PARENT_GONE`, stayed deleted through `apply {all: true}`, a file of its named alone came back in `skipped` with the reason, and the dependency named with its rows was written whole (4 rows).

**Found on the way, not changed:** `PUT /admin/dependency-groups/{id}` saves the request body as the group, entries included — a body without `entries` deletes the group's dependencies (orphan removal), and one carrying stale entries writes them back over newer edits. The admin screen sends the group as it received it, so it is safe from the first and exposed to the second.

**Pinned by** `CatalogSyncDependenciesTest` (12: every table in step and in order; rows nobody touched follow in each table; edited kept, deleted stays; a moved entry, by a release and by an admin; a renamed group; a stale `row_id`; a new dependency arriving with everything of its; children of a deleted dependency and an entry of a deleted group; the per-table baseline; removals and what keeps a row), `CatalogSyncTest` (unchanged rules; its "switched off" case builds the sync over `CatalogSyncTables`), `CatalogSyncAdminTests` (a dependency taken from the release is offered at once; `skipped` with the reason), `ConfigurationExportImportServiceTest.anImportedCatalogIsStillInStepWithTheRelease` (an export imported back: every row in step, the groups found by name under their new ids) and `DataSeederTest`.

**Limits.** Starter templates, module templates, palettes and versions are not synced. A row is taken whole — no field-by-field merge. A group a release renames is a new group to the sync: the new one is added, untouched entries move to it and the old one goes when it is empty. A row recorded while its parent was gone is reported, not added, when the parent comes back by another road than the sync's own `apply` (it shows as `MISSING` for an admin to take). The removal of an unshipped entry counts references in the catalog's own tables only.

### Phase 8: security follow-ups

Four things the earlier rounds' notes left open; no generated byte changed.

**1. Request bodies have a size limit (`config/RequestBodyLimitFilter`).** Every JSON endpoint reads its whole body into memory and nothing bounded it — `menora.wizard.max-documents-size` counts a wizard's documents only once the body is parsed. The filter is first in the chain (`@Order(HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE)`) and answers **413** `{error: "Request too large", detail}` (with `Connection: close`) before anything else runs: by `Content-Length` when the request states one; when it does not (chunked), the body is read there up to the limit and handed on from memory, so it is refused the moment it passes. `menora.request.max-body-size` (32 MB — above two wizard requests' documents, which is what an update carries) for everything, `menora.request.max-import-size` (100 MB) for `POST /admin/import`; `0` switches either off. A form (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, multipart) without a length is left to the container, which reads its fields itself under its own limit.

**2. Team models, presets and templates: only the creator or an admin changes or deletes (`config/TeamDataAccess`, `V39`).** The three were open to every caller, with nothing recorded. Now `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/metadata/fullstack/models`, `/metadata/backend/presets` and `/metadata/fullstack/templates` are allowed when the row has no creator, when the caller (the `menora.audit.user-header` header, compared ignoring case) is its creator, or when the request carries a valid admin token (`Authorization: Bearer …` — the tokens are stateless, so one is as good outside `/admin`). Anyone else: **403** `{error: "Not yours to change", detail: "Saved by alice — only they or a signed-in admin can change or delete it"}`. `V39` adds `updated_by` to the three tables; every answer gains `updatedBy` (the caller's name, or the admin's — `admin` for the shared password with nobody named) and `canEdit` (computed for whoever asked, so the screens need no who-am-I call). The creator is the gateway's user only: a row saved with nobody named stays open to everyone, as every row from before is. Without the single sign-on gateway in front, the header is whatever a caller sends — here as everywhere else on the site.

**3. Typed live-schema connections are off unless switched on.** `menora.live-schema.typed-connections` defaults to `false` (the `@Value` and `application.yml`): a server that connects wherever a caller says is a way to probe its network. Admin-defined schema sources are unaffected; the drawers already follow `GET /metadata/live-schema/options`. **Upgrade note:** a deployment that relied on typing databases in sets `MENORA_LIVESCHEMA_TYPEDCONNECTIONS=true` (with `allowed-hosts`).

**4. The admin login limit (`admin/AdminLoginThrottle`, `V40`).**
- *Counted in the database* (`initializer_admin_login_failure`: kind, address, window start, failures; `JdbcStore`, each step one statement so two instances counting one address both count). Per JVM, an address got its tries on every instance. An in-memory `MemoryStore` remains for the unit tests of the rules. If the table cannot be read or written the sign-in goes on unlimited (logged): a brake, not a lock.
- *A ceiling over every address:* after `admin.login.max-failures-total` (100) wrong passwords within the window, from wherever, the password signs nobody in until the oldest of those windows has passed — guesses spread over many addresses stayed under any per-address limit. Signing in as oneself is not held to it (no shared secret to guess; it is how an admin gets in meanwhile). `0` switches it off — where the password is the only way in, the ceiling is also a way to keep the admins out on purpose.
- *Single sign-on is counted too* (`Kind.SSO`, apart from `Kind.PASSWORD`, per address only): a request no user is named in and a user who is not an admin are failures; the directory not answering is not. 429 with `Retry-After`, like the password.
- *Behind a proxy:* the address is the connection's; `server.forward-headers-strategy` (`native` or `framework`, only where nothing but the proxy reaches the application) makes it the caller's — documented in `application.yml`, the class and the Guide.

**Pinned by** `RequestBodyLimitFilterTest` (5), `SecurityDefaultsTests` (the shipped configuration over MockMvc: a 33 MB body is a 413 on five paths and reaches the admin login on `/admin/import`; a typed connection is a 403), `TeamDataAccessTests` (each rule on each of the three endpoints), the three controllers' round-trip tests (now acting as the creator), `AdminLoginTest` (the ceiling, single sign-on counted), `AdminLoginThrottleStoreTest` (the migration's table, two throttles over one database, expiry, the fail-open), `ShippedConfigurationTest` (the three defaults) and `LiveSchemaIntegrationTests` (which now switches typed connections on for itself).

**Limits.** The body limit counts bytes on the wire, not what a compressed body expands to (the application takes none). A team row's creator cannot be changed, and one saved anonymously can never be claimed. The login ceiling does not tell an attack from a team of admins mistyping. Nothing rate-limits the generation endpoints themselves.

### After phase 8: saving a dependency group leaves its dependencies alone

Not in the plan — found while walking phase 7 by hand. `PUT /admin/dependency-groups/{id}` saved the request body as the group entity, and the group owns its `entries` with cascade and orphan removal: a body without `entries` **deleted every dependency of the group**, and a body with them wrote them over whatever had been saved since — which is what the Groups tab sends, the list as it was when the tab loaded. `POST` had the same fault from the other side: a body carrying an existing group's `id` was merged over that group.

`AdminController.createGroup` / `updateGroup` now copy the group's own fields (`copyGroupFields`: name, sort order, kind) onto a new or the stored row and ignore everything else in the body; a `PUT` to an id nothing has is a 404 `{error, detail}` (it used to insert a group under another id). A group's dependencies are saved only through `/admin/dependency-entries`. `DependencyGroupEntity` is the only catalog entity that owns a child collection, so no other admin endpoint has the fault. The catalog import and the catalog sync build their groups themselves and are unchanged.

Pinned by three cases in `AdminApiIntegrationTests` (`savingAGroupNeitherDeletesNorOverwritesItsDependencies`, `savingAGroupThatIsNotThereIs404AndCreatesNothing`, `aNewGroupTakesNeitherAnIdNorDependenciesFromItsBody`), each of which fails on the old controller.

### Phase 9: nothing here

Phase 9 is the generator site's own: the entry chunk's headroom and its two largest files in parts. No backend file changed. See *Phase 9* in `ui/CLAUDE.md`.

### Phase 10: nothing here

Phase 10 is the layout designer's canvas in the generator site (`ui/CLAUDE.md`, *Phase 10*): it draws the header's tools, the gauge, funnel and time-in-state widgets, composed rows, file and picture fields and the change history as the templates here generate them. No backend file changed; the words on the canvas are copies of `i18n-strings.ts.mustache` — **a wording changed there needs the same change in `ui/src/components/fullstack/layoutPreviewModel.ts` `STRINGS`**.

### Phase 11: nothing here

Phase 11 is the generator site's Config → Fullstack Examples form, which now opens the full-screen layout designer (`ui/CLAUDE.md`, *Phase 11*). No backend file changed: the form saves through the same `/admin/fullstack-examples` calls, and what the designer sets beside the pages (template set, palette, language, RTL, navigation) lands in the example's `settings`, which `FullstackExampleAdminController.validateSettings` checks as before.

### After phase 11: the parked runs (2026-11-20)

The Docker runs parked since phase 2, one at a time, with ~6–10 GB of memory free:

| Run | Result |
|---|---|
| `LiveSchemaDockerTests` — the phase 5 catalog queries on PostgreSQL 16, SQL Server 2022, Oracle 23 | passed (3 tests) |
| `CreateTablesDockerTests -Dsmoke.db=POSTGRESQL` / `MSSQL` / `ORACLE` — every example's tables and the keyword-named model | passed on all three |
| `GeneratedBackendBuildSmokeTests#aBackendOnAnotherDatabasePassesItsOwnTestsOnH2` (`-Dsmoke.be=true`: `postgresql`, `mssql`, `oracle`) | passed (3 real builds) |
| `LocalRunDockerSmokeTests#theApiTestsPassOnTheRealDatabase -Dsmoke.db=oracle` — Orders on Boot 3.5 with Flyway, its API tests on the real database | **failed** at start; after the fix below, 23 of 24 pass |
| the same with `-Dsmoke.db=postgresql` (Boot 3.2.1 with Flyway; Boot 3.5 with and without) | 3.2.1 passed (24); both 3.5 cases fail one test — the second finding below |

**Found and fixed: a generated project with Flyway did not start on Oracle 23.** The baseline ran; Hibernate's validation then refused the first boolean column — `wrong column type encountered in column [active] in table [customers]; found [number (Types#NUMERIC)], but expecting [boolean (Types#BOOLEAN)]`. Oracle 23 has a real `BOOLEAN`, and Hibernate 6.6 asks for it there; `SchemaDialect.ORACLE` writes `number(1,0)`, the name Hibernate writes for Oracle 19 — the version `SchemaDialectTest` holds it to.

*The fix:* a fullstack project's `OracleConfig` (`oracle-config-primary.mustache`, under `{{#hasScaffoldedEntities}}`, so a plain project with the `oracle` dependency keeps its bytes) puts `hibernate.type.preferred_boolean_jdbc_type = TINYINT` (`SchemaDialect.ORACLE_BOOLEAN_SETTING` / `ORACLE_BOOLEAN_JDBC_TYPE`). Booleans are then a NUMBER holding 1 or 0 on every Oracle — the same on 19 as on 23, which is also what the soft-delete SQL (`deleted = 1`, `cast(deleted as integer) = 0`) was written for.
- **Why `TINYINT`.** `BIT` is still the server's `boolean` on 23. `NUMERIC` gives `number(1,0)` and lets the application start, but every write then fails: Hibernate cannot bind a Boolean as a decimal (`Could not convert 'java.lang.Boolean' to 'java.math.BigDecimal'` — seen on the real server, 9 of 24 API tests). `TINYINT` binds as a byte. Hibernate itself would write `number(3,0)` for it; its validation compares the type's name, not its digits, so the baseline's `number(1,0)` is accepted and stays as it is (existing schemas are untouched). A project without Flyway gets `number(3,0)` from `ddl-auto`.
- **Proven** on Oracle 23 in Docker (`theApiTestsPassOnTheRealDatabase -Dsmoke.db=oracle`): the application starts on the Flyway baseline with Hibernate validating, and 23 of the 24 generated API tests pass — every one that reads or writes a boolean. The same project's tests on H2 pass with the setting (`aBackendOnAnotherDatabasePassesItsOwnTestsOnH2[oracle]`). Not run: Oracle 19–21 (no image here; the setting makes Hibernate behave there as on 23). The secondary-datasource template (`oracle-config-secondary`) is unchanged: scaffolded entities are the primary's.
- Pinned by `SchemaDialectTest.onOracle23TheGeneratedSettingKeepsBooleansNumeric` (without the setting Hibernate writes `boolean` on 23; with it the same NUMBER on 19 and 23, every other column the baseline's) and `FullstackDatasourceConfigTests.fullstackEndpoint_oracleKeepsBooleansNumericOnEveryServerVersion`. No golden manifest moved (no case is a fullstack project on Oracle). The template is a catalog row: an existing database takes it on its next start unless an admin edited the row.

**Found, not fixed (ask before adding): on Spring Boot 3.5, deleting a soft-deleted parent of composed rows answers 500.** The 24th test: `OrderApiTest.aRowIsCreatedReadChangedAndDeleted`, `DELETE /api/orders/{id}` → `TransientObjectException: persistent instance references an unsaved transient instance of 'Order'`. `Service.delete` goes through `findById`, which loads the composed rows (`withRows`); with soft delete the parent keeps its rows (the composition cascades PERSIST and MERGE only), so the loaded lines stay managed while their order is removed — and Hibernate 6.6 refuses that at flush, where 6.4 said nothing. **Not Oracle's:** the PostgreSQL run of the same day passes all 24 on Boot 3.2.1 and fails this one test on Boot 3.5, with and without Flyway. It was not seen before because every H2 build of this option set (`aBackendOnAnotherDatabasePassesItsOwnTestsOnH2`, the live smoke tests) is on Boot 3.2, and the Boot 3.5 Docker cases last passed before the rows rode along in `findById`. The likely fix is small: `delete` loading the row without its composed rows when the parent soft-deletes.

Still unrun: `theBackendRunsOnItsComposeServices`, `thePlainSampleTestStartsTheApplicationOnPostgresql`, `nginxSignsCallsAsTheDeveloperOnlyWhenTold` (not among the parked runs asked for), anything on DB2.
